## 1. Problem, Operational Scope, and Contributions

Training multi-layer causal Transformers without a global backward pass is a core problem in local credit assignment [1, 2, 3, 9]. A foundational methodological distinction separates **backward-free local credit assignment** from **approximate or manual backpropagation**: disabling automatic differentiation (`torch.no_grad()`) or replacing local analytical derivatives with finite-difference probes does not remove the backward phase if current-step downstream error vectors are multiplied by layer weight matrices in reverse order ($\mathbf{e}_{\text{in}} = \mathbf{e}_{\text{out}} W$) or relayed from downstream blocks to upstream blocks within the same training step.

In Chung's **Weight Maximization (WM) with eligibility traces** [1], each unit $i$ in stage $m$ injects a local forward exploration perturbation $\xi_{n, i}^{(m)}(t)$ during the forward pass of batch $t$, records a local pre-synaptic eligibility trace $Z_{i, :}^{(m)}(t)$, and updates at a subsequent step using the column weight-norm change $\sum_{j} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)} \Delta W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}$ of its outgoing receiver synapses $W_{:, i}^{(m+1)}$.

### 1.1 Operational Definition of Delayed Perturbation-Differential Column-Norm Credit and Pipeline Clocks

To adapt delayed WM to multi-token Transformer minibatches where receiver layers are updated by adaptive or spectrally orthogonalized optimizers (`AdamW` or `Pre-Polar Muon` [7, 8]), we state precisely what feedback our algorithm constructs, how its normalization is parameterized in code, and how pipeline clocks are ordered:

1. **Installed Receiver Displacement vs. Perturbation-Differential Plasticity Tensor:** At step $t$, each receiver layer $m+1$ updates its live weights $W^{(m+1)}(t) = W^{(m+1)}(t-1) + \delta W^{(m+1)}(t-1)$ via its optimizer from the clean batch gradient (or delayed WM pseudo-gradient). To evaluate the directional effect of stage $m$'s forward perturbation $\xi^{(m)}(t_{\text{orig}})$ on receiver $m+1$'s synaptic plasticity without confounding from other stages' noise or optimizer state history, our reward routine (`compute_stream_wm_rewards_chunked` in `exp26`; `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` in `exp32`–`exp35`, parameterized by `scale_factor`) constructs for each token stream $\mathcal{S}_g$ ($g \in \{1, \dots, G\}$, $|\mathcal{S}_g| = M_g = BT / G$) a **perturbation-differential plasticity tensor**
   $$
   \Delta \tilde{W}_{j, i}^{(m+1, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}; \text{scale\_factor}) := \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{orig}}) \, \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)}(t_{\text{orig}}), \qquad \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} := y_{n, i}^{\text{pert}}(\sigma \xi^{(m)}) - y_{n, i}^{\text{clean}}(0),
   $$
   where $u_{n, :}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{orig}})$ is the receiver's local post-synaptic modulation vector and $\Delta y_{n, :}^{(m)}(t_{\text{orig}})$ is the perturbed-minus-clean forward activation displacement at stage $m$'s output. At reward construction clock $t_{\text{constr}}$, unit $i$ contracts $\Delta \tilde{W}_{:, i}^{(m+1, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}; \text{scale\_factor})$ with its **live outgoing receiver column** $W_{:, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})$:
   $$
   R_i^{(m, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}, t_{\text{constr}}; \text{scale\_factor}) = \sum_{j=1}^{d_{\text{next}}} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) \, \Delta \tilde{W}_{j, i}^{(m+1, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}; \text{scale\_factor}).
   $$
   The parameter update then divides the stream-summed modulation–input product $\sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)} Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)}$ by $N = BT$ (`-(mod_flat.t() @ x_flat) / BT`). Thus, $\Delta \tilde{W}^{(m+1, g)}$ is a local perturbation-differential Hebbian plasticity tensor rather than a decomposition of the installed optimizer displacement $\delta W^{(m+1)}$, and reading $W^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})$ at construction clock $t_{\text{constr}}$ makes the reward directly sensitive to any receiver weight update $\delta W^{(m+1)}(t-1) = W^{(m+1)}(t) - W^{(m+1)}(t-1)$ installed between origin $t_{\text{orig}}$ and construction $t_{\text{constr}}$.
2. **Three-Clock Pipeline Schedules:** For each stage $m \in \{1, 2, 3\}$, we track $(t_{\text{orig}}, t_{\text{constr}}, t_{\text{inst}})$ relative to installation step $t_{\text{inst}} = t$. Across `exp26`–`exp35`, three distinct clock schedules are evaluated:
   - **True Both-Stages Pre-Install (`both_stages_pre_install`, `exp26`–`exp30` and `exp35` Part B2):** `Stage 3`: $(t, t, t)$; `Stage 2`: $(t-1, t-1 \text{ [pre-Stage3-step]}, t)$ (reward constructed at $t-1$ on $W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ prior to Stage 3's batch-$t-1$ update, installed at $t$); `Stage 1`: $(t-2, t-1 \text{ [pre-Stage2-step]}, t)$ (reward constructed and cached at $t-1$ on $W_{\text{proj}}(t-1)$ prior to Stage 2's installation of its batch-$t-2$ update, then installed into Stage 1 at $t$).
   - **Stage-2 Pre-Install / Stage-1 Post-Install (`stage2_pre_install`, `run_exp33_partB.py`, `run_exp34.py`, `exp35` Part B1):** `Stage 3`: $(t, t, t)$; `Stage 2`: $(t-1, t-1 \text{ [pre-Stage3-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t-1$ prior to Stage 3's update); `Stage 1`: $(t-2, t \text{ [post-Stage2-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t$ on live $W_{\text{proj}}(t)$ after Stage 2 installed its batch-$t-2$ update at the end of $t-1$).
   - **Strict Post-Installation Construction (`strict_post_install`, `exp32`–`exp35`):** `Stage 3`: $(t, t, t)$; `Stage 2`: $(t-1, t \text{ [post-Stage3-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t$ on $W_{\text{lm}}(t) = W_{\text{lm}}(t-1) + \delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ strictly *after* Stage 3 installed its batch-$t-1$ update); `Stage 1`: $(t-2, t \text{ [post-Stage2-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t$ on $W_{\text{proj}}(t)$ strictly *after* Stage 2 installed its batch-$t-2$ update).
   *(Note: The `CausalAuditLedger` fields in our JSON logs record declared trainer configuration metadata summarizing the code path of each arm rather than runtime hardware profiler counters; in `exp33`–`exp35`, all `stage2_pre_install` ledgers are reconciled to record `(t-2, t [post-Stage2-step], t)`).*

### 1.2 Summary of Contributions

Across `396` multi-seed experimental arms (`1,187` total runs across Seeds `46, 47, 48` and `42, 43, 44`: **`279` three-seed strictly delayed zero-relay Delayed-WM arms [`837` runs]**, **`13` three-seed node-perturbation arms [`11` `SR-LNP` + `2` `SR-NP` = `39` runs]**, **`4` head/lexical-only backward-free anchors [`11` runs]**, **`3` three-seed same-clock compositional-relay diagnostic controls [`9` runs]**, **`7` three-seed local-pullback diagnostic controls [`21` runs]**, and **`90` three-seed backpropagation reference arms [`36` global BP + `11` block-skip surrogate BP + `26` sub-block direct-skip surrogate BP + `11` 1-hop/all-hop highway surrogate BP + `6` low-rank/Oja coordinate-bridge surrogate BP = `270` runs]** across `exp18, exp21, exp26`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp79`) on ClimbMix `d6` ($L=6, D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}, V=8,192$, $360$–$1,004$ steps $= 0.737\text{M}$–$2.056\text{M}$ tokens), `d12` ($L=12, D=384, V=8,192$, $360$–$720$ steps), and Native `50,332,176` ($L=8, D=512, V=8,192, T=2,048$, $100$ steps $= 13.1\text{M}$ tokens), where every Delayed-WM run executes inside `with torch.no_grad():` (`assert not torch.is_grad_enabled()`) with zero reverse error matrix multiplications through Transformer backbone layer weights ($W_{\text{fc}}, W_{\text{proj}}, W_{q,k,v}$), zero `torch.autograd.grad`/VJP helpers, and zero same-step inter-block relays (while explicitly distinguishing Class II `exp77` arms that use a local causal attention-probability transpose gather $\tilde{u}_v = A_i^\top u_v$ and column-isolated `exp78`–`exp79` arms that evaluate a 2-call local pullback $V_l(U_l^\top e_l)$ across the block-local auxiliary bridge $(U_l, V_l)$), we establish nine results across eleven propositions within the short-horizon developmental regime tested:

- **Reconciled Stream-Partitioned Estimator Analysis & Scale-Normalized Controls (Proposition 1, `exp26`, `exp35`, `exp44`, `exp58`, `exp60`):** We reconcile the exact implemented contraction $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; \text{scale\_factor}) = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)} = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G)$ across both the default code convention `scale_factor = 1` (whose conditional mean scales as $1/M_g$ and covariance as $1/M_g^2$, giving mean $0.5$ on $N=4, G=2, M_g=2, a_n=x_n=1$) and the scale-normalized convention `scale_factor = M_g` (whose conditional mean $\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n$ is invariant to $M_g$, giving $1.0$). We further distinguish **separable channels** (Stage 1: `mlp.c_fc, attn.c_v`, **Proposition 1(a–b)**, where first-order variance vanishes at $M_g = 1$) from **non-separable `RMSNorm` interfaces** (Stage 2: `x_final, x_0`, **Proposition 1(c)**), deriving the exact single-token ($M_g=1$) coordinate mean $\frac{a_{n, i}}{\text{rms}(z_n)}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D) h_n$, cross-coordinate variance $\frac{a_{n, i}^2 x_{n, i}^2 (D - x_{n, i}^2)}{D^2 \text{rms}(z_n)^2} \|h_n\|_2^2$ (giving $0.064$ at $D=2, z=(1, 2)$), and radially projected modulation conditional mean $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D) a_n \neq \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} a_n = \mu_{\text{skip}, n}$ (verified at $D=3, x=(\sqrt{3/2}, \sqrt{3/2}, 0)^\top, a=(1, 0, 1)^\top$, where $\mathbb{E}[P_x\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}] = (1/4, -1/4, 1)^\top \neq \mu_{\text{skip}} = (1/2, -1/2, 1)^\top$; its modulation-vector discrepancy is bounded by $\left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|a_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$ and outer-product matrix Frobenius discrepancy by $\left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|a_n\|_2 \|h_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$, where the normalized discrepancy coefficient $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 / D$ is $O(1/D)$ under a coordinate-concentration assumption $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 = O(1)$; furthermore, multiplying $\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}$ by the closed-form forward leverage inverse $\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)^{-1}$ prior to $P_{x_n}$ in `exp60` restores exact first-order unbiasedness $\cos = 1.000000$ at $D=3$ with zero extra probes and zero local pullbacks). Controlled 3-seed `scale_factor = M_g` runs (`exp35` on `d6` and `exp44` on Native `50.3M`) confirm that holding the conditional mean invariant to $M_g$ leaves learning curves virtually unchanged (`2.6097 -> 2.3361 -> 2.3008 -> 2.2338` for `scale_factor = M_g` vs. `2.5939 -> 2.3364 -> 2.2966 -> 2.2338` for `scale_factor = 1` across $G \in \{1, 64, 256, 2048\}$ on `d6`; `2.2539 ± 0.0013` vs. `2.2541 ± 0.0013` at $G=64$ under `Pre-Polar Muon`; and **`1.9350 ± 0.0003` for `scale_factor = 8.0` vs. `1.9358 ± 0.0002` for `scale_factor = 1.0` at $M_g=8, \text{lr\_embed}=3\text{e-}3$ on Native `50.3M`**, so along this tested intervention path **`96.9%` (`-0.0248` of `-0.0256` BPB) of the $M_g: 8 \to 1$ gain (`1.9350 -> 1.9102`) comes from eliminating $7/8$ within-stream cross-token interference terms**), establishing that variance reduction rather than raw step-scale changes drives the stream-partitioning gain.
- **Forward Causal Attention-Filtered Value Eligibility Trace (Proposition 2, `exp28`):** Because $v(b, s) = W_v x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)$ commutes with causal attention over $s \le \tau$, computing the causal attention-filtered pre-synaptic trace $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)$ during the forward pass and perturbing post-attention value outputs lifts single-step backward-free `attn.c_v` cosine alignment with the language-model cross-entropy loss gradient **$8.2\times$ from `+0.1104` to `+0.9069`** (note that for any attention-output error $E_h \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times d_h}$, $(A_h^\top E_h)^\top X = E_h^\top (A_h X)$ is an exact associative matrix identity verified to $2.665 \times 10^{-15}$ max error in `exp68` Part 1A, so head-filtered value inputs $A_h X$ are an algebraic identity shared with exact backpropagation rather than an algebraic distinction from it). Pairing $Z^{(2)}(t-1)$ with independent current-batch rewards $R^{(2)}(t)$ (`mismatched_origin_control`, `exp28`) degrades `d6` from `2.2338 ± 0.0102` to **`2.7608 ± 0.0056` DEV BPB (`-0.5270` BPB contrast)**.
- **Diagonal Skip-Routing Surrogate, Data-Dependent Kurtosis Expectation Bound, & Compliant Pure-`FP32` Clock/Optimizer Factorials (Propositions 3 & 4, `exp31`–`exp36`, `exp42`, `exp59`):** Routing lexical credit to `wte` via the diagonal skip surrogate $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$ (**Proposition 3**) cuts PyTorch `d6` rule time by **$27.4\%$ (`205.5 -> 149.2` ms/step)** with identical accuracy (`2.1579 ± 0.0004` vs. `2.1577 ± 0.0004`). For Strict Post-Installation Construction, **Proposition 4** decomposes $\delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ into weight decay, momentum history $\beta_1 m^{(t-2)}$, and current-batch gradient $(1-\beta_1) g^{(t-1)}$. Under row-isotropic preconditioning, reward-side projection $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)}$ annihilates the $O(1)$ radial self-update vector $b_{\text{rad}}(n)$ identically, leaving an oblique `RMSNorm`-Jacobian coupling residual whose **expected** squared norm relative to $\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2$ is bounded by the **data-dependent coordinate fourth moment** $\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[\|P_x b\|_2^2]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}\|_2^2} \le \frac{\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}(n))}{D}$ where $\kappa_4(x) := \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D x_i^4$. We show both that unrestricted sparse vectors $x = \sqrt{D/5}(1, 2, 0, \dots, 0)$ have non-vanishing expectation ratio $0.0576$ ($\kappa_4(x)/D = 0.68$) for all $D \ge 2$, and that on a 20-step head/block-0 diagnostic trajectory on ClimbMix `d6` (`exp35` Part 0), $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}})$ has token mean $2.9398$, yielding mean theoretical expectation bound $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}})/D = 0.007656$ and empirical mean ratio $0.007458$ (with individual token maximum $0.124439$ across $40,960$ token evaluations). Across our **16-cell $2 \times 2 \times 2 \times 2$ Stage-2 Clock $\times$ Optimizer factorial (`exp34` + `exp35` Part B1)**, **4-cell True Both-Stages Pre-Install comparison (`exp35` Part B2)**, **compliant zero-pullback pure-`FP32` trace-cache Triton WM clock controls (`exp42` Part A)**, and **720-step 4-matrix and 6-matrix `both_stages_pre_install` vs. `strict_post_install` clock controls (`exp59` Arms 5–6)**:
  1. **Isolating Stage-1 vs. Stage-2 Clock Transitions, Compliant Pure-`FP32` Cache Controls, & 720-Step Clock Stability (`exp35` Part B2, `exp42` Part A, `exp59` Arms 5–6):** Moving Stage 1 from `(t-2, t-1 [pre-Stage2], t)` (`both_stages_pre_install`) to `(t-2, t [post-Stage2], t)` (`stage2_pre_install`) shifts `d6` DEV BPB by only **`+0.0013` to `+0.0024` BPB** across all 4 `entrywise` cells (`2.1341 -> 2.1360` at `b05, 1e-8`; `2.1195 ± 0.0005 -> 2.1217 ± 0.0006` at `b05, 1e-4`), consistent with update-orthogonalized `Pre-Polar Muon` inducing only a `4.04%` (`cos = 0.999238`) Stage-1 reward drift. Moving Stage 2 from `(t-1, t-1, t)` to `(t-1, t [post-Stage3], t)` at `(entrywise, b05, 1e-8)` shifts DEV BPB by **`+0.0219 ± 0.0020` BPB** (`2.1360 -> 2.1579`). Eliminating `BF16` trace caching in a compliant zero-pullback Triton WM trainer (`reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0`, `exp42_fp32_triton_results.json`) reproduces the exact same 3-seed clock contrast (**`2.1359 ± 0.0022` vs. `2.1579 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB** at `b05, 1e-8`, and **`2.1274 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** at `b09, 1e-4`), confirming that the `+0.0219` BPB shift is a genuine receiver-installation effect rather than a `BF16` cache-rounding artifact (while `exp36` provides matching local-pullback diagnostic controls). Under the co-designed optimizer ($\epsilon = 10^{-4}, \text{lrm}: 1.0 \to 0.2$) at `720` steps (`exp59` Arms 5–6), `both_stages_pre_install` (`Stage 2: (t-1, t-1, t), Stage 1: (t-2, t-1, t)`) and `strict_post_install` (`(t-1, t, t)/(t-2, t, t)`) agree within **`0.0012`–`0.0020` BPB** (`1.9977 ± 0.0007` vs. `1.9989 ± 0.0007` on 4-matrix; `1.9692 ± 0.0005` vs. `1.9672 ± 0.0005` on 6-matrix $M=4$).
  2. **Row-Isotropic vs. Entrywise Preconditioning (`Proposition 4`, `exp34`–`exp35`):** Across all 4 `row_iso` cells of the 16-cell cube, Stage-2 Pre-Install and Strict Post-Install agree to within **`0.0003` BPB** (`2.1577` vs. `2.1580`; `2.1662` vs. `2.1659`; `2.1296` vs. `2.1298`; `2.1335` vs. `2.1334`). Under `entrywise` moments, increasing $\epsilon: 10^{-8} \to 10^{-4}$ damps coordinate spread in $d_{j, i} = (\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}} + \epsilon)^{-1}$ and improves Strict Post-Install by **`-0.0279 ± 0.0001` BPB** (`2.1579 -> 2.1300`), while $(\beta_1=0.9, \epsilon=10^{-4})$ reaches **`2.1273 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** (`2.0900 ± 0.0001` VAL-B).
- **Fused Register-Tiled Triton WM Kernels (`38.4x` Kernel Speedup & `8.33x` Steady-State Step Speedup on `d6`; Isolated `1.44x`–`6.33x` Steady-State Step Speedup & `2.63x` Implementation-Package Speedup on Native `50.3M`, `exp37`, `exp43`, `exp44`):** Building on the SRAM-fused outgoing-column contraction concept introduced in [5] (version 5), we specialize fused register-tiled Triton kernels (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` for $M_g=1$ and `_triton_wm_reward_mg_grouped_kernel` for $M_g > 1$) for our 3-stage delayed perturbation-differential WM pipeline. Eliminating the 3D outer-product workspace (`3,498.9 MB` on `d6` `lm_head` at $G=2,048$) cuts the 13-matrix `d6` WM kernel suite **`38.4x` from `134.31 ms` to `3.50 ms/step`** at $G=2,048$ (`cos = 1.000000`, `rel_l2 = 2.26e-8` on `mlp.c_proj`) and **`4.4x` from `4.51 ms` to `1.03 ms/step`** at $G=64$ (`cos = 0.9999973`, `rel_l2 = 2.31e-3`). Combined with batched `Pre-Polar Muon`, steady-state `d6` `fwd + rule` step time at $G=2,048$ drops **`8.33x` from `155.6 ms` to `18.68 ± 0.21 ms/step`** (`6.72s` steady-state estimate for 360 steps; `6.74s`–`6.95s` measured 3-seed training-region GPU time). On Native `50.3M` ($B_{\text{eff}}=64, T=2,048$), `exp32`'s PyTorch baseline (`compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast`) processes stream chunks (`chunk_G=256`, allocating `4.62 GB` temporary chunk workspace; or `chunk_G=32`, allocating `0.62 GB`). In **genuinely isolated kernel substitutions** (`benchmark_native50m_isolated_kernel.json`, measuring within-run steady-state variation across steps `2–4`) holding all storage, precision, trace construction, optimizer execution, and timing boundaries fixed: (i) inside `exp32`'s CPU-offloaded trainer ($M_g=8$, after a `3`-step CPU-heap warmup), swapping only `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast(chunk_G=256)` $\to$ `compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast` speeds up the steady-state (`steps 2–4`) learning rule **`1.60x` (`6,456.78 ± 0.64 -> 4,039.89 ± 0.20 ms/step`)** and steady-state `fwd + rule` **`1.44x` (`7,876.97 ± 0.65 -> 5,460.10 ± 0.52 ms/step`, or `1.50x` rule and `1.35x` `fwd + rule` across all `5` steps `0–4` including pipeline startup)** while reducing peak VRAM from **`9.108 GiB` to `7.629 GiB`**; and (ii) inside `exp43`'s GPU-resident trainer (`20.57–20.65 GiB` peak VRAM, `2` warmup + `3` timed steady-state steps `2–4`, where `chunk_G=256` OOMs), swapping only `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast(chunk_G=32)` $\to$ `compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast` speeds up the 288-call contraction **`6.86x` (`2,363.30 ± 0.72 -> 344.60 ± 0.74 ms`)** and `fwd + rule` **`1.67x` (`5,055.97 ± 1.30 -> 3,033.91 ± 1.36 ms`)** at $M_g=8$, and speeds up the 288-call contraction **`29.54x` (`18,317.09 ± 20.32 -> 620.01 ± 0.22 ms`)**, the learning rule **`9.91x` (`19,689.83 ± 20.99 -> 1,987.43 ± 0.59 ms`)**, and `fwd + rule` **`6.33x` (`21,022.47 ± 21.62 -> 3,319.91 ± 0.59 ms`)** at $M_g=1$. Across 100-step 3-seed training runs (`exp43`–`exp44`), our **combined implementation package** (combining the Triton kernels with GPU-resident traces + pinned MLP ring buffers, `BF16` SDPA and deferred Stage-1 attention-filtered input reconstruction from `BF16` cached inputs, batched Muon orthogonalization, and in-place scatter head scoring, trading **`9.11 GiB -> 20.57 GiB` peak VRAM**—or **`9.61–9.69 GiB` peak VRAM** when all 8 delayed trace tensors are placed in pinned host ring buffers in `exp44`) matches `exp32` in quality (`1.9358 ± 0.0002` vs. `1.9361 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB at $M_g=8$) with a **`2.63x` speedup on matched `fwd + rule` timed regions (`7,857.05 -> 2,991.84 ms/step`, `3.88x` on the learning rule `6,432.04 -> 1,659.19 ms`)**, and reaches **`1.9102 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB** at $M_g=1, \text{lr\_embed}=3\text{e-}3$ and **`1.8978 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.8844 ± 0.0003` VAL-B BPB, `208.39` PPL)** at $M_g=1, \text{lr\_embed}=0.015, \epsilon=10^{-8}$ in `3.26 s/step` (`fwd + rule`; vs. `1.9266 ± 0.0004` at $\epsilon=10^{-4}$ in `exp44`).
- **Joint Co-Design of the Optimizer and Delayed WM Credit (`2.1273 -> 2.0682` DEV BPB at 360 Steps; `1.9989` at 720 Steps, `exp38`):** Adapting the `Pre-Polar Muon` foundation of [8] to delayed WM, we combine `Pre-Polar Muon` update orthogonalization with scale-matched sparse embedding velocity (`lr_wte = 0.12, lr_ve = 0.06, betas = (0.80, 0.95)`) and post-installation drift annealing (`lrm: 1.0 -> 0.2`) on `d6`. Across 3 seeds on `d6` (360 steps, Strict Post-Install), this optimizer package improves Delayed WM from `2.1273 ± 0.0001` to **`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`2.0315 ± 0.0004` VAL-B BPB, `292.66` PPL)**—within **`0.0150` BPB** of matched-subset Exact Backpropagation (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`, which also improves from `2.1080 ± 0.0006` under the same optimizer package). On a Seed-46 Block-0 diagnostic audit (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`), `Pre-Polar Muon` is descriptively associated with a higher `mlp.c_proj` weight stable rank than `AdamW` (`127.24` under `paper20_prepolar` and `141.33` under `codesigned_prepolar` vs. `2.75` under `adamw_hidden`), and the co-designed embedding/annealing schedule increases held-out attention-map Frobenius shift by $9.82\times$ in Layer 0 (`0.0838 -> 0.8233`) and $1.42\times$ across all 6 layers (`0.6046 -> 0.8587`).
- **Controlled Comparisons Against Author-Defined `SR-LNP` and `SR-NP` Node-Perturbation Recipes Across Tokens, Training-Region Time, and Width ($D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, `exp37`, `exp39`, `exp42`, `exp56`, `exp62`–`exp66`):** We compare Delayed WM against two author-defined node-perturbation recipes on the matched 4-matrix `d6` subset: (i) **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, $K \in \{4, 16, 64\}$)**, which uses same-step terminal-skip routing ($g_{\text{bus}} \cdot \gamma_l$) and $\gamma = 0$ local value scoring; and (ii) **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, `exp42`)**, which uses Gaussian draw-mean centering, suffix recomputation from block $l$ onward, shared post-MLP block-output perturbations for `attn.c_proj` and `mlp.c_proj`, analytic `lm_head` gradients, and $\gamma = 0.98$ future-token value accumulation:
  1. **Equal Tokens (`360` steps on `d6`, $D=384$):** `Delayed WM (K=1, G=2048)` (`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `6.74s` measured training-region GPU time / `6.72s` steady-state est.) outperforms `SR-LNP` at $K=4$ (`2.3071 ± 0.0016`, `6.32s` run / `5.48s` est.), $K=16$ (`2.2603 ± 0.0018`, `9.43s` run / `9.23s` est.), and $K=64$ (`2.2041 ± 0.0013`, `23.66s` run / `23.22s` est.), as well as 76-draw `SR-NP` (`2.2415 ± 0.0015` under `Pre-Polar Muon`, `65.19s` run; `2.4264 ± 0.0088` under `Adam`, `84.38s` run).
  2. **Matched Training-Region Time (`~9.4s` and `~13.6s` in the Specialized 4-Matrix `exp39` Trainer):** At `500` steps (`9.41s` run / `9.34s` est.), `Delayed WM` reaches **`2.0322 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9975 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `265.13` PPL)**. At `720` steps (`13.57s` run / `13.45s` est.), `Delayed WM` reaches **`1.9989 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.9646 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `241.96` PPL)**.
  3. **Model Width Scaling ($D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, `exp39`, `exp56`, `exp59`–`exp60`, `exp62`–`exp66`):** As width $D = H \cdot d_h$ scales from `256 -> 384 -> 512 -> 768` at fixed head dimension $d_h = 64$, 4-matrix `Delayed WM`'s gap to 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation shrinks from `+0.0144` to **`+0.0125` BPB** (`2.0558 ± 0.0002` vs. `2.0433 ± 0.0006` at $D=768$). Under whole-matrix $(D, D)$ `Pre-Polar Muon` (`exp49`, `exp56`, `exp60`, `exp62`), Full 6-Matrix `Delayed WM` ($M=4$) reaches `2.0794 ± 0.0009, 2.0552 ± 0.0007, 2.0451 ± 0.0005, 2.0437 ± 0.0006` under $\gamma_K=0.85$ and **`2.0768 ± 0.0010` (`11.11s`), `2.0519 ± 0.0009` (`12.20s`), `2.0427 ± 0.0007` (`15.99s`), `2.0418 ± 0.0002` (`29.92s`) DEV BPB** under `Wiener-MMSE + Hadamard-4` (`82.9%, 75.5%, 62.9%, 50.0%` of the 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain). Crucially, replacing whole-matrix $(D, D)$ `Pre-Polar Muon` on $(W_Q, W_K)$ with **Per-Head Rectangular Stiefel `Pre-Polar Muon` (`(H, d_h, D)`, Proposition 7, `exp63`–`exp66`)** eliminates $H(H-1)d_h^2 / 2$ cross-head Gram rotation constraints and reaches **`2.0731 ± 0.0014` in `11.04s` ($D=256, H=4$), `2.0458 ± 0.0003` in `11.90s` ($D=384, H=6$), `2.0344 ± 0.0005` in `15.77s` ($D=512, H=8$), and `2.0313 ± 0.0009` in `29.47s` ($D=768, H=12$) DEV BPB**—gaining **`-0.0037, -0.0061, -0.0083, -0.0105` BPB** monotonically with head count $H$, recovering **`96.5%`, `103.7%`, `96.0%`, and `87.5%`** of the 360-step shared-LR 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain (`2.0789 -> 2.0590`, `2.0532 -> 2.0316`, `2.0442 -> 2.0191`, `2.0433 -> 2.0153`; or **`37.5%`–`39.9%`** of the $D=384$ `Per-Head Global-BP` gain `2.0532 -> 1.9934/1.9916` and **`43.8%`–`48.0%`** of the $D=384$ `Per-Head Sub-Block Surrogate-BP` gain `2.0600 -> 2.0088`), and strictly beating the smaller 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation reference (`2.0789, 2.0532, 2.0442, 2.0433`) across every width by **`-0.0058` to `-0.0120` DEV BPB** (while trailing 6-matrix Exact Backpropagation `2.0590, 2.0316 [1.9934/1.9916 Per-Head], 2.0191, 2.0153` by `+0.0141` to `+0.0524` DEV BPB).
- **Three-Tier Attention Credit-Assignment Hierarchy ($W_V$ vs. $W_Q$ vs. $W_K$), Reward-Side Tangent Projection Variance (Proposition 5), Closed-Form Parameter-Free Wiener-MMSE Key Gather & Unconfounded Same-Rate ($\eta_K=0.024$) Controls (Proposition 6, `exp59`–`exp63`), Groupwise/Headwise Stiefel Polar Orthogonalization (Zhang et al. [12]; Proposition 7) vs. Deconfounded 3-Component Per-Head Pre-Polar Muon Package & Group Granularity Sweep $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ (`exp63`–`exp68`: `2.0458 ± 0.0003` [`Per-Head QK Pre-Polar`], `2.0455 ± 0.0001` [`Per-Head QK Exact SVD Polar, No Equilibration`], **`2.0443 ± 0.0002` [`Per-Head QKV Pre-Polar`, $g_Q=g_K=g_V=1$, and `2.0726, 2.0443, 2.0317, 2.0274` across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ in `exp70`]**, `2.0454 ± 0.0002` [`Per-Head QK + Joint V Exact SVD`, $g_{QK}=1, g_V=6$, `exp71`], & **`2.0436 ± 0.0002` [`Per-Head QKV Exact SVD Polar`, $g_Q=g_K=g_V=1$, unconfounded `-0.0018 ± 0.0002` BPB pure Exact-SVD $W_V$ decoupling gain]** at 360st; `1.9565 ± 0.0004` in `23.79s` [`Per-Head QK Pre-Polar`], `1.9561 ± 0.0008` [`Per-Head QK Exact SVD Polar`, vs. `1.9629 ± 0.0007` `Joint Exact SVD Polar`], `1.9558 ± 0.0012` [`Per-Head QK + Joint V Exact SVD`, `exp71`], **`1.9548 ± 0.0004` in `23.86s` [`Per-Head QKV Pre-Polar`]**, & **`1.9543 ± 0.0006` [`Per-Head QKV Exact SVD Polar`, unconfounded `-0.0015 ± 0.0006` BPB pure Exact-SVD $W_V$ decoupling gain]** at 720st; & `1.9524 ± 0.0004` in `19.91s` [`Fixed-QK`] / **`1.9509 ± 0.0003` in `19.67s` [`Fixed-QK+V`, `exp70`]** [Upper-Half $M=2$, 840st] on `d6`), Depth-Asymmetry Deconfounding Across Credit Engines (`exp63`), & Scale-Transfer to Native `50.3M` (`1.8978 -> 1.8861 ± 0.0016` [Upper-Half $M=2, \gamma_K=0.85, \eta_K=0.024$], `1.8849 ± 0.0003` [Upper-Half $M=2$ `Wiener-MMSE`], `1.8823 ± 0.0007` [Upper-Half $M=4, \gamma_K=0.85 + H_4, \eta_K=0.024$], `1.8803 ± 0.0007` [Upper-Half $M=4$ `Wiener-MMSE + H_4`], **`1.8779 ± 0.0002` [Upper-Half $M=2$ `Wiener-MMSE + Per-Head Stiefel` in `5.60s/step`]**, & **`1.8750 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB / `1.8616 ± 0.0003` VAL-B / `195.50` PPL [Upper-Half $M=2$ `Wiener-MMSE + Per-Head QKV` ($g_Q=g_K=g_V=1$) in `5.60s/step, 10.24 GiB` VRAM]**, `1.8729 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB / `1.8593 ± 0.0008` VAL-B / `194.36` PPL [Upper-Half $M=4$ `Wiener-MMSE + H_4 + Per-Head QK` in `6.34s/step`], & **`1.8702 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB / `1.8565 ± 0.0008` VAL-B / `192.86` PPL [Upper-Half $M=4$ `Wiener-MMSE + H_4 + Per-Head QKV` ($g_Q=g_K=g_V=1$) in `6.33s/step, 10.36 GiB` VRAM]**, `exp46`, `exp48`, `exp55`, `exp61`, `exp65`, `exp69`):** Building on the separate attention-internal jittering (including gates), per-head attention-output alignment, token-local query credit ($\gamma = 0$), and $\gamma$-discounted future-token key credit of Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`) and concurrent `general_pretraining_18/v8`–`v10` [5], we evaluate attention-routing credit assignment in our 3-stage delayed WM pipeline (`exp45`–`exp67`). Conditioned on fixed pre-attention activations and oracle downstream $\nabla_{y_{\text{attn}}}\mathcal{L}$ (`gy_attn` from `loss.backward()` at initialized `seed = 46`, Layer 0): (i) **Tier 1 (`attn.c_v`, $m_{\text{eff}} = 1$, coordinate-isolated & forward-factorizable via $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}$)** satisfies a conditional single-block reconstruction identity **`cos = 1.0000`** at $K=1$ (distinct from its `+0.9069` end-to-end delayed backward-free cosine in Table 1); (ii) **Tier 2 (`attn.c_q`, $m_{\text{eff}} = d_h$, token-local in time $\tau$ because query $q_\tau^{(h)}$ enters only row $\tau$ of $\mathbf{A}^{(h)}$, but coupled across the $d_h$ head channels inside $\langle q_\tau^{(h)}, k_s^{(h)}\rangle / \sqrt{d_h}$)** achieves empirical single-draw cosine **`cos = 0.1357`** at this initialized diagnostic state (comparable to $1/\sqrt{64} = 0.1250$), scaling with draw count $K$ to `0.2657` ($K=4$), `0.4748` ($K=16$), and `0.7385` ($K=64$); and (iii) **Tier 3 (`attn.c_k`, non-local across future queries $\tau \ge s$)** satisfies the conditional first-order expectation identity $\mathbb{E}[\widehat{g}_{k_s}^{(\gamma_K)}] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau + O(\sigma)$: same-position correlation ($\gamma_K = 0$) captures only $J_{ss}^\top g_s$ while truncating $\sum_{\tau > s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$ (`cos = 0.0076` at $K=1$, `0.0534` at $K=64$), whereas accumulating future query rewards $\widetilde{R}_{k,s}^{(\gamma_K)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s, \tau - s \le W_l} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} R_{\text{head},\tau}$ restores all future terms at $\gamma_K = 1$ (illustrated by our 2-token finite-difference Rademacher enumeration with `eps_fd = 1e-5`, `tol = 1e-9`) and trades a truncation bias whose size depends on the omitted future sensitivity mass at $\gamma_K \in \{0.85, 0.98\}$ for lower cross-time key superposition variance (`K=64` cosine: `0.0534` at $\gamma_K=0$, `0.1329` at $\gamma_K=0.85$, `0.1565` at $\gamma_K=0.98$, `0.1340` at $\gamma_K=1.00$). Furthermore, **Proposition 5** proves that for a normalized head vector $u \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ ($\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$) and fixed tangent sensitivity $g \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ ($g^\top u = 0$) expressed in the coordinate basis where $\xi \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ has independent Rademacher signs (specifically, the **pre-rotary coordinate frame** $(u_{\text{pre}}, g_{\text{pre}}) = (R_\theta^\top u_{\text{post}}, R_\theta^\top g_{\text{post}})$ when signs are drawn before orthogonal RoPE $R_\theta$), while forward `RMSNorm` removes radial perturbations to first order, unprojected reconstruction $\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) = (g^\top \xi)\xi$ has conditional covariance trace exceeding tangent-projected reconstruction $\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}(\xi) = P_u \hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi)$ by the exact fourth-moment difference $\Delta = \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}) - \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}) = \frac{1}{d_h}\mathbb{E}[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2] = \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{1 \le i < j \le d_h}(u_i g_j + u_j g_i)^2 = \|g\|_2^2 - \frac{2}{d_h}\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2 \ge 0$ (giving $\Delta = 1$ on $d_h=2, u=(\sqrt{2}, 0)^\top, g=(0, 1)^\top$ and $d_h=4, u=(2, 0, 0, 0)^\top, g=(0, 1, 0, 0)^\top$, and $\Delta = 0$ on the boundary $d_h=2, u=(1, 1)^\top, g=(1, -1)^\top$). Going beyond fixed recency discounting $\gamma_K^{\tau-s}$, **Proposition 6 (`exp59`–`exp63`)** analyzes general causal query-to-key gather weights $W_{\tau, s}$ in $\widetilde{R}_{k, s}^{(W)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} R_{\text{head}, \tau}$: solving for the per-pair linear minimum-MSE shrinkage weight under isotropic sensitivity prior across $M$ averaged Rademacher probes yields the closed-form parameter-free **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter** $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1)A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \in [0, 1]$, which approaches $1$ on dominant links ($A_{\tau, s} \to 1$, eliminating the $A_{\tau, s}^2$ squaring bias of `Rake` $W_{\tau, s}=A_{\tau, s}$) while suppressing cross-key superposition noise ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}} \to 0$) when query $\tau$ attends elsewhere ($A_{\tau, s} \to 0$). On `d6` ($M=4$, `exp59` Part 1), moving from $\gamma_K = 0.85$ $\to$ `rake_attn` $\to$ `wiener_mmse` increases 6-block mean key cosine alignment **`2.98x` at Step 0 (`0.0317 -> 0.0870 -> 0.0945`) and `3.22x` at Step 120 (`0.0284 -> 0.0727 -> 0.0914`, and `3.38x` on Upper-Half `3..5`: `0.0223 -> 0.0618 -> 0.0753`)**. Under the exact `exp49` update law (`exact_exp49_opt=True`, $\eta_K = 0.024$, `exp59`–`exp63`), unconfounded **same-rate ($\eta_K = 0.024$) $\gamma_K = 0.85$ controls (`exp63` Arms 1–3)** score `2.0531 ± 0.0005` in `11.80s` at 360 steps and **`1.9671 ± 0.0006` in `23.29s` (i.i.d.) / `1.9675 ± 0.0010` in `23.57s` (`Hadamard-4`) at 720 steps**—matching $\eta_K = 0.012$ (`1.9672 ± 0.0005`, `exp53`) within `-0.0001` BPB at 720 steps and showing that **`97.5%` (`-0.0039` of `-0.0040` BPB) of the 720-step `Wiener-MMSE` improvement (`1.9632 ± 0.0006` in `29.14s` [dense], `1.9633 ± 0.0009` in `24.36s` [sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$, `16.4%` faster], `1.9634 ± 0.0008` in `24.52s` [`Hadamard-4`], and `1.9632 ± 0.0009` in `24.54s` [`Hadamard-4 + Stage-2 leverage inversion`], paired seedwise differences `-0.0040 ± 0.0003` vs. `1.9672` and `-0.0035, -0.0040, -0.0042` = `-0.00390 ± 0.00036` vs. matched-$\eta_K=0.024$ `1.9671` [dense], and `-0.0043, -0.0038, -0.0043` = `-0.00413 ± 0.00029` vs. matched `Hadamard-4` `1.9675`, recovering `68.8%` [`-0.0357 / -0.0519` BPB] of the 720-step 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain) comes from replacing $\gamma_K=0.85$ with `Wiener-MMSE` at matched $\eta_K = 0.024$** (while at 360 steps, `Wiener-MMSE + Hadamard-4` reaches `2.0519 ± 0.0009` in `12.20s`, recovering **`75.5%` [`-0.0163 / -0.0216` BPB]** of the 360-step 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain `2.0532 -> 2.0316`). Furthermore, adapting the **Group Muon head-grouping framework of Zhang et al. (`arXiv:2605.08933v1`, 2026) [12]** to backward-free perturbation credit (**Proposition 7**, `exp63`–`exp67`), for full-row-rank update blocks $G = [G_1^\top, \dots, G_{H/g}^\top]^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ ($m_i = g d_h$), the **idealized groupwise exact Stiefel polar factor** $U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}} = (G_i G_i^\top)^{-1/2} G_i = U_{\text{svd}, i} V_{\text{svd}, i}^\top \in \operatorname{St}(g d_h, D)$ defined by Zhang et al. [12] eliminates inter-group cross-head orthogonality constraints $U_i^{\text{joint}} (U_j^{\text{joint}})^\top = 0$, preserves exact Frobenius energy $\|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{grp}}\|_F^2 = \sum_i g d_h = D = \|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}\|_F^2$, and satisfies Zhang et al.'s [12] nuclear-norm inequality $\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{grp}}(G), G \rangle_F = \sum_i \|G_i\|_* \ge \|G\|_* = \langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(G), G \rangle_F$. Because $G$ in backward-free WM is a noisy perturbation estimator $\hat{G} = \bar{G} + \Delta$, greater self-alignment $\sum_i \|\hat{G}_i\|_* \ge \|\hat{G}\|_*$ with the noisy input $\hat{G}$ does not by itself guarantee higher alignment with the population gradient $\bar{G}$ or improved training loss; moreover, our **implemented 3-component Per-Head Pre-Polar Muon package** (`2-pass row/column RMS equilibration + 5-step BF16 PolarExpress + per-head Frobenius sphere restoration`) can algebraically reverse alignment ordering against $G$ on adversarial $2 \times 2$ inputs ($G = \begin{pmatrix}100 & 1 \\ 1 & 2\end{pmatrix}$: `102.2411 > 102.0000` raw exact polar $\to$ `73.5391 < 100.4581` after 2-pass equilibration, `exp66` Part 1A). To test these mechanisms empirically on ClimbMix `d6`, our **real checkpoint audits (`exp66` Part 1B & `exp67` Part 1, Step 0 and Step 120 across $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$)** show that $M=4$ perturbation noise keeps $\hat{G}_{Q,i}, \hat{G}_{K,i}$ near-full-rank at Step 120 ($\operatorname{rank}_{99\%}/m_i = 0.9245$ and $0.8012$ at $g=1$), keeping Zhang et al.'s [12] (`Appendix D.2`) polynomial approximate-whitening Frobenius norm gap modest (`+20.01` and `+30.13` vs. `380.61` and `365.06`) while monotonically lifting oracle gradient cosines from $g=6 \to 3 \to 2 \to 1$ (`0.1311 -> 0.1529 -> 0.1664 -> 0.1804` on $W_Q$ and `0.0388 -> 0.0452 -> 0.0495 -> 0.0557` on $W_K$ at Step 120). Across our **selected deconfounding, granularity, and per-head surrogate-hierarchy experiments (`exp66` Part 2, `exp67` Part 2, & `exp68` Part 2, `23` three-seed arms = `69` runs)**: (i) in our selected `{Joint (g=6), Per-Head QK (g_QK=1), Per-Head QKV (g_QKV=1)} x {Pre-Polar Muon, Exact FP32 SVD Polar} x {Global Sphere, Per-Head Sphere}` deconfounding controls, sphere-retraction granularity changes DEV BPB by `0.0000–0.0001` BPB (`2.0519 ± 0.0009` vs. `2.0519 ± 0.0009` at 360st; `1.9566 ± 0.0005` vs. `1.9565 ± 0.0004` at 720st), while **Pure Exact FP32 SVD Polar Factor [no equilibration, global sphere]** improves from `2.0521 ± 0.0010` ($g=6$) to **`2.0455 ± 0.0001`** ($g_{QK}=1$, a **`-0.0066 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB** contrast) and **`2.0436 ± 0.0002` (`2.0064 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `273.61` PPL, `exp68` Arm 2, $g_{QKV}=1$, a `-0.0085 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB contrast)** at 360 steps, and from **`1.9629 ± 0.0007` (`1.9277 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `219.18` PPL, `exp67` Arm 8, $g=6$)** to **`1.9561 ± 0.0008` (`1.9210 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `215.15` PPL, `exp66` Arm 6, $g_{QK}=1$, a `-0.0068 ± 0.0011` DEV BPB contrast)** and **`1.9543 ± 0.0006` (`1.9192 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `214.08` PPL, `exp68` Arm 3, $g_{QKV}=1$, a `-0.0086 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB contrast)** at 720 steps, closely matching 5-step BF16 `Pre-Polar Muon` (`2.0519 -> 2.0458 -> 2.0443` at 360st and `1.9634 -> 1.9565 -> 1.9548` at 720st); (ii) unlike GPT-2 Small backpropagation in Zhang et al. [12] where intermediate grouping overtakes $g=1$ later in training, backward-free WM in our short-horizon ClimbMix `d6` regime improves monotonically as $g$ decreases from $6 \to 3 \to 2 \to 1$ at **both 360 steps (`2.0519 -> 2.0504 ± 0.0007 -> 2.0486 ± 0.0002 -> 2.0458 ± 0.0003`) and 720 steps (`1.9634 -> 1.9615 ± 0.0015 -> 1.9599 ± 0.0005 -> 1.9565 ± 0.0004`, `exp67` Arms 1–4)** (we keep our Step-0/Step-120 checkpoint rank and batch sub-block-skip oracle-cosine audit provisional as a descriptive diagnostic rather than a causal proof of why this short-horizon regime differs from [12]); (iii) because the exact value gradient satisfies $(A_h^\top E_h)^\top X = E_h^\top (A_h X)$ (`exp68` Part 1A), head-filtered value inputs are an associative algebraic identity shared by both exact backpropagation and backward-free WM, and applying $g_V=1$ (`Fixed-QK+V`, $g_Q=g_K=g_V=1$) on ClimbMix `d6` improves **both** Backward-Free Delayed WM (**`2.0443 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB [`2.0071` VAL-B, `274.12` PPL, `exp67` Arm 5]** under Pre-Polar and **`2.0436 ± 0.0002` [`exp68` Arm 2]** under Exact SVD at 360 steps; **`1.9548 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB [`1.9198` VAL-B, `214.38` PPL, `23.86s`, `exp68` Arm 1]** under Pre-Polar and **`1.9543 ± 0.0006` [`exp68` Arm 3]** under Exact SVD at 720 steps, gaining `-0.0015` to `-0.0019` BPB over `Fixed-QK` and recovering **`110.6%` / `113.9%`** at 360st and **`85.0%` / `85.9%`** at 720st of the shared-LR 4-to-6-matrix Global-BP gain, **`38.8%` / `39.9%`** at 360st and **`49.7%` / `50.3%`** at 720st of the `Per-Head QKV Global-BP` gain, and **`46.7%` / `48.0%`** at 360st and **`50.1%` / `50.6%`** at 720st of the `Per-Head Sub-Block Surrogate-BP` gain) **and** Exact Global Backpropagation (**`1.9916 ± 0.0003` DEV BPB [`1.9544` VAL-B, `237.17` PPL, `exp68` Arm 4]** at 360st and **`1.8536 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB [`1.8185` VAL-B, `162.34` PPL, `exp68` Arm 5]** at 720st, gaining `-0.0018` and `-0.0013` BPB over `Per-Head QK Global BP`); and (iv) completing the matched **4-level `Per-Head QK` ($g_Q=g_K=1$) surrogate hierarchy (`exp67` Arms 6–7 & `exp68` Arms 6–9)** preserves the expected ordering across the four credit regimes under the specified recipe at 360 steps (**`Global-BP` `1.9934 ± 0.0002` $\to$ `Block-Skip` `2.0068 ± 0.0007` $\to$ `Sub-Block` `2.0088 ± 0.0004` $\to$ `Delayed-WM` `2.0458 ± 0.0003`**) and improves every 720-step credit engine by an invariant `-0.0069` to `-0.0078` BPB over Joint $g=6$ (**`Global-BP` `1.8549 ± 0.0007` $\xrightarrow{+0.0343}$ `Block-Skip` `1.8892 ± 0.0009` $\xrightarrow{+0.0046}$ `Sub-Block` `1.8938 ± 0.0005` $\xrightarrow{+0.0627\text{ (QK)} / +0.0610\text{ (QKV)}}$ `Delayed-WM` `1.9565 ± 0.0004` [`1.9548` QKV]**). Meanwhile, 840-step matched-time Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=2$ `Per-Head Pre-Polar + Wiener-MMSE + TopK-32` reaches **`1.9524 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB (`1.9177 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `212.99` PPL in `19.91s`)—outperforming 1,004-step 4-Matrix Delayed WM (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`) by `-0.0196` DEV BPB (`-0.0215` VAL-B, `-11.74` PPL) in `1.64s` less time**. Simultaneously, our **720-step depth-asymmetry deconfounding across credit engines (`exp63` Arms 4–7)** under `exact_exp49_opt=True` shows that under **Exact Global BP**, Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` (`1.8759 ± 0.0003` in `13.94s`, `83.2%` of full 6-block gain `1.9423 -> 1.8625`) beats Lower-Half `(0, 1, 2)` (`1.8790 ± 0.0013` in `13.68s`, `79.3%`) by only `-0.0031` BPB, whereas detaching inter-block Jacobian composition under **Sub-Block Direct-Skip Surrogate BP** expands the Upper-vs.-Lower gap **$5.0\times$ to `-0.0155` BPB** (`1.9075 ± 0.0005` in `11.21s` vs. `1.9230 ± 0.0009` in `11.28s`, raising Upper-Half retention to **`92.7%` [`-0.0744 / -0.0803` BPB]**, matching Delayed WM's `90.1%`–`94.4%`; as a recipe-specific descriptive contrast between these two credit engines, `-0.0031 / -0.0155 = 20.0%` vs. `-0.0124 / -0.0155 = 80.0%`). Finally, on **Native `50,332,176`** ($L=8, D=512, H=4, d_h=128, T=2,048$, 100 steps $= 13.1\text{M}$ tokens, `exp65`), unconfounded same-rate ($\eta_K=0.024$) $\gamma_K=0.85$ controls on Upper-Half `(4, 5, 6, 7)` score **`1.8861 ± 0.0016` DEV BPB (`1.8729 ± 0.0018` VAL-B)** at $M=2$ and **`1.8823 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.8690 ± 0.0010` VAL-B)** at $M=4 + \text{Hadamard-4}$ (confirming that `Wiener-MMSE` `1.8849 ± 0.0003` [$M=2$] and `1.8803 ± 0.0007` [$M=4 + H_4$] outperform $\gamma_K=0.85$ at matched $\eta_K=0.024$), while **Per-Head Rectangular Stiefel `Pre-Polar Muon` `(4, 128, 512)` (`exp65` Arms 3–4)** improves Upper-Half `(4..7)` `Wiener-MMSE` at $M=2$ by **`-0.0070` DEV BPB (`-0.0073` VAL-B, `-3.93` PPL)** to **`1.8779 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`1.8644 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `197.06` PPL, `5.60s/step`, `10.21 GiB` VRAM)**—surpassing even $M=4$ Joint `Pre-Polar Muon` (`1.8803 ± 0.0007`) at half the SDPA probes—and improves $M=4$ `Wiener-MMSE + Hadamard-4` by **`-0.0074` DEV BPB (`-0.0075` VAL-B, `-4.06` PPL)** to **`1.8729 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.8593 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `194.36` PPL — `1.8730 / 1.8721 / 1.8737` in `6.34s/step`, `10.34 GiB` VRAM)**, and extending per-head Stiefel polar orthogonalization `(4, 128, 512)` to Tier-1 $W_V$ (`Fixed-QK+V`, $g_Q=g_K=g_V=1$ across all 8 value projections, `exp69`) further improves Upper-Half `(4..7)` $M=2$ by **`-0.0029` DEV BPB (`-0.0028` VAL-B)** to **`1.8750 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB (`1.8616 ± 0.0003` VAL-B, `195.50` PPL — `1.8754 / 1.8751 / 1.8746` in `5.60s/step`, `10.24 GiB` VRAM)** and improves $M=4$ `Wiener-MMSE + Hadamard-4` by **`-0.0027` DEV BPB (`-0.0028` VAL-B, `-1.50` PPL)** to **`1.8702 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.8565 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `192.86` PPL — `1.8704 / 1.8693 / 1.8709` in `6.33s/step`, `10.36 GiB` VRAM)**, widening the fixed-token gain over 4-matrix `exp43` (`1.8978 ± 0.0007`) by **`2.21x` to `-0.0276` DEV BPB (`-0.0279` VAL-B, `-15.53` PPL)**.

- **Energy-Matched Linear Spectral Tapering (`Exact-SVD Taper`, Proposition 8) vs. Equilibrated `PrePolar-Blend` ($\gamma_{QK}=0.20$, `exp72`–`exp75`) and Un-Equilibrated Cubic Gram Spectral Tapering (`Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` & `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper`, $\gamma_Q=0.20, \gamma_K=0.35$) & Gavish-Donoho [13] Optimal Singular-Value Shrinkage Under a Motivating Marchenko-Pastur Perturbation-Noise Heuristic (Proposition 9, `exp76`: `2.0443 -> 2.0382 ± 0.0003` [`PrePolar-Blend`] / `2.0377 ± 0.0003` [`Exact-SVD Taper`] / **`2.0337 ± 0.0004` [`Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` in `14.49s`, matching `2.0337 ± 0.0004` `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` & `2.0337 ± 0.0017` `Gavish-Donoho`]** at 360st, and **`2.0640, 2.0337, 2.0206, 2.0138` across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$**; `1.9548 -> 1.9476 ± 0.0008` [`PrePolar-Blend`] / `1.9465 ± 0.0007` [`Exact-SVD Taper`] / **`1.9407 ± 0.0006` [`Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` in `29.07s`] / `1.9408 ± 0.0007` [`Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper`] / `1.9401 ± 0.0021` [`Gavish-Donoho`]** at 720st; **`1.9420 -> 1.9337 ± 0.0015` [`202.29` PPL in `22.90s`]** at 840st Upper-Half $M=2$; and **`1.8620 ± 0.0027` [`Exact-SVD Taper` $M=2$, `exp75`], `1.8592 ± 0.0002` [`PrePolar-Blend` $M=2$] / `1.8583 ± 0.0030` [`Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` $M=2$, `186.53` PPL], & `1.8554 ± 0.0008` [`PrePolar-Blend` $M=4$, `184.97` PPL]** on Long-Context Native `50,332,176`):** Auditing post-polar alignment $\text{cosU} := \cos(U(\hat{G}), U(G_{\text{oracle}}))$ vs. pre-polar alignment $\text{cosG} := \cos(U(\hat{G}), G_{\text{oracle}})$ across ClimbMix `d6` checkpoints (`exp72` Part 1 & `exp76` Part 1) motivates why hard polar equalization ($\sigma_r \mapsto 1$) suits coordinate-isolated Tier-1 `W_V` ($g_V=1$) and `mlp.c_fc` ($k_{\text{mlp}}=1$, where $\text{cosU} = 0.9731$ and $0.9352$ at Step 120, whereas row-splitting tall $1536 \times 384$ `mlp.c_fc` into $k_{\text{mlp}}=4$ square $384 \times 384$ blocks breaks column-orthonormality $U^\top U = I_{384}$, drops $\text{cosU}: 0.9352 \to 0.8076$, and leaves 360/720-step DEV BPB unchanged at `2.0444 ± 0.0002` / `1.9546 ± 0.0005` in `exp72` Arms 3–4, and tapering `W_V/mlp.c_fc` with $\gamma_{V,\text{mlp}}=0.10$ in `exp75` Arm 4 slightly degrades 720-step DEV BPB from `1.9476` to `1.9480 ± 0.0009`) while remaining limited on subspace-coupled Tier-2/Tier-3 $(W_Q, W_K)$ ($\text{cosU} = 0.1682$ on $W_Q$, $0.0658$ on $W_K$, noting that small observed singular values $\hat{\sigma}_r$ alone do not prove a mode is pure perturbation noise): when $M \in \{2, 4\}$ head-coupled probes populate all $d_h$ channels of each head block $\hat{G}_h \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h \times D}$, hard polar equalization allocates full unit energy ($s_r = 1$) to trailing singular modes. **Proposition 8** proves that for un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Taper` (`exact_svd_taper_batched` in `run_exp73` and `run_exp65`), $U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h) := \sqrt{d_h}\frac{(1-\gamma)U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h} \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F}{\|(1-\gamma)U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h} \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F\|_F}$ preserves exact left/right singular frames and exact Frobenius update energy $\|U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h)\|_F^2 = d_h = \|U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h)\|_F^2$ while mapping normalized singular values $\hat{\sigma}_r := \sqrt{d_h}\sigma_r/\|\hat{G}_h\|_F$ to $s_r(\gamma) = \frac{(1-\gamma) + \gamma \hat{\sigma}_r}{c(\gamma)}$ with $c(\gamma) = \sqrt{(1-\gamma)^2 + 2\gamma(1-\gamma)\bar{\sigma} + \gamma^2} \le 1$, strictly attenuating modes below the exact threshold $\hat{\sigma}_r < \hat{\sigma}^*(\gamma) := \frac{c(\gamma) - (1-\gamma)}{\gamma} \le 1$ and boosting modes with $\hat{\sigma}_r > \hat{\sigma}^*(\gamma)$ (verified in float64 & pure-stdlib Check 4E both on the 3-mode threshold counterexample $(\sqrt{2.18}, 0.9, 0.1)$ at $\gamma=0.2$, where $\hat{\sigma}^*(0.2) = 0.858390 < 0.9 < 1 \implies s_2(0.2) = 1.008565 > 1$, and on $G_{\text{bar}}=\operatorname{diag}(4,2,0,0), \hat{G}=\operatorname{diag}(4,2,1,1)$, where $\gamma=0 \to 0.2$ cuts orthogonal noise-subspace energy from `50.00% -> 41.12%` and lifts $\cos(U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}, G_{\text{bar}}): 0.6708 \to 0.7451$ and $\cos(U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}, U_{\text{bar}}): 0.7071 \to 0.7648$). We explicitly distinguish un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Taper` from our fast polynomial heuristic `PrePolar-Blend` (`prepolar_blend_batched` in `run_exp72`–`run_exp75`), which applies 2 passes of row/column $\ell_2$ equilibration $X = \mathcal{E}_2(\hat{G}_h)$ before 5-step BF16 `PolarExpress` $O_{\text{pe}} = \text{PolarExpress}(X)$ and blends $(1-\gamma)O_{\text{pe}} + \gamma \|O_{\text{pe}}\|_F \frac{X}{\|X\|_F}$ (because equilibration alters singular vectors and singular values prior to blending—for example, mapping $\operatorname{diag}(4,2,1,1) \mapsto I_4$—Proposition 8 governs `Exact-SVD Taper` rather than `PrePolar-Blend`). Across widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ at 360 steps (`exp72` Arm 1 & `exp75` Arms 1–3), `PrePolar-Blend` ($\gamma_{QK}=0.20$) reaches **`2.0675 ± 0.0017` ($D=256$), `2.0382 ± 0.0003` ($D=384$; `2.0377 ± 0.0003` `Exact-SVD Taper`), `2.0259 ± 0.0002` ($D=512$), and `2.0207 ± 0.0008` ($D=768$) DEV BPB**—gaining **`-0.0051, -0.0061, -0.0058, -0.0067` BPB** over untapered `Fixed-QK+V` and strictly beating 4-Matrix Exact BP (`2.0789, 2.0532, 2.0442, 2.0433`) across every width (while trailing matched-optimizer Per-Head Global BP `1.9899` / `1.9879 ± 0.0006` at $D=384$, `exp71`/`exp75`). At 720 steps (`exp72` Arm 2 & `exp73` Arm 2), `PrePolar-Blend` reaches **`1.9476 ± 0.0008` in `23.78s`** and un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Taper` reaches **`1.9465 ± 0.0007` (`1.9115 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `209.56` PPL, `128.55s`)**. Going further, **Proposition 9 (`exp76`)** uses the Marchenko-Pastur law at aspect ratio $\beta = d_h/D = 1/6$ as a **motivating random-matrix heuristic** (noting that real WM updates have correlated pre-attention inputs $X$, Hadamard-$4$ multiplexing, and Wiener-MMSE key gathering rather than i.i.d. isotropic Gaussian noise, so empirical bulk counts are descriptive rather than unconditional noise proofs): on ClimbMix `d6` ($d_h=64, D=384$), only **`13.0–20.6` (`20.4%–32.2%`) of the 64 singular modes** of $\hat{G}_Q, \hat{G}_K$ exceed the reference MP edge $1 + \sqrt{1/6} \approx 1.40825$ ($\mu_{1/6} = 0.94415701$). Accordingly: (i) applying the **Gavish-Donoho (`arXiv:1405.7511`; optimal shrinker `arXiv:1305.5870`) [13] piecewise optimal singular-value shrinker** $\eta^*(y) = \frac{1}{y}\sqrt{((y^2 - \beta - 1)^2 - 4\beta)_+}$ for $y = \sigma_r / (\sigma_{\text{med}}/\sqrt{\mu_\beta}) > 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ and $\eta^*(y) = 0$ for $y \le 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ (`gavish_donoho_pure`, `exp76` Arms 3 & 8) zeros out the $\sim 75\%$ trailing bulk and reaches **`2.0337 ± 0.0017` DEV BPB (`1.9961` VAL-B, `266.21` PPL, `48.96s`)** at 360 steps and **`1.9401 ± 0.0021` DEV BPB (`1.9051 ± 0.0021` VAL-B, `205.88` PPL, `98.28s`)** at 720 steps; and (ii) to eliminate the $3.4\times$ SVD runtime overhead and escape the 2-pass equilibration diagonal collapse of `PrePolar-Blend`'s skip branch, forming the **un-equilibrated cubic Gram-filtered skip tensor** $G_3 := (G_n G_n^\top) G_n$ from $G_n = \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F$ (two batched $d_h \times d_h$ and $d_h \times D$ matrix multiplications, **zero SVD**) raises normalized singular values to the cube ($\hat{\sigma}_r^{(3)} = \frac{\sqrt{d_h}\sigma_r^3}{\sqrt{\sum_j \sigma_j^6}}$, suppressing modes $\sigma_r \le \sigma_1/3$ by $\ge 27\times$ in amplitude and $\ge 729\times$ in energy while preserving exact left/right singular vectors in `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper`, Check 4F): blending $(1-\gamma)O_{\text{pe}} + \gamma \|O_{\text{pe}}\|_F \frac{G_3}{\|G_3\|_F}$ (where `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend`, `exp76` Arms 9–10, retains the 2-pass equilibrated polar branch $O_{\text{pe}} = \text{PolarExpress}(\mathcal{E}_2(\hat{G}_h))$, whereas `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper`, Arms 11–12, uses the un-equilibrated $U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h)$) with tier-asymmetric $\gamma_Q = 0.20, \gamma_K = 0.35$ reaches **`2.0337 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB (`1.9961 ± 0.0003` VAL-B, `266.22` PPL) in `14.49s` at 360 steps (`3.38x` faster than SVD, matching `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` `2.0337 ± 0.0004` in `48.86s` to `0.0000` BPB!)** and **`1.9407 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9056 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `206.20` PPL) in `29.07s` at 720 steps** (matching `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` `1.9408 ± 0.0007` in `97.93s` to `0.0001` BPB and recovering `112.1%` [`-0.0582 / -0.0519` BPB] of the 720-step shared-LR 4-to-6-matrix Global-BP gain; against 720-step 4-Matrix Exact Global BP `1.9423 ± 0.0031`, 0-hop `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` `1.9407` and `Gavish-Donoho` `1.9401` achieve lower 3-seed mean DEV BPB with paired-$t$ differences **`-0.0016 ± 0.0026` [95% CI `[-0.0080, +0.0048]`]** and **`-0.0022 ± 0.0034` [95% CI `[-0.0106, +0.0062]`]**, i.e., statistical parity/near-tie at 0-hop before `exp77`'s 1-hop highway pipeline below)! At `840` steps of Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=2$ (`exp76` Arm 13), `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` reaches **`1.9337 ± 0.0015` DEV BPB (`1.8990 ± 0.0014` VAL-B, `202.29` PPL) in `22.90s`**—improving over 840st linear `PrePolar-Blend` (`1.9420`) by **`-0.0083` BPB** and beating 1,004-step 4-Matrix WM (`1.9720` in `21.55s`) by **`-0.0383` DEV BPB (`-22.44` PPL)**; across widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ at 360 steps (`exp76` Arms 9, 14–16), `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` reaches **`2.0640 ± 0.0007` ($D=256$), `2.0337 ± 0.0004` ($D=384$), `2.0206 ± 0.0013` ($D=512$), and `2.0138 ± 0.0002` ($D=768$) DEV BPB**—gaining **`-0.0086, -0.0106, -0.0111, -0.0136` BPB** monotonically with width over untapered `Fixed-QK+V`, strictly beating 4-Matrix Exact BP (`2.0789, 2.0532, 2.0442, 2.0433`) by `-0.0149` to `-0.0295` BPB, and reaching numerical proximity (`-0.0015 ± 0.0008` BPB, 95% CI `[-0.0034, +0.0003]`) to shared-`lr=0.006` Joint 6-Matrix Exact BP (`2.0153 ± 0.0005`) at $D=768$ (while trailing matched-optimizer Per-Head Global BP `1.9899` / `1.9879` / `1.9929` at $D=384$). Crucially, applying `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` ($\gamma_Q=0.20, \gamma_K=0.35$) to **noise-free Global and Sub-Block-Skip Backpropagation (`exp76` Arms 17–20)** reveals a **double dissociation**: whereas cubic Gram tapering improves noisy Delayed WM by **`-0.0106` BPB (360st) and `-0.0141` BPB (720st)** over hard polar (and `-0.0045` / `-0.0069` BPB over linear `PrePolar-Blend`), on noise-free backpropagation gradients it **degrades** Global BP (`1.9899 -> 1.9929 ± 0.0002` at 360st; `1.8530 -> 1.8559 ± 0.0006` at 720st) and Sub-Block-Skip BP (`2.0052 -> 2.0071 ± 0.0012` at 360st; `1.8920 -> 1.8941 ± 0.0013` at 720st) by `+0.0019` to `+0.0030` BPB vs. hard polar and `+0.0032` to `+0.0050` BPB vs. linear `PrePolar-Blend` (`1.9879` / `1.8518` and `2.0039` / `1.8904`). Finally, on **Long-Context Native `50,332,176`** ($L=8, D=512, H=4, d_h=128, T=2,048$, `exp74`, `exp75` Arms 7–8, & `exp76` Arms 21–22), deconfounded un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Polar` ($\gamma=0$, `exp75` Arm 7) scores **`1.8747 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`1.8613 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `195.32` PPL, `624.05s`)** and un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Taper` ($\gamma_{QK}=0.20$, `exp75` Arm 8) improves by **`-0.0127` DEV BPB** to **`1.8620 ± 0.0027` DEV BPB (`1.8479 ± 0.0026` VAL-B, `188.47` PPL, `624.29s`)**, while on Upper-Half `(4..7)` $M=2$ un-equilibrated `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` (`exp76` Arm 21) reaches **`1.8583 ± 0.0030` DEV BPB (`1.8442 ± 0.0029` VAL-B, `186.53` PPL — `1.8616 / 1.8558 / 1.8576` in `6.04s/step`, `10.25 GiB` VRAM)** and equilibrated `PrePolar-Blend` ($\gamma_{QK}=0.20$, `exp74` Arms 1–2) reaches **`1.8592 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`1.8450 ± 0.0003` VAL-B, `186.99` PPL, `5.57s/step`)** at $M=2$ and **`1.8554 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.8410 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `184.97` PPL, `6.32s/step`)** at $M=4$ (where 3-seed paired-$t$ 95% CIs on Native `50.3M` show near-tie parity among the tapered variants—`Gram3` vs. `Exact-SVD Taper` at $M=2$: `-0.0037 ± 0.0016`, 95% CI `[-0.0076, +0.0002]`; `Gram3` vs. `PrePolar-Blend` at $M=2$: `-0.0009 ± 0.0032`, 95% CI `[-0.0087, +0.0070]`; and `Gram3` vs. `PrePolar-Blend` at $M=4$: `+0.0042 ± 0.0042`, 95% CI `[-0.0063, +0.0146]`—while `PrePolar-Blend` $M=4$ significantly improves over untapered `Fixed-QK+V` `1.8702 ± 0.0008` by `-0.0148` DEV BPB and widens the fixed-token gain over 4-matrix `exp43` `1.8978 ± 0.0007` by $3.39\times$ to `-0.0424` DEV BPB)!
- **Breaking the 0-Hop Direct-Skip Compositional Barrier via Superposed 4-Stage Post-Install Dual 1-Hop (MLP + Attention-Value) Highway Weight Maximization (`Proposition 10`, `exp77`: `57` Three-Seed Arms = `171` Runs; `2.0337 -> 2.0291 ± 0.0005` at 360st, `1.9407 -> 1.9247 ± 0.0008` [`47.02s`] / **`1.9238 ± 0.0008` [`1.8888` VAL-B, `196.91` PPL, statistically significantly beating 720st 4-Mat Exact BP `1.9423` by `-0.0184 ± 0.0038`, 95% CI `[-0.0278, -0.0091]`]** at 720st, & **`1.9217 -> 1.9014 ± 0.0009` [`1.8664` VAL-B, `185.12` PPL, `47.53s`]** at 840st on `d6`; **`2.0606 ± 0.0006, 2.0291 ± 0.0005, 2.0157 ± 0.0009, 2.0082 ± 0.0009` across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ [statistically significantly beating shared-LR 6-Mat Exact BP `2.0153` at $D=768$ by `-0.0071 ± 0.0006`, 95% CI `[-0.0085, -0.0057]`]**; and **12-Layer Depth Scaling `d12` [$L=12, D=384$, `50.95M` Active, 28-Link Serial Graph in Fixed $O(1)=4$ Pipeline Slots]: `1.9370 ± 0.0011 -> 1.9239 ± 0.0006` [`1.8889` VAL-B, `196.95` PPL, `141.59s`, 95% CI `[-0.0149, -0.0112]`]**):** All 3-stage Delayed-WM arms in `exp26`–`exp76` route credit exclusively along **0-hop direct terminal-skip paths** ($l \to x_{\text{final}}$), creating a structural ceiling at `Sub-Block-Skip Surrogate BP` (`1.8920 ± 0.0007` vs. `Global BP` `1.8530 ± 0.0009` at 720st on `d6`, a `+0.0390` BPB compositional gap; and `1.8840 ± 0.0004` vs. `1.8378 ± 0.0008` on 12-layer `d12`, a `+0.0462` BPB gap). Decomposing this inter-subblock gap under matched $\eta_K=0.024$ Per-Head QKV Surrogate BP (`exp77`, `15` surrogate/global BP arms = `45` runs) reveals that **1-hop parallel highways across all downstream subblocks $i > l$** capture roughly half of the entire deep compositional gap without $\ge 2$-hop serial chaining: on `d6` (720st), `1-Hop Value Highway BP` reaches `1.8869 ± 0.0006` (`-0.0051` BPB = `13.1%`), `1-Hop MLP Highway BP` reaches `1.8769 ± 0.0011` (`-0.0151` BPB = `38.7%`, recovering `94.4%` of `All-Hop MLP Highway BP` `1.8760 ± 0.0012`), and **`Dual 1-Hop (MLP + Value) Highway BP` reaches `1.8731 ± 0.0003` (`1.8380` VAL-B, `171.27` PPL, `-0.0189` BPB = `48.5%` of the total `d6` compositional gap; `1.9983 ± 0.0002` at 360st = `45.1%`; and `1.8604 ± 0.0011` on 12-layer `d12` at 720st = `51.1%` [`-0.0236 / -0.0462` BPB] of the 12-layer compositional gap)**, while the remaining `~50%` (`1.8731 -> 1.8530`) requires nonlinear query/key attention routing (`z_attn -> Q, K`) and $\ge 2$-hop paths. Extending beyond the residual input-bus pooling in [10], **Proposition 10 (`exp77`)** proves how to realize both 1-hop MLP and 1-hop Attention-Value branch transport **with zero reverse error matmuls through Transformer backbone weights, zero VJP helpers, and zero same-step relays in a fixed $O(1)$ 4-stage post-install pipeline buffer independent of depth $L$ and width $D$**: (i) at step $t$, batch $B_{t-2}$'s 0-hop Stage-1 MLP hidden modulation $u_{\text{mlp}}^{(i, 0)}(t-2)$ and post-attention value modulation $u_v^{(i, 0)}(t-2)$ are contracted via our forward-perturbation/outgoing-column WM reader against live `mlp.c_fc` weights $W_{\text{fc}}^{(i)}(t)$ and—after a local causal future-query attention gather $\tilde{u}_{v, s}^{(i, 0)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} A_{\tau, s}^{(i)} u_{v, \tau}^{(i, 0)}$ using cached forward causal attention probabilities $A^{(i)}(t-2)$ (**zero extra forward passes**)—against live `attn.c_v` weights $W_v^{(i)}(t)$, followed in `exp77_1hop_mlp_wm_engine.py` by direct reward-side radial tangent projection $P_{x_n^{(i)}}$, whose exact first-order conditional expectation is $\frac{1}{\operatorname{rms}(z_n^{(i)})} P_{x_n^{(i)}} \operatorname{diag}(1 - (x_{n, d}^{(i)})^2 / D) W^\top u_n^{(i, 0)}$ (differing from the exact 1-hop Surrogate-BP Jacobian by Proposition 1(c)'s $O(\max_d x_{n, d}^2 / D)$ diagonal coordinate-leverage factor, whereas inserting the closed-form leverage inverse $L(x) = \operatorname{diag}(1 - x_d^2/D)^{-1}$ prior to $P_x$—verified as an exact theoretical corollary alongside the uncorrected estimator using the full tangent displacement $(P_x \xi)_d$ across all $2^8 = 256$ Rademacher sign configurations in `verify_paper5.py` Check 4G, and evaluated on 0-hop WM in `exp60` Arms 3 & 6, though not applied in the `exp77` training runs—eliminates this diagonal factor identically; in a separate fixed-state simultaneous $\sigma = 10^{-3}, G = BT$ contraction diagnostic on ClimbMix `d6` in `audit_exp77_mechanisms.py` and `exp77_dual_1hop_highway_wm_results.json` [`step_0_cosine_alignment` and `step_120_cosine_alignment`, evaluated at initialization and at a 120-step Global-BP-warmed checkpoint across the 5 linear/separable matrix families], each contraction matches its corresponding Surrogate-BP target to $\cos \in [0.9978, 0.9996]$ while Step-120 cosine alignment vs. **Exact Global Backpropagation** rises from `0.8322 -> 0.9731` on `wte`, `0.8969 [L0: 0.7299] -> 0.9878 [L0: 0.9598]` on 6-layer mean `attn.c_v`, `0.9061 [L0: 0.7650] -> 0.9866 [L0: 0.9608]` on `attn.c_proj`, `0.9415 [L0: 0.8418] -> 0.9917 [L0: 0.9764]` on `mlp.c_fc`, and `0.9408 [L0: 0.8437] -> 0.9913 [L0: 0.9758]` on `mlp.c_proj`); and (ii) rather than delaying 0-hop credit to $(t-3, t-4)$ (`delayed_4stage` $\beta=0$, which incurs a `+0.0029 ± 0.0018` BPB staleness penalty `1.9407 -> 1.9435`), **Superposed Multi-Horizon Delivery** delivers 0-hop credit immediately at $(t-1, t-2)$ and superposes $\beta_{\text{1hop}}$-weighted 1-hop credit at $(t-3, t-4)$ (`qk_p4comb`: Stage-1 $(Q, K, V, \text{fc})$ reads the combined $(0\text{-hop} + 1\text{-hop})$ Stage-2 modulation of $B_{t-3}$ at $t$!) linearly inside Muon's Nesterov momentum buffer before a **single** `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` orthogonalization per step (`1.9362 ± 0.0003` vs. `delayed_4stage` `1.9393 ± 0.0013` on 1-hop MLP alone, paired gain **`-0.0031 ± 0.0010` BPB, 95% CI `[-0.0056, -0.0006]`**). Combining both highways (**Superposed 4-Stage Dual 1-Hop [MLP + Val] Highway WM**) reaches **`2.0291 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`1.9914` VAL-B, `262.90` PPL, `24.12s`) at 360st** (95% CI `[-0.0067, -0.0024]` vs. v14 0-hop `2.0337`), **`1.9247 ± 0.0008` (`47.02s`, $\beta=0.75$) and `1.9238 ± 0.0008` (`1.8888 ± 0.0009` VAL-B, `196.91` PPL, `73.17s`, $\beta=0.75$ `qk_p4comb`, matching `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` `1.9253 ± 0.0008` vs. `1.9255 ± 0.0012` at $\beta=0.50$, 95% CI `[-0.0013, +0.0008]`) at 720st**—gaining **`-0.0168 ± 0.0014` DEV BPB (95% CI `[-0.0202, -0.0134]`)** over v14 0-hop (`1.9407`), **recovering `89.4%` (`-0.0169 / -0.0189`) of the Surrogate-BP compositional gain**, and **statistically significantly beating 720-step 4-Matrix Exact Global BP (`1.9423 ± 0.0031`) by `-0.0184 ± 0.0038` DEV BPB (95% CI `[-0.0278, -0.0091]`, `-10.20` PPL)**! At `840` steps (`d6` $M=4$), Dual 1-Hop WM reaches **`1.9014 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.8664 ± 0.0011` VAL-B, `185.12` PPL, `47.53s`)**, beating matched 840-step 0-hop $M=4$ (`1.9217 ± 0.0003` in `37.52s`) by **`-0.0203 ± 0.0012` BPB (95% CI `[-0.0233, -0.0173]`)**; across widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ at 360st, Dual 1-Hop WM reaches **`2.0606 ± 0.0006, 2.0291 ± 0.0005, 2.0157 ± 0.0009, 2.0082 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB** (monotonically widening 1-hop gains `-0.0034, -0.0046, -0.0049, -0.0056` BPB over v14 0-hop with all four 95% CIs excluding zero, and **statistically significantly beating shared-LR 6-Matrix Exact BP `2.0153 ± 0.0005` at $D=768$ by `-0.0071 ± 0.0006` BPB, 95% CI `[-0.0085, -0.0057]`**); and on **12-layer `d12`** ($L=12, D=384$, `50.95M` active, a 28-link serial graph trained in the exact same $O(1)=4$ pipeline slots), Dual 1-Hop WM improves over `d12` 0-hop WM from `2.0308 ± 0.0005 -> 2.0270 ± 0.0003` at 360st and from **`1.9370 ± 0.0011` (`99.92s`) to `1.9239 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.8889 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `196.95` PPL, `141.59s`) at 720st—a `-0.0131 ± 0.0008` BPB gain (95% CI `[-0.0149, -0.0112]`)**!
- **Relaxing the Single-Chart Terminal Representation Assumption ($P_l = I_D$) in 3-Stage (`t-2`) Global-Backward-Free Delayed WM via Low-Rank Predictive (`LP-WM`) and Oja-Stiefel Coordinate-Transport Bridges (`Oja-LP-WM`, `Proposition 11`, `exp78` & `exp79`: `27` Three-Seed Arms = `81` Runs Across Seeds `42, 43, 44`; Surrogate-BP Ceiling Improved `2.0065 -> 2.0001 ± 0.0011` on `d6` [95% CI `[-0.0072, -0.0054]`] & `2.0031 -> 2.0011 ± 0.0012` on `d12` [95% CI `[-0.0038, -0.0003]`]; 2-Call Column-Isolated Local Bridge Pullback Eliminates Input-Space $(D-1)\|V_l U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$ and Latent-Space $(r-1)\|U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$ Scalar Transport Variance; Unlike synthetic gradients (`Decoupled Neural Interfaces` [14], which train an auxiliary network against backpropagated downstream gradients $\nabla_{h_l}\mathcal{L}$ to predict the gradient vector directly; we note that neither the elementary Rademacher contraction variance identity nor Newton–Schulz polar retraction is itself a new mathematical principle, and instead focus on the learned forward-displacement coordinate bridge and its 3-stage empirical evaluation), in **Proposition 11 (`exp78` & `exp79`)** each block $l \in \{0, \dots, L-2\}$ maintains a lightweight rank-$r=64$ **Coordinate-Transport Bridge** $B_l(h) = h + U_l (V_l^\top h)$ trained by projected/retracted adaptive alternating least-squares (`oja_stiefel_bridge_step` in `exp79_oja_stiefel_bridge_and_d12_scaling.py`) on the readout-anchored forward displacement target $d_{l, n}^{\text{tgt}} = (\operatorname{sg}(\hat{h}_{L, n}) - \hat{h}_{l, n}) + 0.18\, w_{y_n}^{\text{norm}}$ purely from clean forward activations and target token embeddings (with $V_l \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ maintained **approximately orthogonal**, $V_l^\top V_l \approx I_r$, near the tall Stiefel manifold $\operatorname{St}(D, r)$ via column-normalized heuristic Gram-Frobenius scaling $c_0 = \max(1, (\|G_0\|_F/\sqrt{r})^{1/2})$ and finite $K_{\text{NS}}=3$ Newton-Schulz polar retraction steps per update [$K_{\text{NS}}=5$ at initialization, achieving $\max_{i,j}|(V_l^\top V_l - I_{64})_{i,j}| < 3\times 10^{-2}$ at $K_{\text{NS}}=3$ and $< 10^{-4}$ at $K_{\text{NS}}=5$ at $D=384, r=64$], and $(U_l, V_l)$ updated in ambient $\mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ by entrywise `AdamW` [$\beta_1=0.90, \beta_2=0.99, \epsilon=10^{-4}$] on the alternating residual-error Euclidean gradients of $\frac{1}{2N}\|d_l^{\text{tgt}} - (\hat{h}_l V_l)U_l^\top\|_F^2$ with trust-region cap $\|U_l\|_F/\sqrt{D} \le 0.25$, plus an optional next-block bridge $N_l(h) = h + U_{N,l}(V_{N,l}^\top h)$ predicting $\operatorname{sg}(\hat{h}_{l+1}) - \hat{h}_l$, adding only $0.67\%$ [`+0.25M`] active parameters on `d6` and $0.82\%$ [`+0.54M`] on `d12`): (i) **Stage-1 downstream error modulation $e_l \in \mathbb{R}^D$ across the auxiliary bridge $B_l(h) = h + U_l(V_l^\top h)$ at `prev1` without reverse error matrix multiplications through Transformer backbone reconstructs $\widehat{a}_{\text{in}} = (\xi_h^\top V_l U_l^\top e_l)\xi_h$ with conditional covariance trace $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}_{\xi_h}(\widehat{a}_{\text{in}}) = (D - 1)\|V_l U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$ (which equals $(D - 1)\|U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$, or $383\times$ signal energy at $D=384$, when $V_l^\top V_l = I_r$, and holds approximately up to the finite-$K_{\text{NS}}$ retraction residual when $V_l^\top V_l \approx I_r$), or drawing latent-space signs $\xi_z \sim \operatorname{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^r)$, which yields $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}_{\xi_z}(\hat{z}_{\text{scal}}) = (r - 1)\|U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$ ($63\times$ signal energy at $r = 64$)—versus our **2-call column-isolated local bridge pullback** (`column_isolated`, `exp78` & `exp79` Arms 5–10, 14–18, `backbone_reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0`, `bridge_local_pullback_calls_per_step = 20` on single-horizon `d6`), which invokes our fused Triton WM kernel twice at $M_g=1$ (`compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(U_l, e_l, sigma * xi_z, G=2048)` to materialize $u_{z, j} = R_{z, j}\xi_{z, j}/\sigma = e_l^\top U_{l, :, j}$ with `var = 0`, followed in the same step by `compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(V_l.t(), u_z, sigma * xi_h, G=2048)` to obtain $u_h = V_l u_z = V_l(U_l^\top e_l)$ with `var = 0`, an exact two-map local pullback across the block-local rank-$r$ auxiliary bridge $(U_l, V_l)$ with zero backbone reverse matmuls and zero $A_i^\top u_v$ attention pullbacks). In `exp79` Arms 3–4 (`oja_lp_scalar` / `oja_lp_scalar_mh`, `2.0371 ± 0.0004` and `2.0375 ± 0.0006` vs. `2.0335 ± 0.0006` and `2.0335 ± 0.0004` in Arms 5–6, a `-0.0036` to `-0.0041` BPB contrast, $p \le 0.0016$), we explicitly note that `scalar_mode=True` also omits Step-1 cross-covariance seeding, uses pre-schedule bridge learning rates $(\eta_V, \eta_U) = (0.006, 0.006)$ rather than $(0.04, 0.004)$, and column-normalizes $U_l$ in the encoder update, so the empirical contrast between Arms 3–4 and Arms 5–6 compares these two complete bridge recipes rather than isolating the Stage-2 contraction alone; and (iii) **Rescaled Step-1 Cross-Covariance Cold-Start Seeding, Empirical $R_l^2(t)$ Wiener Gating, & 720-Step `d6` and `d12` Scaling (`Proposition 11(a)`, `exp78` Arms 6–9 & `exp79` Arms 7–10, 13–18):** Seeding $U_l(1) = \alpha_{\text{init}} C_{\text{init}}$ with $\alpha_{\text{init}} = 0.030\sqrt{D}/\|C_{\text{init}}\|_F$ and $C_{\text{init}} = \frac{1}{BT}(d_l^{\text{tgt}})^\top (\hat{h}_l V_l)$ from Step 1's clean forward activations lifts Step-1 layer-mean explained variance $R_l^2(1) = 1 - \|d_l^{\text{tgt}} - (\hat{h}_l V_l)U_l^\top\|_F^2 / \|d_l^{\text{tgt}}\|_F^2$ from `0.0000` (`U_l(0)=0` cold start in `exp78`) to **`0.2798–0.2905` (`0.2075–0.3622` per-layer across `d6` and `d12` column-isolated `Oja-LP-WM` arms, vs. `0.1835–0.1935` layer-mean [`0.1285–0.2530` per-layer] after Step 1's unseeded AdamW update on `oja_lp_scalar`, growing to `0.5189–0.5658` layer-mean [`0.3346–0.6610` per-layer] at Step 720 and up to `0.7469` per-layer at Step 360)** while gating bridge injection by $g_l(t) = \operatorname{clip}(\text{ema}(R_l^2(t))^{1/2}\cdot 1.35, 0.35, 1.0)$. At `720` steps on `d6` (Seeds `42, 43, 44`), 3-stage `LP-LowRank-WM` (`1.9373 ± 0.0003`, `30.86s`), `LP-Multi-Horizon-WM` (`1.9370 ± 0.0004`, `1.9017` VAL-B, `204.13` PPL, `34.44s`), `Oja-LP-WM` (`1.9371 ± 0.0006`, `1.9017` VAL-B, `204.17` PPL, `32.78s`), and `Oja-LP-MH-WM` (`1.9371 ± 0.0007`, `36.13s`) all statistically significantly outperform 3-stage 0-hop WM (`1.9410 ± 0.0006`, `206.40` PPL, `27.82s`) by **`-0.0037` to `-0.0040` DEV BPB (`-2.06` to `-2.27` PPL, 95% CIs `[-0.0050, -0.0031]` and `[-0.0051, -0.0028]`, $p \le 0.0061$)**. Crucially, on **12-layer `d12`** ($L=12, D=384$), 3-stage `Oja-LP-MH-WM` already beats 3-stage 0-hop WM at `360` steps (`2.0326 ± 0.0009` vs. `2.0334 ± 0.0012`, paired diff **`-0.0008 ± 0.0003` BPB, 95% CI `[-0.0015, -0.0001]`, $p = 0.0379$**), and at `720` steps improves over `d12` 3-stage 0-hop WM (`1.9384 ± 0.0015`, `1.9037` VAL-B, `204.92` PPL, `51.09s`) by **`-0.0047 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (95% CI `[-0.0069, -0.0025]`, $p = 0.0121$)** to **`1.9337 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.8991` VAL-B, `202.31` PPL, `61.58s`)** under single-horizon `Oja-LP-WM` and by **`-0.0049 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB (95% CI `[-0.0073, -0.0025]`, $p = 0.0131$)** to **`1.9335 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.8989 ± 0.0003` VAL-B, `202.21` PPL, `68.11s`)** under multi-horizon `Oja-LP-MH-WM`—statistically significantly beating 6-layer `d6` 720-step `Oja-LP-WM` (`1.9371 ± 0.0007`) by **`-0.0036 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (95% CI `[-0.0058, -0.0013]`, $p = 0.0209$)** and offering a **$2.08\times$–$2.30\times$ faster 3-stage (`t-2`, zero $A_i^\top u_v$ gathers) quality–time tradeoff relative to 4-stage `d12` Dual 1-Hop WM (`1.9239 ± 0.0006` in `141.59s`, a `+0.0096` BPB quality gap)**!

---

## 2. Related Work and Methodological Distinctions

**Weight Maximization, Unbiased WM, Synthetic Gradients (`DNI`), and Node-Perturbation Methods (Dust).** Chung [1] introduced Weight Maximization (WM) for networks of self-interested reinforcement learning agents (*Learning by Competition of Self-Interested Reinforcement Learning Agents*, arXiv:2010.09770), proving that when each unit modulates its eligibility trace by the change in squared Euclidean norm of its outgoing weights, expected updates follow the gradient of downstream weight magnitude without backward error transport. Chung [2] subsequently formulated *Unbiased Weight Maximization* (UWM, arXiv:2307.13270) for Bernoulli-logistic units using an unbiased outgoing-weight product estimator. These external papers establish the original WM learning principle; they do not provide an inherited convergence guarantee for our multi-token perturbation-differential surrogate or diagonal skip routing. In earlier decoupled-learning work, Jaderberg et al. [14] introduced *Decoupled Neural Interfaces using Synthetic Gradients* (*ICML*, 2017, arXiv:1608.05343), where an auxiliary predictor $M_l(h_l)$ estimates the backpropagated loss gradient $\hat{\delta}_l \approx \nabla_{h_l}\mathcal{L}$ directly and is trained by regressing onto downstream synthetic-plus-1-step backpropagated gradients. Our block-local coordinate-transport bridges (`LP-WM` and `Oja-LP-WM`, **Proposition 11**) share with [14] the goal of unlocking asynchronous/short-horizon local credit assignment via a learned block-local auxiliary module, but differ fundamentally in target and training signal: rather than predicting gradients or regressing against backpropagated downstream derivatives, $(U_l, V_l)$ predicts the **readout-anchored forward activation displacement** $d_l^{\text{tgt}} = (\operatorname{sg}(\hat{h}_L) - \hat{h}_l) + 0.18\, w_y^{\text{norm}}$ (or pure forward state displacement $\operatorname{sg}(\hat{h}_{l+1}) - \hat{h}_l$ on next-block bridges $N_l$) purely from clean forward states and target embeddings, and transports the delayed Stage-2 terminal readout modulation across $(U_l, V_l)$ via a 2-call column-isolated local bridge pullback $V_l(U_l^\top e_l)$ with zero reverse error matrix multiplications through Transformer backbone weights. Recently, Dahal et al. [9] introduced *Dust* (*Pretraining Transformers Without Backpropagation*, QLabs, October 2026), which trains Transformer language models without backpropagation by injecting $K$ tokenwise Gaussian activation perturbation draws across separate layer passes, recomputing the affected network suffix from cached prefixes, rewarding direct layers via draw-centered token-loss reductions ($\gamma = 0$), and rewarding attention internals (`Section 2.3` of [9]) via separate per-role jittering (including attention gates) and per-head local attention-output alignment—scoring queries token-locally ($\gamma = 0$) because each query affects only its own causal row, and scoring keys/values by accumulating future query-token rewards ($\gamma = 0.98$, plus $49\text{k}$–$65\text{k}$ vocabulary-slab head draws and ES on residual scalars). We explicitly credit Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`) for separate attention-internal probes (including gates), per-head attention-output rewards, token-local query vs. future-token key/value credit, and averaging independent local estimates. Because our node-perturbation comparators and baseline experiments train the 4-matrix subset (`mlp.c_fc, mlp.c_proj, attn.c_v, attn.c_proj` plus `wte, value_embeds, lm_head`) at small per-step draw counts rather than replicating [9]'s 65k-head-slab, $K \in [64, 16384]$ suffix-recomputation protocol, **we do not claim head-to-head superiority over published Dust [9]**. Instead, we evaluate two author-defined node-perturbation comparators on our matched 4-matrix architecture: (i) **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, `exp37`–`exp39`)**, which shares the terminal-skip residual factors ($\gamma_l, \alpha_0$) of our Delayed WM pipeline but differs in credit timing (constructing $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}$ and immediately using it for same-step embedding and hidden-layer estimates rather than 3-stage delayed packets), perturbation location, and attention-credit treatment (scoring value perturbations locally with $\gamma = 0$ rather than via the forward attention-filtered trace); and (ii) **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, `exp42`)**, an author-defined suffix-recomputed recipe that uses Gaussian draw-mean centering, suffix recomputation from block $l+1$ onward, shared post-MLP block-output perturbations $\hat{g}_{\text{wr}}$ for both `attn.c_proj` and `mlp.c_proj` (which omits `attn.c_proj`'s path through the same block's MLP), an analytic local loss gradient on `lm_head`, and $\gamma = 0.98$ future-token accumulation on per-head attention-output alignment.

**Concurrent rStation Studies on Forward-Only and Delayed Transformer Credit Assignment, Groupwise Polar Orthogonalization (`Group Muon`), and Singular-Value Shrinkage Under Marchenko-Pastur Noise.** Four concurrent rStation papers and external work on head-grouped Muon optimization and random-matrix singular-value denoising inform our formulation:
- In `general_pretraining_18/v7`–`v10` [5] and `general_pretraining_21/v1`–`v3` [10], `cartan_transport` independently develops token micro-groups (stream partitioning), column-isolated WM rewards, the replacement of sibling-unit interference by within-group token interference, exact symmetrized unordered-pair conditional covariance formulas, and SRAM-fused outgoing-column contractions (`Delayed-Trace-Co-WM-S`) combined with $P=64$ and $P=256$ suffix forward probes for attention on ClimbMix `d6` (`2.04412 ± 0.00014` BPB at $P=64$ in $633.6\text{s}/635.2\text{s}$, `2.08637 ± 0.01282` at $P=256$, and `2.14471 ± 0.00464` at $1,000$ steps), 2-seed (`s42` and `s137`) 200-step recency-weighted value-pooling diagnostics (`2.14681` and `2.13220` BPB at $\gamma = 1.00$, `2.13081` at $\gamma = 0.98$), $P$-probe local attention credit assignment (`v8`–`v10`) with token-local query scoring ($\gamma = 0$), discounted future-token key/value accumulation ($\gamma = 0.98$), and upper-block ($l \ge L/2$) writer perturbation scaling (`wsig04`), and (in `general_pretraining_21/v1`–`v3` [10]) residual-highway input-bus pooling, the Diagonal-Attention DAG Theorem / Three-Level Causal Credit Hierarchy, shared cross-slab Rademacher-Hadamard value multiplexing, and block-strided value probing (`2.07883` and `2.06198` [+OE] 200-step BPB on `d6`, `2.03766` at $600$ steps, `2.06742` at $D=512$, and `2.01977` at $T=2,048$).
