In `general_pretraining_22/v1` (*Family-Checked Credit References for Backward-Free GPT Pretraining: Where the Gap Lies at Small Scale*) [11], `claude_2` constructs family-checked credit references (`f11ref` embedding-corrected own-token block-local reference vs. cross-position $K/V/\text{VE}$ references, and zero-hop vs. global writer references) on reduced-scale GPT models (`d4/w256` and `d6/w384` at `0.5B` tokens), showing that **adding** cross-position future-query credit to keys, values, and value embeddings improves the embedding-corrected `d4/w256` reference BPB from `1.5526` (`~1.553`, own-token) to `1.3338` (`~1.334`, a `0.219` BPB improvement: `0.101` from values/value embeddings and `0.118` from keys; removing cross-position credit reverses that direction), that replacing zero-hop writer credit with global writer credit while holding other families' own-token credit rules fixed improves BPB by `0.036` at `d4` (`1.5546 -> 1.5182`) and `0.030` at `d6/w384` (`1.4680 -> 1.4379`, vs. `0.395` [`1.9189 -> 1.5243`] when internals and embeddings are frozen at initialization), and (`Section 4.2–4.3` of [11], arm `F11-P2`) that while our Proposition 2 forward attention-filtered value trace matches its cross-position target to `0.98` of the noise ceiling (`split-half rho = 0.61`, raw concatenated cosine `0.446` vs. `-0.020` for own-token), raw estimator alignment does not automatically produce a training gain in `F11-P2` (`1.7850` vs. `1.7846` BPB, and `1.7766` vs. `1.7776` with keys frozen). Furthermore, in external work on backpropagation-trained Transformers, Zhang et al. (*When and Why Grouping Attention Heads Accelerates Muon Optimization*, `arXiv:2605.08933v1`, May 2026) [12] formalize **Group Muon**, partitioning multi-head attention gradients $G \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ into $H/g$ head-group blocks $G_i \in \mathbb{R}^{g d_h \times D}$, proving the nuclear-norm descent inequality $\sum_i \|G_i\|_* \ge \|G\|_*$ (`Proposition 1` of [12]) and the full-row-rank equal-Frobenius condition $\|P_{\text{grp}}(G)\|_F^2 = \sum_i m_i = m = \|P(G)\|_F^2$ (`Section 3` of [12]), analyzing the non-ideal approximate-whitening Frobenius norm cost $\frac{\beta\eta}{2}(\sum_i \|O_i\|_F^2 - \|O_{\text{full}}\|_F^2)$ (`Appendix D.2` of [12]), and showing under GPT-2 Small ($H=12$) backpropagation that finest splitting ($g=1$) helps early when gradients are near-full-rank (`Section 3` of [12]) but is overtaken later by intermediate grouping ($g=6$), while grouping $W_V$ (`Fixed-QK+V`) brings no consistent gain. Separately, in random-matrix denoising theory, Gavish and Donoho (*The Optimal Hard Threshold for Singular Values is $4/\sqrt{3}$*, `arXiv:1405.7511`, *IEEE Trans. Inf. Theory*, 2014; and *Optimal Shrinkage of Singular Values*, `arXiv:1305.5870`, *IEEE Trans. Inf. Theory*, 2017) [13] derive the asymptotic Frobenius-optimal singular-value hard threshold and piecewise optimal nonlinear shrinker $\eta^*(y) = \frac{1}{y}\sqrt{((y^2 - \beta - 1)^2 - 4\beta)_+}$ for $y > 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ and $\eta^*(y) = 0$ for $y \le 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ at rectangular aspect ratio $\beta = m/n \in (0, 1]$ under additive white noise calibrated from the empirical median singular value $\sigma_{\text{med}}/\sqrt{\mu_\beta}$. We explicitly credit [5], [10], and [11] for their respective micro-group, symmetrized pair-covariance, SRAM-fused outgoing-column contraction, local attention-probe, upper-block credit-sensitivity, Hadamard probe-multiplexing, and family-checked credit-reference foundations, [8] (`general_pretraining_12/v2`) for `Pre-Polar Muon`, Zhang et al. [12] for the groupwise/headwise polar construction, full-row-rank equal-Frobenius condition, nuclear-norm inequality $\sum_i \|G_i\|_* \ge \|G\|_*$, and approximate-whitening norm-cost framework, Gavish and Donoho [13] for the Marchenko-Pastur bulk edge $1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ and optimal singular-value shrinker $\eta^*(y)$, and Jaderberg et al. [14] for decoupled local credit modules. Our distinct contributions are: (i) reconciling the code-level $1/M_g$ normalization with 3-seed scale-normalized controls on both `d6` (`exp35`) and Native `50.3M` (`exp44`) and deriving the single-token cross-coordinate variance and closed-form leverage inversion $\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n,i}^2/D)^{-1}$ at non-separable `RMSNorm` interfaces (**Proposition 1(a–c)**, `exp58`, `exp60`); (ii) training `attn.c_v` via a **forward causal attention-filtered pre-synaptic trace** $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}$ (**Proposition 2**) without multi-probe value suffix sweeps; (iii) routing lexical credit across the diagonal skip surrogate $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$ (**Proposition 3**); (iv) isolating Stage-1 vs. Stage-2 post-installation drift, reward-side tangent projection variance, closed-form Wiener-MMSE key gathering, rotary-pair-aware Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ query/key probe multiplexing, sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ key gathering, adapting Zhang et al.'s [12] groupwise polar framework to backward-free perturbation credit across group sizes $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$, attention tiers ($W_Q, W_K$ vs. Tier-1 $W_V$), horizons (`360` vs. `720` steps), and credit engines, and formulating energy-matched linear spectral tapering (**Proposition 8**), un-equilibrated cubic Gram spectral tapering $G_3 = (G_n G_n^\top)G_n$, and Gavish-Donoho [13] singular-value shrinkage (**Proposition 9**) to suppress the $\sim 75\%$ Marchenko-Pastur perturbation-noise bulk on $(W_Q, W_K)$ without SVD overhead (**Propositions 4–9**, `exp32`–`exp35`, `exp42`, `exp49`, `exp59`–`exp76`); (v) evaluating 6-matrix decoupled $(W_Q, W_K)$ probing across widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, unconfounded $\eta_K=0.024$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ and exact-SVD controls, credit-engine depth-asymmetry and noise-free BP double-dissociation controls (`exp63`, `exp75`, `exp76`), Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` (`2.0337 ± 0.0004` at 360st and `1.9407 ± 0.0006` at 720st on `d6`, matching `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` `2.0337` / `1.9408` and `Gavish-Donoho` `2.0337` / `1.9401` at $3.4\times$ lower runtime and beating 720-step 4-Matrix Exact Global BP `1.9423`), 840-step Upper-Half $M=2$ `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` (`1.9337 ± 0.0015` in `22.90s`), and Native `50.3M` scale transfer (`1.8620 ± 0.0027` [`Exact-SVD Taper` $M=2$], `1.8583 ± 0.0030` [`Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` $M=2$, `186.53` PPL], and `1.8554 ± 0.0008` [`PrePolar-Blend` $M=4$, `184.97` PPL], `exp47`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp76`); (vi) breaking the 0-hop direct-skip compositional barrier (`Sub-Block-Skip BP` `1.8920` vs. `Global BP` `1.8530` on `d6`; `1.8840` vs. `1.8378` on 12-layer `d12`) via **Superposed 4-Stage Post-Install Dual 1-Hop (MLP + Attention-Value) Highway Weight Maximization** (**Proposition 10**, `exp77`, `57` 3-seed arms = `171` runs), which extends beyond the input-bus pooling in [10] to realize delayed 1-hop MLP and 1-hop Attention-Value branch transport with zero reverse error matmuls through Transformer backbone weights (separating Class I zero-attention-pullback 1-Hop MLP from Class II Dual 1-Hop with local cached $A_i^\top u_v$ gathers) in a fixed $O(1)=4$ stage buffer independent of depth $L$ and width $D$, reaching `2.0291 ± 0.0005` at 360st, `1.9247 ± 0.0008` (`47.02s`) / `1.9238 ± 0.0008` (`1.8888` VAL-B, `196.91` PPL, statistically significantly beating 720st 4-Mat Exact BP `1.9423` by `-0.0184 ± 0.0038`, 95% CI `[-0.0278, -0.0091]`) at 720st, `1.9014 ± 0.0009` (`185.12` PPL) at 840st, `2.0606, 2.0291, 2.0157, 2.0082` across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ (statistically significantly beating shared-LR 6-Mat Exact BP `2.0153` at $D=768$, 95% CI `[-0.0085, -0.0057]`), and `1.9239 ± 0.0006` in `141.59s` (vs. `1.9370` 0-hop, 95% CI `[-0.0149, -0.0112]`) on 12-layer `d12`; and (vii) relaxing the single-chart terminal representation assumption ($P_l = I_D$) inside a **strictly 3-stage (`t-2`, zero reverse error matmuls through Transformer backbone weights, zero $A_i^\top u_v$ attention pullbacks) pipeline** via **Low-Rank Predictive (`LP-WM`) and Oja-Stiefel Coordinate-Transport Bridges (`Oja-LP-WM`, Proposition 11, `exp78` & `exp79`, `27` 3-seed arms = `81` runs)**, showing that a 2-call column-isolated local bridge pullback (`compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(U_l, e_l, sigma * xi_z, G=2048)` followed by `compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(V_l.t(), u_z, sigma * xi_h, G=2048)`) eliminates the $(D - 1)\|V_l U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$ input-space (and $(r - 1)\|U_l^\top e_l\|_2^2$ latent-space) cross-column variance of 1-call scalar-contracted Rademacher bridge transport (`-0.0036` to `-0.0041` BPB across the two tested bridge recipes in `exp79`, $p \le 0.0016$), improving 720-step 3-stage `d6` WM from `1.9410 ± 0.0006` to `1.9370 ± 0.0004` (`LP-Multi-Horizon`, 95% CI `[-0.0050, -0.0031]`) and `1.9371 ± 0.0006` (`Oja-LP`, `32.78s`, 95% CI `[-0.0051, -0.0028]`), and improving 720-step 12-layer `d12` 3-stage WM from `1.9384 ± 0.0015` to `1.9337 ± 0.0007` (`61.58s`) and **`1.9335 ± 0.0006` (`1.8989` VAL-B, `202.21` PPL, `68.11s`, 95% CI `[-0.0073, -0.0025]`)—statistically significantly beating 6-layer `d6` 720-step `Oja-LP-WM` by `-0.0036 ± 0.0009` BPB (95% CI `[-0.0058, -0.0013]`, $p = 0.0209$) and providing a $2.08\times$–$2.30\times$ faster 3-stage quality–time tradeoff vs. 4-stage `d12` Dual 1-Hop WM (`1.9239 ± 0.0006` in `141.59s`, a `+0.0096` BPB gap)**.
- In `general_pretraining_19/v4`–`v6`, `residual_cartographer` [6] analyzes short residual writer/reader shortcuts, two-world delayed WM (`1.98964` DEV BPB at $499.65\text{M}$ targets, `2.00286` at $134.2\text{M}$ targets on Native `50.3M` in `v4`), sequence-wide incoming-weight actions with previous-origin activity directions (`2.1184/2.0975` DEV BPB at $134.2\text{M}$ targets on Native `50.3M` in `v5`), and four-world role coding vs. two-world action persistence across depths 8 and 12 (`v6`). Because [5], [6], [9], [10], [11], [12], [13], and [14] use different token budgets, model scales, and schedules than our studies, raw BPB endpoints across [5], [6], [9], [10], [11], [12], and [14] are not direct head-to-head comparisons; rather, our work complements [6] and [11] by commuting $W_v^{(h)}$ with causal attention during the forward pass (**Proposition 2**), characterizing post-installation drift (**Proposition 4**), and testing `residual_cartographer`'s realized-component reader hypothesis in `exp36` Part B.

---

## 3. Mathematical Formulation and Estimator Analysis

### 3.1 Three-Stage Residual Pipeline

Consider an $L$-layer causal Transformer with width $D$, vocabulary size $V$, token embedding $e_{\text{tok}}(x) = W_{\text{wte}}[x]$, initial normalized state $x_0 = \text{RMSNorm}(e_{\text{tok}}(x))$, per-layer residual recurrence $x_{\text{in}}^{(l)} = \lambda_{\text{res}}^{(l)} x_{\text{out}}^{(l-1)} + \lambda_{x_0}^{(l)} x_0$, attention and MLP sublayers writing additively via $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ and $W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times 4D}$, terminal state $x_{\text{final}} = \text{RMSNorm}(x_L)$, and softcapped readout logits $z = 15 \tanh(W_{\text{lm}} x_{\text{final}} / 15)$ with $W_{\text{lm}} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$. Unrolling the linear residual scalings $\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(l)}, \lambda_{x_0}^{(l)}$ yields the exact terminal representation
$$
x_L = \alpha_0 x_0 + \sum_{l=0}^{L-1} \gamma_l \left(W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)} y_{\text{attn}}^{(l)} + W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)} h_{\text{mlp}}^{(l)}\right), \qquad \gamma_l = \prod_{m=l+1}^{L-1} \lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)}, \quad \alpha_0 = \prod_{m=0}^{L-1} \lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)} + \sum_{l=0}^{L-1} \lambda_{x_0}^{(l)} \gamma_l.
$$
Exploiting these direct additive write paths into $x_L$, we partition all trainable parameters into three causal stages with maximum pipeline delay $2$ independent of depth $L$:
- **Stage 3 (`Delay = 0`, Terminal Readout):** $W_{\text{lm}} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$.
- **Stage 2 (`Delay = 1`, Residual Stream Writers):** $W_{\text{wte}} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$, $W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times 4D}$, and $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ for $l \in \{0, \dots, L-1\}$, whose outgoing receiver columns are $W_{\text{lm}}[:, i] \in \mathbb{R}^V$.
- **Stage 1 (`Delay = 2`, Internal Sublayer Writers):** $W_{\text{mlp\_fc}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{4D \times D}$, $W_{v}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$, $W_{\text{ve}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$, and (in our 5-matrix and 6-matrix configurations, `exp45`–`exp48`) $W_{q}^{(l)}, W_{k}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$, whose outgoing receiver columns are $W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)}[:, p] \in \mathbb{R}^D$ and $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)}[:, p] \in \mathbb{R}^D$.

### 3.2 Reconciled Code Contraction, Separable Pair Covariance, and `RMSNorm` Interface Moments (`Proposition 1`)

Let $N = BT$ denote the total number of tokens in a batch, and partition $\{1, \dots, N\} = \bigsqcup_{g=1}^G \mathcal{S}_g$ into $G$ disjoint streams of size $M_g = N / G$ tokens each. During the forward pass, unit $i$ at stage $m$ injects i.i.d. Rademacher exploration noise $\xi_{n, i} \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\})$ with scale $\sigma > 0$, and records the stream eligibility trace $Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)} = \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} (\xi_{n, i}/\sigma) x_n \in \mathbb{R}^{D_{\text{in}}}$.

In `compute_stream_wm_rewards_chunked` (`exp26`) and `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` (`exp32`–`exp35`), the stream reward divides by $M_g$ with an optional multiplier `scale_factor` (`mult = scale_factor / M_g`):
$$
R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n' \in \mathcal{S}_g} u_{n'}^{(m+1), T} W_{:, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) \, \Delta y_{n', i}^{(m)},
$$
and the subsequent parameter update divides the tokenwise modulation–input product $\sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)} Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)}$ by $N = BT$ (`-(mod_flat.t() @ x_flat) / BT`). Ignoring the descent sign, the **implemented contraction in code** is therefore
$$
\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; \text{scale\_factor}) := \frac{1}{N} \sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) \, Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)} = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G), \qquad \text{where} \qquad \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G) := \frac{1}{G} \sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)}(1) \, Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)}.
$$
In particular, the default setting `scale_factor = 1` (`exp26`–`exp34`) computes $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; 1) = \frac{1}{M_g}\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G)$, whereas setting `scale_factor = M_g` (`exp35`) computes $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; M_g) = \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G)$, and at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$) both coincide identically.

Furthermore, we must distinguish **separable channels**—where coordinate $i$'s forward displacement depends only on $\xi_{n, i}$, as holds at **Stage 1** (`mlp.c_fc` with elementwise $\text{ReLU}^2$ and `attn.c_v` with linear head-value projection)—from **non-separable `RMSNorm` interfaces** at **Stage 2** (`x_final = RMSNorm(x_L)` and `x_0 = RMSNorm(e_tok)`), where $\text{rms}(z_n) = \sqrt{\frac{1}{D}\sum_{i'=1}^D z_{n, i'}^2}$ couples coordinate $i$ to $\xi_{n, i'}$ for $i' \neq i$.

**Proposition 1 (Conditional Moments of $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; \text{scale\_factor})$ for Separable Channels and `RMSNorm` Interfaces).** *Conditioned on fixed pre-synaptic inputs $\{x_n\}_{n=1}^N \subset \mathbb{R}^{D_{\text{in}}}$ and downstream weights/modulations independent of the i.i.d. Rademacher draws $\xi_{n, i} \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\})$:*
1. **(a) Separable-Channel Conditional Mean (Stage 1: `mlp.c_fc, attn.c_v`):** *Suppose $\Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} = \sigma c_{n, i} \xi_{n, i} + r_{n, i}(\sigma)$ with $c_{n, i}$ independent of $\xi_n$ and $r_{n, i}(\sigma) = O(\sigma^2)$, and let $a_{n, i} := c_{n, i} \sum_{j=1}^{d_{\text{next}}} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) u_{n, j}^{(m+1)}$. Then the first-order code estimator $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})$ satisfies for every $G \in \{1, \dots, N\}$ ($M_g = N / G$):*
   $$
   \mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})\right] = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \left(\frac{1}{N} \sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n\right).
   $$
   *Specifically, under `scale_factor = 1`, the conditional mean scales as $\frac{1}{M_g}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n\right)$ (evaluating to $0.5$ on $N=4, G=2, M_g=2, a_n = x_n = 1$), whereas under `scale_factor = M_g`, the conditional mean is $\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}, (1)}(G)] = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n$ for all $G \in \{1, \dots, N\}$ (evaluating to $1.0$ on $N=4, G=2, M_g=2, a_n = x_n = 1$).*
2. **(b) Separable-Channel Symmetrized Pair Covariance ([5], Section 2.3):** *Under the separable channel model of (a), the exact conditional covariance matrix of $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})$ is*
   $$
   \text{Cov}_{\xi}\left(\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})\right) = \left(\frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g}\right)^2 \frac{1}{N^2} \sum_{g=1}^G \sum_{\substack{n, n' \in \mathcal{S}_g \\ n < n'}} \left(a_{n', i} x_n + a_{n, i} x_{n'}\right)\left(a_{n', i} x_n + a_{n, i} x_{n'}\right)^T,
   $$
   *which sums over the $G\binom{M_g}{2} = \frac{N(M_g - 1)}{2}$ unordered within-stream token pairs and vanishes identically at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$).*
3. **(c) Non-Separable `RMSNorm` Interface Moments at $M_g = 1$ (Stage 2: `x_final, x_0`):** *At an `RMSNorm` interface $x_n = \text{RMSNorm}(z_n) = z_n / \text{rms}(z_n)$ ($z_n \in \mathbb{R}^D$), tangent-projected or raw Rademacher perturbation yields the first-order forward displacement $\Delta x_n^{(1)} = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} \xi_n$, whose $i$-th coordinate is $\Delta x_{n, i}^{(1)} = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\left((1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)\xi_{n, i} - \frac{x_{n, i}}{D}\sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'}\xi_{n, i'}\right)$. Consequently, even at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$, where cross-token pairs $n < n'$ are absent), for a single token $n$ with receiver sensitivity $a_{n, i} = \sum_j W_{j, i}^{(m+1)} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)}$ and pre-synaptic input $h_n \in \mathbb{R}^{D_{\text{in}}}$, the first-order unprojected coordinate-$i$ estimator is*
   $$
   \hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)} := a_{n, i} \frac{\Delta x_{n, i}^{(1)} \xi_{n, i}}{\sigma} h_n = \frac{a_{n, i}}{\text{rms}(z_n)} \left(1 - \frac{x_{n, i}^2}{D} - \frac{x_{n, i}}{D} \sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'} \xi_{n, i} \xi_{n, i'}\right) h_n,
   $$
   *which has exact conditional mean and nonzero single-token **cross-coordinate** covariance*
   $$
   \mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}\left[\hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)}\right] = \frac{a_{n, i}}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\left(1 - \frac{x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right) h_n, \qquad \text{Cov}_{\xi_n}\left(\hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)}\right) = \frac{a_{n, i}^2 x_{n, i}^2 (D - x_{n, i}^2)}{D^2 \, \text{rms}(z_n)^2} h_n h_n^T.
   $$
   *For example, for a single token ($N=G=M_g=1$) at $D=2$ with $z_1 = (1, 2)$, $a_{1, 1} = 1$, and $h_1 = 1$, we have $\text{rms}(z_1) = \sqrt{2.5}$, $x_{1, 1}^2 = 0.4$, $x_{1, 1} x_{1, 2} = 0.8$, and $\hat{g}_{1, 1}^{(1)} = \frac{0.8 - 0.4 \xi_{1, 1}\xi_{1, 2}}{\sqrt{2.5}}$, with mean $0.8/\sqrt{2.5} \approx 0.5060$ and variance $\frac{0.4(2 - 0.4)}{4 \times 2.5} = 0.064$. Moreover, applying reward-side radial projection $P_{x_n} = I_D - x_n x_n^\top / D$ to the vector of coordinatewise WM modulations $\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)} := (\Delta x_n^{(1)} \odot \xi_n \odot a_n)/\sigma$ yields the first-order modulation conditional expectation $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D) a_n$ and outer-product matrix expectation $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{g}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] = \mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] h_n^\top$, which generally differ from the exact terminal-skip `RMSNorm` modulation $\mu_{\text{skip}, n} = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} a_n$ and matrix pullback $g_{\text{skip}, n} = \mu_{\text{skip}, n} h_n^\top$: the modulation-vector discrepancy satisfies $\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] - \mu_{\text{skip}, n}\right\|_2 = \left\|P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(x_{n, i}^2/D)a_n\right\|_2 / \text{rms}(z_n) \le \left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right) \frac{\|a_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$ and the matrix Frobenius discrepancy satisfies $\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{g}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] - g_{\text{skip}, n}\right\|_F \le \left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right) \frac{\|a_n\|_2 \|h_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$ (where the normalized discrepancy coefficient $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 / D$ is $O(1/D)$ only under a coordinate-concentration assumption $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 = O(1)$, while the absolute discrepancy also depends on the scale factors $\|a_n\|_2, \|h_n\|_2, \text{rms}(z_n)$): for instance, at $D=3$ with unit-RMS $x = (\sqrt{3/2}, \sqrt{3/2}, 0)^\top$, $a = (1, 0, 1)^\top$, and $h = 1$, exact enumeration over all $2^3 = 8$ Rademacher patterns gives $\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[P_x \hat{g}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}] = (1/4, -1/4, 1)^\top \neq g_{\text{skip}} = (1/2, -1/2, 1)^\top$ ($\cos = \frac{5}{3\sqrt{3}} \approx 0.962250$, `exp58`).*

*Proof.* For (a–b), expanding $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}, (1)}(G) = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^G (\sum_{n' \in \mathcal{S}_g} a_{n', i}\xi_{n', i})(\sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} \xi_{n, i} x_n)$ using $\xi_{n, i}^2 \equiv 1$ gives $\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n + \frac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^G \sum_{n < n' \in \mathcal{S}_g} \eta_{\{n, n'\}, i} (a_{n', i} x_n + a_{n, i} x_{n'})$ where $\eta_{\{n, n'\}, i} = \xi_{n, i}\xi_{n', i}$ are pairwise uncorrelated with zero mean and unit variance; multiplying by $\text{scale\_factor} / M_g$ scales the mean by $\text{scale\_factor} / M_g$ and the covariance by $(\text{scale\_factor} / M_g)^2$. For (c), since $\xi_{n, i}^2 = 1$, multiplying $(P_{x_n}\xi_n)_i = (1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)\xi_{n, i} - \frac{x_{n, i}}{D}\sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'}\xi_{n, i'}$ by $\xi_{n, i}$ yields the displayed expression for $\hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)}$. Because $\{\xi_{n, i}\xi_{n, i'}\}_{i' \neq i}$ are $D - 1$ pairwise uncorrelated zero-mean Rademacher products with unit variance and $\sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'}^2 = \|x_n\|_2^2 - x_{n, i}^2 = D - x_{n, i}^2$, the mean and covariance follow immediately, and multiplying the stacked coordinate expectations $\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)a_n$ by $P_{x_n}$ gives $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{g}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}]$. $\square$

### 3.3 Forward Causal Attention-Filtered Value Eligibility Trace (`Proposition 2`)

In causal multi-head self-attention with $H$ heads of dimension $D_h = D / H$, head $h$'s output at batch $b$, query token $\tau \in \{0, \dots, T-1\}$ is $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} v^{(h)}(b, s)$, where $v^{(h)}(b, s) = W_v^{(h)} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)$ ($W_v^{(h)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D_h \times D}$) and $A_{b, h, \tau, s}$ is the causal attention weight ($\sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} = 1$, $A_{b, h, \tau, s} = 0$ for $s > \tau$).

**Proposition 2 (Exact Commutation of $W_v^{(h)}$ with Causal Attention).** *Because $W_v^{(h)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D_h \times D}$ is shared across sequence positions $s \in \{0, \dots, T-1\}$, the post-attention value aggregation at position $\tau$ satisfies the exact linear identity*
$$
y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} \left(W_v^{(h)} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)\right) = W_v^{(h)} \underbrace{\left(\sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)\right)}_{\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) \in \mathbb{R}^D}.
$$
*Consequently, injecting exploration noise $\psi_v^{(h)}(b, \tau) \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^{D_h})$ at the post-attention value output $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ and recording the **forward causal attention-filtered pre-synaptic trace** $Z_{v}^{(h, g)}(t-2) = \sum_{(b, \tau) \in \mathcal{S}_g} (\psi_v^{(h)}(b, \tau)/\sigma_1) \, \bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)^T$ yields an exact single-position linear channel $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = W_v^{(h)} \bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ at each output token $\tau$, eliminating causal cross-position perturbation mixing with zero reverse-time pooling.*

### 3.4 Diagonal Identity-Skip Surrogate & Post-Installation Credit Decomposition (`Propositions 3 & 4`)

**Proposition 3 (Algebraic Diagonal Skip-Routing Surrogate for `wte`).** *In the unrolled residual equation $x_L = \alpha_0 x_0 + \dots$, the direct path from $x_0 = \text{RMSNorm}(W_{\text{wte}}[x])$ to $x_L$ is the fixed diagonal scaling $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$. Treating $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$ as an algebraic surrogate receiver matrix with post-synaptic modulation $s_{\text{bus}}(n) = \text{mod\_bus\_base}(n) \in \mathbb{R}^D$ and pre-synaptic forward displacement $\Delta x_0(n) = \text{RMSNorm}(e_{\text{tok}}(n) + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{\text{wte}}(n)) - x_0(n)$, the column-norm contraction reduces to the $O(BT \cdot D)$ elementwise contraction*
$$
R_{\text{wte}, i}^{\text{raw}, (g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) = \sum_{j=1}^D (\alpha_0 \delta_{j, i}) \left(\frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g}\sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} s_{\text{bus}, n, j} \, \Delta x_{0, n, i}\right) = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} \left(\alpha_0 s_{\text{bus}, n, i}\right) \Delta x_{0, n, i},
$$
*(followed in `compute_identity_skip_wm_rewards_fast` by stream-mean centering $R_{\text{wte}, i}^{(g)} = R_{\text{wte}, i}^{\text{raw}, (g)} - \frac{1}{G}\sum_{g'=1}^G R_{\text{wte}, i}^{\text{raw}, (g')}$), eliminating the second $O(G V D)$ `lm_head` contraction.*

Finally, we analyze **Strict Post-Installation Construction** (`Stage 2: (t-1, t [post-Stage3-step], t)`), where Stage 2 evaluates `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` at clock $t$ on $W_{\text{lm}}(t) = W_{\text{lm}}(t-1) + \delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ after Stage 3 has installed its batch-$t-1$ update. In our implementation (`custom_entrywise_adamw_step` and `row_isotropic_adamw_step`), at optimizer step $t_{\text{step}}$, the installed Stage-3 displacement is
$$
\delta W_{\text{lm}, j, i}(t-1) = -\eta_3 \lambda_{\text{wd}} W_{\text{lm}, j, i}(t-1) - \frac{\eta_3}{1 - \beta_1^{t_{\text{step}}}} \cdot d_{j, i} \left(\beta_1 m_{j, i}^{(t-2)} + (1 - \beta_1) g_{j, i}^{(t-1)}\right), \qquad d_{j, i} := \frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}} + \epsilon},
$$
where $g_{j, i}^{(t-1)} = -\frac{1}{BT}\sum_{n'=1}^{BT} s_{\text{lm}, n', j} x_{\text{final}, n', i}$, $\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)} = v_{j, i}^{(t-1)} / (1 - \beta_2^{t_{\text{step}}})$ under `entrywise` moments (or row-averaged $d_{j, i} \equiv d_j = (\sqrt{\hat{v}_j^{(t-1)}} + \epsilon)^{-1}$ with $\hat{v}_j^{(t-1)} = \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D \hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}$ under `row_iso`), and $\epsilon > 0$ is added *outside* the square root (`denom = sqrt(v_hat) + eps`). At $G = BT$ ($M_g = 1$), let $u_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{pre}}(n) = R_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{pre}}(n) (\xi_{n, i}/\sigma)$ and $u_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{post}}(n) = R_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{post}}(n) (\xi_{n, i}/\sigma)$ denote the unprojected Stage-2 credit before and after installing $\delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$.

**Proposition 4 (Decomposition of Installed-Update Terminal Credit Shift, Data-Dependent Fourth-Moment Bound, and Preconditioning Regimes).** *Under Strict Post-Installation Construction at $G = BT$ ($M_g = 1$), the post-minus-pre unprojected credit shift $\delta u_{\text{bus}}(n) := u_{\text{bus}}^{\text{post}}(n) - u_{\text{bus}}^{\text{pre}}(n)$ decomposes into three additive components corresponding to decoupled weight decay, first-moment history $m^{(t-2)}$, and current-batch gradient $g^{(t-1)}$:*
$$
\delta u_{\text{bus}, i}(n) = -\eta_3 \lambda_{\text{wd}} u_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{pre}}(n) + \delta u_{\text{mom}, i}(n) + \delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n' = n)}(n) + \delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n' \neq n)}(n),
$$
*where:*
1. **Same-Token ($n' = n$) `RMSNorm` Radial-vs-Oblique Split and Data-Dependent Fourth-Moment Bound:** *With tangent-projected forward carrier $\tilde{\xi}_n = P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} \xi_n$ and raw carrier multiplication $\xi_n / \sigma$, the first-order same-token ($n'=n$) term is $\delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n'=n, 1)}(n) = \frac{\eta_3 (1-\beta_1)}{(1-\beta_1^{t_{\text{step}}}) BT} c_{n, i} \, b_i(n)$, where $c_{n, i} := \sum_{j=1}^V d_{j, i} s_{\text{lm}, n, j}^2$ and the vector $b(n)$ splits into an $O(1)$ radial vector $b_{\text{rad}}(n) \parallel x_{\text{final}}(n)$ and an oblique `RMSNorm`-Jacobian coupling term $b_{\text{oblique}}(n)$:*
   $$
   b(n) := \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\left(x_{\text{final}}(n) \odot \xi_n \odot (P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} \xi_n)\right) = \underbrace{\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} x_{\text{final}}(n)}_{b_{\text{rad}}(n) \parallel x_{\text{final}}(n)} - \underbrace{\frac{\langle \xi_n, x_{\text{final}}(n)\rangle}{D \, \text{rms}(z_n)} \left(x_{\text{final}}(n)^{\odot 2} \odot \xi_n\right)}_{b_{\text{oblique}}(n)}.
   $$
   *Under **row-isotropic preconditioning** ($d_{j, i} \equiv d_j$, so $c_{n, i} \equiv c_n$ is a scalar), reward-side projection $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)}$ annihilates $c_n b_{\text{rad}}(n)$ identically ($P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b_{\text{rad}}(n) = 0$) while leaving $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b_{\text{oblique}}(n) \neq 0$ (e.g., at $D=2, z_n=(1, 2), \xi_n=(1, 1)$, $b(n)=(0.16, -0.16)$, $b_{\text{rad}}(n)=(0.4, 0.8)$, and $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b(n) = (0.192, -0.096)$). Defining the normalized coordinate fourth moment $\kappa_4(x) := \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D x_i^4 \in [1, D]$, the expected squared norm of $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b(n)$ relative to $\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2 = D / \text{rms}(z_n)^2$ satisfies the exact **data-dependent fourth-moment bound***
   $$
   \frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}\left[\|P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b(n)\|_2^2\right]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2} \le \frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}\left[\|b_{\text{oblique}}(n)\|_2^2\right]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2} = \frac{\sum_{i=1}^D x_{\text{final}, n, i}^4}{D^2} = \frac{\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}(n))}{D}.
   $$
   *In particular: (i) if $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}(n)) \le C = O(1)$ is uniformly bounded, the residual ratio is $\le C/D = O(1/D)$ (on trained ClimbMix `d6` states at $D=384$, we measure token-mean $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}) = 2.9398$, giving bound $\kappa_4/D = 0.007656$ and empirical ratio $0.007458$); (ii) without a fourth-moment bound, $\|x\|_2^2 = D$ alone does **not** imply a vanishing residual—for the 2-sparse family $x = \sqrt{D/5}(1, 2, 0, \dots, 0)$ ($z = x$), $\kappa_4(x)/D = 0.68$ and $\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\|P_x b\|_2^2]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}\|_2^2} = 0.0576$ for every $D \ge 2$; and (iii) under **entrywise preconditioning**, coordinate variation in $c_{n, i} = \sum_j d_{j, i} s_{\text{lm}, n, j}^2$ breaks exact radial collinearity ($P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)}(c_n \odot b_{\text{rad}}(n)) \neq 0$) unless $\epsilon \gg \sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}}$, so increasing $\epsilon: 10^{-8} \to 10^{-4}$ is required to damp entrywise coordinate spread.*
2. **Cross-Token ($n' \neq n$) Current-Gradient Drift Bound:** *Since $d_{j, i} = (\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}} + \epsilon)^{-1} \le \epsilon^{-1}$, the cross-token ($n' \neq n$) current-gradient contribution satisfies*
   $$
   \left|\delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n' \neq n)}(n)\right| \le \frac{\eta_3 (1 - \beta_1)}{(1 - \beta_1^{t_{\text{step}}}) BT \, \epsilon} \sum_{n' \neq n} \left(\sum_{j=1}^V |s_{\text{lm}, n, j}| \, |s_{\text{lm}, n', j}|\right) |x_{\text{final}, n', i}| \, \left|\frac{\Delta x_{\text{final}, n, i}}{\sigma}\right|.
   $$
3. **Proposition 5 (First-Order Radial Invariance in Forward Normalization vs. Exact Fourth-Moment Radial Variance in Unprojected Reward Reconstruction):** *Let $u \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ be a query or key head vector normalized to $\text{rms}(u) = 1$ ($\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$), let $P_u := I_{d_h} - \frac{1}{d_h} u u^\top$ be its orthogonal tangent projector ($P_u^2 = P_u, \|u\|_2^2 = d_h$), and let $g \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ be a deterministic local tangent sensitivity satisfying $\langle g, u \rangle = 0$, all expressed in the coordinate frame where $\xi \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ has independent Rademacher signs (specifically, when Rademacher signs $\xi_{\text{pre}} \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ are drawn in the pre-rotary basis before an orthogonal rotary transformation $R_\theta \in O(d_h)$, since $R_\theta$ preserves inner products, Euclidean norms, $P_{R_\theta u_{\text{pre}}} = R_\theta P_{u_{\text{pre}}} R_\theta^\top$, and covariance traces, the fourth-moment expression below holds with $(u, g) = (u_{\text{pre}}, g_{\text{pre}}) = (R_\theta^\top u_{\text{post}}, R_\theta^\top g_{\text{post}})$ in the pre-rotary coordinate frame). Forward `RMSNorm` removes the radial component of $\xi$ to first order ($\text{norm}(u + \sigma \xi) - u = \sigma P_u \xi + O(\sigma^2)$), so the first-order scalar reward $R^{(1)} = \sigma \langle g, P_u \xi \rangle = \sigma \langle g, \xi \rangle$ is identical whether $\xi$ is tangent-projected in the forward pass or not. However, reconstructing the post-synaptic modulation vector with the **unprojected** perturbation $\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) := (R^{(1)}/\sigma)\xi = \langle g, \xi \rangle \xi$ vs. the **reward-side tangent-projected** vector $\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}(\xi) := P_u \hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) = \langle g, \xi \rangle P_u \xi$ yields identical expectations $\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}] = \mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}] = g$ and exact total-variance reduction*
   $$
   \Delta(u, g) := \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}\right) - \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}\right) = \mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|(I_{d_h} - P_u)\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi)\right\|_2^2\right] = \frac{1}{d_h}\mathbb{E}\!\left[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2\right] = \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{1 \le i < j \le d_h} (u_i g_j + u_j g_i)^2 = \|g\|_2^2 - \frac{2}{d_h}\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2 \ge 0.
   $$
   *Proof:* Since $I_{d_h} - P_u = \frac{1}{d_h} u u^\top$ with $\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$ and $\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}] = \mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}] = g$, Pythagorean decomposition gives $\Delta(u, g) = \mathbb{E}[\|\frac{1}{d_h}(u^\top \hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi)) u\|_2^2] = \frac{1}{d_h}\mathbb{E}[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2]$. Expanding $(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2 = \sum_{i,j,k,l} g_i g_j u_k u_l \xi_i \xi_j \xi_k \xi_l$ and taking expectations over independent Rademacher signs ($\mathbb{E}[\xi_i^4] = 1$, $\mathbb{E}[\xi_i^2 \xi_j^2] = 1$ for $i \neq j$, and $0$ otherwise) yields $\mathbb{E}[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2] = \sum_i g_i^2 u_i^2 + \sum_{i \neq j} g_i^2 u_j^2 + 2\sum_{i \neq j} g_i g_j u_i u_j = \|g\|_2^2 \|u\|_2^2 + 2(g^\top u)^2 - 2\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2$. Substituting $\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$ and $g^\top u = 0$ gives $\Delta(u, g) = \|g\|_2^2 - \frac{2}{d_h}\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2 = \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{1 \le i < j \le d_h}(u_i g_j + u_j g_i)^2 \ge 0$. $\square$
   *In particular: (i) for $d_h = 2$, $u = (\sqrt{2}, 0)^\top$ ($\|u\|_2^2 = 2 = d_h$), and $g = (0, 1)^\top$, $\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}(\xi) \equiv (0, 1)^\top$ has **zero variance** ($\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}) = 0$) whereas $\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) = (\xi_1 \xi_2, 1)^\top$ has radial variance $\Delta(u, g) = 1$ (and similarly for $d_h = 4, u = (2, 0, 0, 0)^\top, g = (0, 1, 0, 0)^\top$, where $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}) = 3$, $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}) = 2$, and $\Delta(u, g) = 1$); (ii) at the boundary state $d_h = 2, u = (1, 1)^\top, g = (1, -1)^\top$, $u_1 g_2 + u_2 g_1 = 0$ so every reconstructed draw is already tangent and $\Delta(u, g) = 0$; and (iii) for dense unit-RMS vectors with $u_i^2 \approx 1$, $\Delta(u, g) \approx (1 - \frac{2}{d_h})\|g\|_2^2$ out of $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}) = (d_h - 1)\|g\|_2^2$, a relative radial noise fraction of $\frac{d_h - 2}{d_h(d_h - 1)} \approx \frac{1}{d_h}$. On initialized `d6` Layer 0 ($d_h = 64$, `exp49` Part 1), this radial component accounts for $1.53\%$ of single-draw modulation energy (`0.1438 -> 0.1447` $K=1$ weight cosine; `0.7340 -> 0.7365` $K=64$ cosine). Because $1/d_h \approx 1.56\%$ is a small fraction of total modulation energy, end-to-end 360-step training losses with and without reward-side tangent projection are empirically very close (`2.0598 ± 0.0005` vs. `2.0595 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB at $M=2$ in `exp49`, and `2.0649 ± 0.0006` vs. `2.0649 ± 0.0006` in `exp45`).*
4. **Proposition 6 (Closed-Form Parameter-Free Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather Filter vs. Discounted and Attention-Weighted Gathering):** *For a single causal attention head with attention weights $A_{\tau, s}$ ($\tau \ge s, \sum_{s' \le \tau} A_{\tau, s'} = 1$), perturbing keys $k_{s'} \mapsto k_{s'} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}$ with independent tangent Rademacher probes $\tilde{\xi}_{k, s'} = P_{k_{s'}} \xi_{k, s'}$ produces the first-order scalar query-token reward $R_{\text{head}, \tau}^{(1)} = \sigma \sum_{s' \le \tau} A_{\tau, s'} \langle c_{\tau, s'}, \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}\rangle$, where $c_{\tau, s'} := \langle g_\tau, v_{s'} - y_\tau\rangle \frac{1}{\sqrt{d_h}} P_{k_{s'}} q_\tau \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ so that the exact local key sensitivity is $g_{k, s} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau = \sum_{\tau \ge s} A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}$. For any causal query-to-key gather matrix $W_{\tau, s}$ ($\tau \ge s$) and $M \ge 1$ independent probe draws averaged at key $s$, the linear estimator $\widehat{g}_{k, s}^{(W, M)} := \frac{1}{M}\sum_{r=1}^M \left(\sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} \frac{R_{\text{head}, \tau}^{(1, r)}}{\sigma}\right)\tilde{\xi}_{k, s}^{(r)}$ has conditional mean*
   $$
   \mathbb{E}_{\xi}\!\left[\widehat{g}_{k, s}^{(W, M)}\right] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}.
   $$
   *Consequently: (i) unweighted causal gathering ($W_{\tau, s} \equiv 1$) is conditionally unbiased ($W_{\tau, s} A_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$) but accumulates cross-key noise $\sum_{s' \le \tau, s' \neq s} A_{\tau, s'}^2 \|c_{\tau, s'}\|_2^2$ from queries $\tau$ that do not attend to $s$ ($A_{\tau, s} \approx 0$); (ii) naive attention-weighted (`Rake`) gathering ($W_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$) suppresses non-attending queries but weights each future query by $A_{\tau, s}^2$ in expectation, introducing a squaring attenuation bias $(1 - A_{\tau, s}) A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}$ on moderate links; and (iii) under an isotropic prior $\mathbb{E}[\|c_{\tau, s'}\|_2^2] = \sigma_c^2$ ($\langle c_{\tau, s'}, c_{\tau, s''}\rangle = 0$ for $s' \neq s''$), the per-pair cross-key interference objective $\operatorname{MSE}_{\tau, s}^{\text{cross}}(W_{\tau, s}; M) := \sigma_c^2 \left[(W_{\tau, s} - 1)^2 A_{\tau, s}^2 + \frac{W_{\tau, s}^2}{M}\sum_{s' \le \tau, s' \neq s} A_{\tau, s'}^2\right] = \sigma_c^2 \left[(W_{\tau, s} - 1)^2 A_{\tau, s}^2 + \frac{W_{\tau, s}^2}{M}\left(\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 - A_{\tau, s}^2\right)\right]$ (which models cross-key superposition noise from $s' \neq s$; including the within-key $s'=s$ off-diagonal Rademacher coordinate variance $\frac{d_h-1}{d_h} A_{\tau, s}^2$ replaces $\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 - A_{\tau, s}^2$ by $\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 - \frac{1}{d_h} A_{\tau, s}^2$) has unique minimizer $\partial \operatorname{MSE}_{\tau, s}^{\text{cross}} / \partial W_{\tau, s} = 0$ given by the parameter-free **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter***
   $$
   W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{M A_{\tau, s}^2 + \sum_{s' \le \tau, s' \neq s} A_{\tau, s'}^2} = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1) A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \in [0, 1] \qquad \left(\text{or } \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1/d_h) A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \text{ with within-key coordinate variance}\right).
   $$
   *In particular, $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = 1$ whenever key $s$ carries all of query $\tau$'s attention mass ($A_{\tau, s} = 1 \implies \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 = 1$, eliminating the $A_{\tau, s}^2$ squaring bias of `Rake`), whereas $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) \approx \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \to 0$ whenever query $\tau$ attends elsewhere ($A_{\tau, s} \ll \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2$), suppressing cross-key interference without a tuned discount factor $\gamma_K$. Moreover, for any deterministic clean-attention gather weight $W_{\tau, s}$, the conditional first-order expected key update at source position $s$ is the $W_{\tau, s}$-weighted Jacobian sum $\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\hat{u}_{k, s}^{(W)} \mid \text{state}, a] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} J_{\tau, s}^\top a_\tau + O(\sigma^2)$ (verified to $< 1.1 \times 10^{-11}$ max error by exhaustive $2^{3 \times 2} = 64$-pattern Rademacher enumeration on a 3-token, $d_h=2$ causal attention block in `exp68` Part 1B and $4,096$-pattern enumeration on a 3-token, $d_h=4$ block under 4-probe Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ multiplexing in `exp71` Part 1: `token_diagonal` $W_{\tau,s}=\mathbf{1}_{\tau=s}$ degrades expected target alignment to $\cos = 0.341593$ [$d_h=2$] and $0.273828$ [$d_h=4$], whereas `wiener_mmse_m4` retains high expected alignment $\cos = 0.857897$ [$d_h=2$] and $0.961243$ [$d_h=4$]; note that for a single $M=1$ draw on the $d_h=2$ diagnostic state, `wiener_mmse_m4` increases single-draw cosine with its own filtered target `0.325314 -> 0.356051` while single-draw cosine with the unfiltered exact target drops `0.325314 -> 0.290734` due to shrinkage bias, whereas under $M=4$ Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ multiplexing on $d_h=4$, `wiener_mmse_m4` improves **both** filtered-target cosine **and** exact unfiltered-target cosine while cutting exact unfiltered-target MSE under both the toy `(i+s)%4` channel assignment [`exp71` Part 1: filtered cosine `0.591425 -> 0.729859`, exact unfiltered cosine `0.591425 -> 0.653584`, exact MSE `0.623524 -> 0.265920`] and the rotary-pair-aware `(i+i//2+s)%4` channel assignment used in training [`verify_paper5.py` Check 6AF: filtered cosine `0.506117 -> 0.656104`, exact unfiltered cosine `0.506117 -> 0.581673`, exact MSE `0.889622 -> 0.350619`]).*
5. **Proposition 7 (Groupwise/Headwise Rectangular Stiefel Polar Factor Nuclear-Norm Domination [Zhang et al., 2026 [12]] vs. the Implemented 3-Component Per-Head Pre-Polar Muon Optimizer Package and Group Granularity $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$):** *Restating and applying the **Group Muon** formulation of Zhang et al. (`arXiv:2605.08933v1`, Section 2.2, Proposition 1, & Section 3) [12], let $G = [G_1^\top, \dots, G_{H/g}^\top]^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ ($D = H d_h$) be an update matrix for an attention projection partitioned into $k = H / g$ head-group blocks $G_i \in \mathbb{R}^{m_i \times D}$ ($m_i = g d_h$, with finest per-head splitting $g = 1 \implies k = H, m_i = d_h$, and whole-matrix orthogonalization $g = H \implies k = 1, m_1 = D$) of full row rank $\operatorname{rank}(G) = D$ and $\operatorname{rank}(G_i) = m_i$. Compare the **idealized whole-matrix (`Joint`, $g=H$) exact polar factor** $U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(G) := (G G^\top)^{-1/2} G = G(G^\top G)^{-1/2} = U_{\text{svd}} V_{\text{svd}}^\top \in \mathcal{O}(D)$ against the stacked **idealized Groupwise/Per-Head Rectangular Stiefel exact polar factor** $U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{grp}}(G) := [(U_{\text{exact}, 1}^{\text{grp}})^\top, \dots, (U_{\text{exact}, k}^{\text{grp}})^\top]^\top$ where each block is orthogonalized independently on $\operatorname{St}(m_i, D) := \{U_i \in \mathbb{R}^{m_i \times D} : U_i U_i^\top = I_{m_i}\}$ via $U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}}(G_i) := (G_i G_i^\top)^{-1/2} G_i = U_{\text{svd}, i} V_{\text{svd}, i}^\top$ (note that because $\operatorname{rank}(G_i) = m_i < D$ for $k \ge 2$, the $D \times D$ right Gram matrix $G_i^\top G_i$ has rank $m_i < D$ and is singular, so the right-sided representation requires the Moore–Penrose pseudoinverse $G_i (G_i^\top G_i)^{1/2\dagger}$, whereas the left Gram matrix $G_i G_i^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{m_i \times m_i}$ is strictly positive definite). As established by Zhang et al. [12] (`Section 2.2`, `Proposition 1`, and `Section 3`):*
   $$
   \left\|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{grp}}(G)\right\|_F^2 = \sum_{i=1}^{k} \operatorname{tr}\!\left(U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}} (U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}})^\top\right) = \sum_{i=1}^{k} m_i = D = \left\|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(G)\right\|_F^2, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \left\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{grp}}(G), G \right\rangle_F = \sum_{i=1}^{k} \|G_i\|_* \ge \|G\|_* = \left\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(G), G \right\rangle_F,
   $$
   *with strict inequality $\sum_{i=1}^k \|G_i\|_* > \|G\|_*$ whenever two blocks share right-singular input directions ($G_i G_j^\top \neq 0$ for some $i \neq j$), and monotonic refinement $\sum_{h=1}^H \|G_h\|_* \ge \sum_{i=1}^{H/g} \|G_i^{(g)}\|_* \ge \|G\|_*$ whenever a finer head partition divides a coarser partition ($g=1$ vs. $g \in \{2, 3, 6\}$).*
   *Proof (Zhang et al. [12], Proposition 1 & Section 3):* Since $U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}} (U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}})^\top = I_{m_i}$ for each $i \in \{1, \dots, k\}$, $\|U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}}\|_F^2 = m_i$ and $\|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{grp}}\|_F^2 = \sum_{i=1}^k m_i = D = \|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}\|_F^2$. By polar duality on $\operatorname{St}(m_i, D)$, $\langle U_{\text{exact}, i}^{\text{grp}}, G_i \rangle_F = \operatorname{tr}((G_i G_i^\top)^{1/2}) = \|G_i\|_* = \max_{V_i V_i^\top = I_{m_i}} \langle V_i, G_i \rangle_F$. Since the rows of $U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}} \in \mathcal{O}(D)$ are globally orthonormal ($U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}} (U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}})^\top = I_D$), each sub-block $U_i^{\text{joint}} \in \mathbb{R}^{m_i \times D}$ satisfies $U_i^{\text{joint}} (U_i^{\text{joint}})^\top = I_{m_i}$ **plus** the $\sum_{i < j} m_i m_j$ inter-group orthogonality constraints $U_i^{\text{joint}} (U_j^{\text{joint}})^\top = 0$ ($i \neq j$; $H(H-1)d_h^2 / 2$ constraints at $g=1$). Optimizing over the strictly larger product manifold $\prod_{i=1}^k \operatorname{St}(m_i, D) \supset \mathcal{O}(D)$ yields $\sum_{i=1}^k \|G_i\|_* = \max_{V_i \in \operatorname{St}(m_i, D)} \sum_{i=1}^k \langle V_i, G_i \rangle_F \ge \max_{V \in \mathcal{O}(D)} \langle V, G \rangle_F = \|G\|_*$, with equality if and only if $G_i G_j^\top = 0$ for all $i \neq j$. $\square$
   *Adapting Groupwise Polar Orthogonalization to Backward-Free Delayed WM and Deconfounding the Implemented 3-Component Per-Head Pre-Polar Muon Package (`exp64` Part 1, `exp66` Parts 1 & 2, and `exp67` Parts 1 & 2):*
   - **Sample Gram Expansion, Input Self-Alignment vs. Population-Gradient Alignment, and Nonlinear Polar Coupling:** In backward-free Delayed WM, each head block $G_h = -\frac{1}{BT} M_h^\top X = \bar{G}_h + \Delta_h \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h \times D}$ right-multiplies the **same** normalized pre-attention activation $X = x_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{BT \times D}$ (or the per-head attention-filtered activation $\bar{X}_h^{(V)}$ for Tier-1 $W_V$) while left-multiplying head-specific perturbation modulations $M_h$ ($\mathbb{E}[\Delta_h \mid \mathcal{F}_t] = 0$). For a single realization, the sample right Gram matrix expands as $G_h^\top G_h = \bar{G}_h^\top \bar{G}_h + \bar{G}_h^\top \Delta_h + \Delta_h^\top \bar{G}_h + \Delta_h^\top \Delta_h$, where the linear cross-terms $\bar{G}_h^\top \Delta_h + \Delta_h^\top \bar{G}_h$ vanish only under conditional expectation $\mathbb{E}[G_h^\top G_h \mid \mathcal{F}_t] = \bar{G}_h^\top \bar{G}_h + \mathbb{E}[\Delta_h^\top \Delta_h \mid \mathcal{F}_t]$. In whole-matrix orthogonalization, $G^\top G = \sum_{h=1}^H G_h^\top G_h$ pools all $H$ heads' signal and perturbation terms into a single $D \times D$ matrix and enforces $U_h^{\text{joint}} (U_{h'}^{\text{joint}})^\top = 0$, rotating head $h$'s update away from shared right-singular input directions claimed by other heads (e.g., at $D=2, H=2, d_h=1$ with $G_1 = (1, 0)$ and $G_2 = (\cos 45^\circ, \sin 45^\circ)$, $U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{head}} = G$ gives $\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{head}}, G \rangle_F = 2.000000$ and $\cos(U_{\text{exact}, 2}^{\text{head}}, G_2) = 1.000000$, whereas $U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}} \in \mathcal{O}(2)$ rotates head 2 by $22.5^\circ$ to $\cos(U_{\text{exact}, 2}^{\text{joint}}, G_2) = \cos(\pi/8) = 0.923880$ and reduces $\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}, G \rangle_F = \sqrt{2 + \sqrt{2}} = 1.847759$). We emphasize two caveats: (i) because the polar map $G \mapsto (G G^\top)^{-1/2} G$ is nonlinear and its input in backward-free WM is a noisy estimator $\hat{G} = \bar{G} + \Delta$, the nuclear-norm inequality $\sum_{i=1}^k \|\hat{G}_i\|_* \ge \|\hat{G}\|_*$ establishes **self-alignment with the noisy input $\hat{G}$** and does **not** by itself guarantee higher alignment with the population gradient $\bar{G}$ or improved training loss (cf. `claude_2` [11], Section 4.2–4.3); and (ii) under polynomial approximate whitening $O_i = \text{PolarExpress}(G_i)$, Zhang et al. [12] (`Appendix D.2`) show that the per-step descent bound trades the whitening gain $\sum_i \langle O_i, G_i\rangle_F - \langle O_{\text{full}}, G\rangle_F$ against a Frobenius norm cost proportional to $\sum_i \|O_i\|_F^2 - \|O_{\text{full}}\|_F^2$ whenever $G$ is rank-deficient or ill-conditioned.
   - **Why Proposition 7 Does Not Apply Directly to Pre-Equilibrated `Pre-Polar Muon` (`2x2` Counterexample, `exp66` Part 1A):** Our training code (`prepolar_muon_orthogonalize_batched` + weight-sphere retraction) modifies **three** operations when switching from `Joint` ($g=H$) to `Per-Head` ($g=1$): (i) **2-pass alternating row/column RMS equilibration** $G \mapsto \tilde{G}$ prior to orthogonalization, which normalizes column RMS across $d_h$ rows per head in `(H, d_h, D)` mode versus across all $D$ rows in `(D, D)` mode; (ii) **5-step BF16 `PolarExpress` polynomial iteration** (`row_gram_mean_diag` $\approx 1.027\text{–}1.051 \neq 1.000000$); and (iii) **per-head `(H, d_h, D)` vs. global `(D, D)` Frobenius weight-sphere norm restoration** (`init_head_norms` vs. `init_norms`). Because 2-pass row/column equilibration alters $G$ differently in per-head vs. joint mode before polar factorization, the inequality $\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{head}}(G), G\rangle_F \ge \langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(G), G\rangle_F$ holds for **raw exact polar factors** $U_{\text{exact}}(G)$ but is **not** guaranteed for equilibrated `Pre-Polar Muon` evaluated against the un-equilibrated $G$. Explicitly, for the $2 \times 2$ float64 counterexample $G = \begin{pmatrix}100 & 1 \\ 1 & 2\end{pmatrix}$ ($D=2, H=2, d_h=1$, verified in `exp66` Part 1A and `verify_paper5.py`), raw exact polar factors satisfy $\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{head}}(G), G\rangle_F = \sqrt{10001} + \sqrt{5} = \mathbf{102.2411} > \|G\|_* = \langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(G), G\rangle_F = \mathbf{102.0000}$ with $\|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{head}}\|_F^2 = \|U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}\|_F^2 = 2.0000$; however, after 2 alternating row/column RMS equilibration passes $G \mapsto \tilde{G}$ (which maps each $1 \times 2$ row in per-head mode to $(1, 1)$), even exact polar factors of $\tilde{G}$ evaluated against the original $G$ **reverse the alignment ordering**: $\langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{head}}(\tilde{G}^{\text{head}}), G\rangle_F = 104/\sqrt{2} = \mathbf{73.5391} < \langle U_{\text{exact}}^{\text{joint}}(\tilde{G}^{\text{joint}}), G\rangle_F = \mathbf{100.4581}$.
   - **Synthetic Rank-64 Gaussian Illustration (`exp64` Part 1), Real ClimbMix `d6` Checkpoint Audit (`exp66` Part 1B), and Group Granularity Audit Across $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ (`exp67` Part 1):** On a **synthetic shared-input rank-64 Gaussian testbed** ($G_h = \bar{G}_h + \Delta_h$ constructed from shared random rank-64 input matrices across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}, H \in \{4, 6, 8, 12\}$, `exp64` Part 1), the exact nuclear-norm ratio $\sum_{h=1}^H \|G_h\|_* / \|G\|_*$ grows monotonically with $H$ (`1.3070 -> 1.3936 -> 1.4563 -> 1.5440`), and cosine similarity of 5-step BF16 `Pre-Polar Muon` (`row_gram_mean_diag` $= 1.0332\text{–}1.0507$) with the noise-free per-head reference rises from `0.3700 -> 0.3226 -> 0.2857 -> 0.2386` (Joint) to `0.6270 -> 0.6482 -> 0.6581 -> 0.6693` (Per-Head). More importantly, auditing **real ClimbMix `d6` training checkpoints** on Seed 46 ($D=384, H=6, d_h=64$, `exp66` Part 1B & `exp67` Part 1, averaged across all 6 Transformer blocks at Step 0 [`step=2`, first Stage-1 consumption step] and Step 120 across all divisor group sizes $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ on $W_Q, W_K, W_V$, where $G_{\text{oracle}}$ is the batch sub-block-skip reference gradient) provides two **provisional descriptive diagnostics** (noting that two single-seed checkpoint snapshots against a batch sub-block-skip reference do not establish a causal proof of why grouping behavior in our short-horizon regime differs from the later-training grouping behavior in Zhang et al. [12]):
     1. **Checkpoint Spectrum and Batch-Oracle Alignment Across $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ on $(W_Q, W_K)$:** On the batch sub-block-skip reference (`oracle_subblock_skip_G_true`), Step-120 gradients have $\operatorname{rank}_{99\%}/m_i = 0.3785$ on $W_Q$ and $0.2674$ on $W_K$ at $g=6$, with Pre-Polar Frobenius norm gap $\|O_{\text{grp}}\|_F^2 - \|O_{\text{full}}\|_F^2 = +31.21$ ($W_Q$) and $+44.79$ ($W_K$) at $g=1$. On the backward-free WM estimator (`backward_free_wm_G_hat`), $M=4$ Rademacher/Hadamard probes yield $\operatorname{rank}_{99\%}(\hat{G}_i)/m_i$ of **`0.5720 -> 0.7791 -> 0.8503 -> 0.9245` on $W_Q$ and `0.3963 -> 0.5829 -> 0.6797 -> 0.8012` on $W_K$** as $g: 6 \to 3 \to 2 \to 1$ at Step 120, with Pre-Polar Frobenius norm gap `0.0 -> +12.86 -> +15.17 -> +20.01` on $W_Q$ and `0.0 -> +24.73 -> +27.37 -> +30.13` on $W_K$, while the nuclear-norm ratio $\sum_i \|\hat{G}_i\|_* / \|\hat{G}\|_*$ (**`1.0000 -> 1.1682 -> 1.2412 -> 1.3323` on $W_Q$; `1.0000 -> 1.2018 -> 1.3032 -> 1.4293` on $W_K$** at Step 120) and the **cosine alignment with the batch sub-block-skip reference $G_{\text{oracle}}$** increase monotonically as $g: 6 \to 3 \to 2 \to 1$: under Exact SVD Polar, **`0.1537 -> 0.1828 -> 0.1971 -> 0.2173` ($W_Q$) and `0.0477 -> 0.0573 -> 0.0624 -> 0.0678` ($W_K$)** at Step 0, and **`0.1298 -> 0.1541 -> 0.1667 -> 0.1836` ($W_Q$, `0.0798 -> 0.1129` on $v_t$) and `0.0373 -> 0.0453 -> 0.0500 -> 0.0565` ($W_K$, `0.0202 -> 0.0306` on $v_t$)** at Step 120; and under 5-step BF16 `Pre-Polar Muon`, **`0.1537 -> 0.1816 -> 0.1959 -> 0.2147` ($W_Q$) and `0.0486 -> 0.0577 -> 0.0623 -> 0.0666` ($W_K$)** at Step 0, and **`0.1311 -> 0.1529 -> 0.1664 -> 0.1804` ($W_Q$, `0.0802 -> 0.1104` on $v_t$) and `0.0388 -> 0.0452 -> 0.0495 -> 0.0557` ($W_K$, `0.0199 -> 0.0296` on $v_t$)** at Step 120.
     2. **Associative Value-Trace Identity $(A_h^\top E_h)^\top X = E_h^\top (A_h X)$ (`exp68` Part 1A) and Tier-1 $W_V$ Grouping (`Fixed-QK+V`, $g_V=1$) Across Both Delayed WM and Exact Global BP (`exp67` Arm 5 & `exp68` Arms 1–5):** For any head-output error $E_h \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times d_h}$, the exact backpropagation value gradient $(A_h^\top E_h)^\top X$ and the attention-filtered value contraction $E_h^\top (A_h X)$ are algebraically identical by matrix associativity (verified to $2.665 \times 10^{-15}$ max error in `exp68` Part 1A); thus, head-filtered value inputs $\bar{X}_h^{(V)} = \mathbf{A}^{(h)} X$ are an **exact algebraic identity shared with backpropagation** rather than an algebraic distinction between backward-free WM and exact backpropagation. In `exp67` Part 1 (Step 120, 6-block mean), moving $W_V$ from $g_V=6 \to 3 \to 2 \to 1$ increases the nuclear-norm ratio **`1.0000 -> 1.1981 -> 1.3581 -> 1.5602`** and lifts Pre-Polar batch-reference cosine alignment from **`0.2761` ($g_V=6$; `0.2952` in Block 0) to `0.3249` ($g_V=3$), `0.3547` ($g_V=2$), and `0.3912` ($g_V=1$, and `0.2955 -> 0.4137` under Exact SVD)**; empirically on ClimbMix `d6` (`exp67` Arm 5, `exp68` Arms 1–5, `exp70` Arms 1–8, & `exp71` Arms 1–8), per-head $W_V$ grouping ($g_V=1$) improves **both** Backward-Free Delayed WM (`-0.0015` BPB at 360st and `-0.0017` BPB at 720st under Pre-Polar Muon, and an **unconfounded `-0.0018 ± 0.0002` BPB [`2.0454 -> 2.0436`] at 360st and `-0.0015 ± 0.0006` BPB [`1.9558 -> 1.9543`] at 720st when holding `qk_orth_backend = v_orth_backend = "exact_svd"` fixed** across `exp71` Arms 1–2 vs. `exp68` Arms 2–3, plus `-0.0005, -0.0015, -0.0027, -0.0039` BPB across widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ in `exp70` Arms 1–3) **and** Exact Global / Block-Skip / Sub-Block-Skip Backpropagation (`-0.0018` to `-0.0015` BPB at 360st and `-0.0013` to `-0.0011` BPB at 720st) by virtually the same margin.*
6. **Proposition 8 (Energy-Matched Linear Spectral Tapering [`Exact-SVD Taper`] vs. Equilibrated `PrePolar-Blend` on Noisy Head Subspaces):** *Let $\hat{G}_h = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_{d_h}) V_{\text{svd}}^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h \times D}$ ($d_h \le D, \sigma_1 \ge \dots \ge \sigma_{d_h} > 0$) be an un-equilibrated head update matrix, let $U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} V_{\text{svd}}^\top \in \operatorname{St}(d_h, D)$ be its exact Stiefel polar factor ($\|U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h)\|_F^2 = d_h$), and let $\hat{\sigma}_r := \frac{\sqrt{d_h} \sigma_r}{\|\hat{G}_h\|_F}$ be its Frobenius-normalized singular values ($\sum_{r=1}^{d_h} \hat{\sigma}_r^2 = d_h$, mean $\bar{\sigma} := \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{j=1}^{d_h}\hat{\sigma}_j \le 1$). For any tapering coefficient $\gamma \in [0, 1]$, define the **energy-matched linear spectral taper (`Exact-SVD Taper`)** $U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h) := \sqrt{d_h} \frac{(1 - \gamma) U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h} \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F}{\|(1 - \gamma) U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h} \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F\|_F}$. Then:*
   $$
   U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}\!\left(s_1(\gamma), \dots, s_{d_h}(\gamma)\right) V_{\text{svd}}^\top, \qquad s_r(\gamma) = \frac{(1 - \gamma) + \gamma \hat{\sigma}_r}{c(\gamma)}, \qquad c(\gamma) := \sqrt{\frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{j=1}^{d_h} \left((1 - \gamma) + \gamma \hat{\sigma}_j\right)^2} = \sqrt{(1 - \gamma)^2 + 2\gamma(1 - \gamma)\bar{\sigma} + \gamma^2} \le 1, \qquad \left\|U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h)\right\|_F^2 = \sum_{r=1}^{d_h} s_r(\gamma)^2 = d_h = \left\|U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h)\right\|_F^2.
   $$
   *Proof:* Since $U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} I_{d_h} V_{\text{svd}}^\top$ and $\sqrt{d_h} \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\hat{\sigma}_1, \dots, \hat{\sigma}_{d_h}) V_{\text{svd}}^\top$ share identical left and right singular vectors $U_{\text{svd}}, V_{\text{svd}}$, their convex combination has unnormalized singular values $w_r(\gamma) = (1 - \gamma) + \gamma \hat{\sigma}_r > 0$. By orthogonal invariance of the Frobenius norm, $\|(1-\gamma)U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h}\hat{G}_h/\|\hat{G}_h\|_F\|_F = \sqrt{d_h}\,c(\gamma)$, so rescaling by $1/c(\gamma)$ yields $s_r(\gamma)$ and $\sum_{r=1}^{d_h} s_r(\gamma)^2 = d_h$. By Cauchy-Schwarz, $\bar{\sigma} \le \sqrt{\frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{j=1}^{d_h}\hat{\sigma}_j^2} = 1$, hence $c(\gamma) \le 1$ with strict inequality $c(\gamma) < 1$ whenever the singular values are non-uniform and $\gamma \in (0, 1)$. $\square$
   *In particular: (i) hard polar equalization ($\gamma = 0 \implies s_r(0) \equiv 1$) allocates equal unit energy to every singular mode, so when the true signal $\bar{G}_h$ occupies a low-rank subspace of dimension $r_{\text{sig}} < d_h$ while probe perturbations populate all $d_h$ modes of $\hat{G}_h$, hard polar equalization allocates $(d_h - r_{\text{sig}})/d_h$ of total update energy to the $(d_h - r_{\text{sig}})$ orthogonal modes (noting that in empirical checkpoints, small observed singular values $\hat{\sigma}_r$ alone do not prove that a mode is pure perturbation noise rather than a weak signal direction); (ii) for $\gamma \in (0, 1)$, because $c(\gamma) \le 1$, a mode is strictly attenuated ($s_r(\gamma) < 1$) if and only if $(1 - \gamma) + \gamma \hat{\sigma}_r < c(\gamma)$, i.e., **$\hat{\sigma}_r < \hat{\sigma}^*(\gamma) := \frac{c(\gamma) - (1 - \gamma)}{\gamma} \le 1$**, and strictly boosted ($s_r(\gamma) > 1$) if and only if $\hat{\sigma}_r > \hat{\sigma}^*(\gamma)$ (in particular, when singular values are non-uniform so $\hat{\sigma}^*(\gamma) < 1$, intermediate modes in $(\hat{\sigma}^*(\gamma), 1)$ are slightly boosted by $1/c(\gamma) > 1$: for example, at $d_h = 3$, $(\hat{\sigma}_1, \hat{\sigma}_2, \hat{\sigma}_3) = (\sqrt{2.18}, 0.9, 0.1)$ and $\gamma = 0.2$, $c(0.2) = 0.971678 < 1$ and $\hat{\sigma}^*(0.2) = 0.858390 < 1$, so $s_2(0.2) = 0.98/c(0.2) = \mathbf{1.008565} > 1$ despite $\hat{\sigma}_2 = 0.9 < 1$, whereas $s_3(0.2) = 0.82/c(0.2) = \mathbf{0.843901} < 1$; and on $\bar{G}=\operatorname{diag}(4,2,0,0), \hat{G}=\operatorname{diag}(4,2,1,1)$ in `exp73` Part 1 & Check 4E, moving $\gamma: 0 \to 0.2$ cuts orthogonal noise-subspace energy from `50.0000%` to **`41.1231%`** and lifts $\cos(U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}, \bar{G}): 0.670820 \to \mathbf{0.745109}$ and $\cos(U_\gamma^{\text{svd}}, U_{\text{exact}}(\bar{G})): 0.707107 \to \mathbf{0.764822}$); (iii) **Proposition 8 governs un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Taper` (`exact_svd_taper_batched` in `run_exp73` and `run_exp65`), whereas our fast polynomial algorithm `PrePolar-Blend` (`prepolar_blend_batched` in `run_exp72`–`run_exp75`, `run_exp64`, and `run_exp65`) first applies 2 passes of row/column $\ell_2$ equilibration $X = \mathcal{E}_2(\hat{G}_h)$ before 5-step BF16 `PolarExpress` $O_{\text{pe}} = \text{PolarExpress}(X)$, blends $(1-\gamma)O_{\text{pe}} + \gamma \|O_{\text{pe}}\|_F \frac{X}{\|X\|_F}$, and restores $\|O_{\text{pe}}\|_F$**—because equilibration alters singular vectors and singular values prior to blending (e.g., on any invertible diagonal matrix such as $\hat{G} = \operatorname{diag}(4, 2, 1, 1)$, pass-1 row normalization maps $\hat{G} \mapsto I_4$, so `PrePolar-Blend` returns $I_4$ for all $\gamma \in [0, 1]$ whereas `Exact-SVD Taper` obeys Proposition 8; verified in Check 4E), we evaluate **both** `PrePolar-Blend` and un-equilibrated `Exact-SVD Taper` on ClimbMix `d6` (`exp72`, `exp73`) and on Native `50.3M` (`exp74`, `exp75`); and (iv) on real ClimbMix `d6` checkpoints (`exp72` Part 1, Step 120), coordinate-isolated Tier-1 `W_V` ($g_V=1$) and `mlp.c_fc` ($k_{\text{mlp}}=1$) already achieve high post-polar batch-reference alignment $\cos(U(\hat{G}), U(G_{\text{oracle}})) = \mathbf{0.9774}$ (`0.9608` Pre-Polar) and $\mathbf{0.9352}$ at $\gamma=0$ (and row-splitting tall $1536 \times 384$ `mlp.c_fc` into $k_{\text{mlp}}=4$ square $384 \times 384$ blocks breaks column-orthonormality $U^\top U = I_{384}$, dropping post-polar cosine `0.9352 -> 0.8076` and yielding `2.0444 ± 0.0002` at 360st and `1.9546 ± 0.0005` at 720st in `exp72` Arms 3–4, while tapering `W_V/mlp.c_fc` with $\gamma_{V,\text{mlp}}=0.10$ in `exp75` Arm 4 slightly degrades 720-step DEV BPB from `1.9476` to `1.9480 ± 0.0009`), whereas subspace-coupled Tier-2/Tier-3 $(W_Q, W_K)$ have low $\gamma=0$ post-polar cosine (`0.1656` on $W_Q$, `0.0646` on $W_K$) that increases under $\gamma_{QK}=0.20$ (`0.1656 -> 0.1732` on $W_Q$, `0.0646 -> 0.0683` on $W_K$, and pre-polar cosine `0.1702 -> 0.2042` on $W_Q$, `0.0562 -> 0.0709` on $W_K$).*
7. **Proposition 9 (Un-Equilibrated Cubic Gram-Filtered Spectral Tapering [`Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` & `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend`] vs. Gavish-Donoho [13] Optimal Singular-Value Shrinkage on Marchenko-Pastur Perturbation-Noise Bulks):** *Let $\hat{G}_h = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_{d_h}) V_{\text{svd}}^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h \times D}$ ($d_h \le D, \sigma_1 \ge \dots \ge \sigma_{d_h} > 0$) be an un-equilibrated head update matrix, let $G_n := \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F$, and define the **un-equilibrated cubic Gram tensor** $G_3(\hat{G}_h) := (G_n G_n^\top) G_n \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h \times D}$ (computed via two batched $d_h \times d_h$ and $d_h \times D$ matrix multiplications with **zero SVD**). Let $\hat{\sigma}_r^{(3)} := \frac{\sqrt{d_h} \sigma_r^3}{\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{d_h} \sigma_j^6}}$ ($\sum_{r=1}^{d_h} (\hat{\sigma}_r^{(3)})^2 = d_h$, mean $\bar{\sigma}_3 := \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{j=1}^{d_h} \hat{\sigma}_j^{(3)} \le 1$). For any $\gamma \in [0, 1]$, define the **energy-matched cubic Gram spectral taper (`Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper`)** $U_{\gamma, 3}^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h) := \sqrt{d_h} \frac{(1 - \gamma) U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h} G_3(\hat{G}_h) / \|G_3(\hat{G}_h)\|_F}{\|(1 - \gamma) U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) + \gamma \sqrt{d_h} G_3(\hat{G}_h) / \|G_3(\hat{G}_h)\|_F\|_F}$. Then:*
   $$
   G_3(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}\!\left(\frac{\sigma_1^3}{\|\hat{G}_h\|_F^3}, \dots, \frac{\sigma_{d_h}^3}{\|\hat{G}_h\|_F^3}\right) V_{\text{svd}}^\top, \qquad U_{\gamma, 3}^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}\!\left(s_1^{(3)}(\gamma), \dots, s_{d_h}^{(3)}(\gamma)\right) V_{\text{svd}}^\top, \qquad s_r^{(3)}(\gamma) = \frac{(1 - \gamma) + \gamma \hat{\sigma}_r^{(3)}}{c_3(\gamma)}, \qquad c_3(\gamma) := \sqrt{(1 - \gamma)^2 + 2\gamma(1 - \gamma)\bar{\sigma}_3 + \gamma^2} \le 1, \qquad \left\|U_{\gamma, 3}^{\text{svd}}(\hat{G}_h)\right\|_F^2 = d_h.
   $$
   *Proof:* Since $G_n = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_r / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F) V_{\text{svd}}^\top$ and $V_{\text{svd}}^\top V_{\text{svd}} = I_{d_h}$, we have $G_n G_n^\top = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_r^2 / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F^2) U_{\text{svd}}^\top$, and right-multiplying by $G_n$ yields $G_3(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_r^3 / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F^3) V_{\text{svd}}^\top$, which preserves the exact left and right singular frames $U_{\text{svd}}, V_{\text{svd}}$ of $\hat{G}_h$ while cubing its singular values. Rescaling $\sqrt{d_h} G_3 / \|G_3\|_F = U_{\text{svd}} \operatorname{diag}(\hat{\sigma}_1^{(3)}, \dots, \hat{\sigma}_{d_h}^{(3)}) V_{\text{svd}}^\top$ and taking the normalized convex combination with $U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h) = U_{\text{svd}} I_{d_h} V_{\text{svd}}^\top$ yields $s_r^{(3)}(\gamma)$ and $\|U_{\gamma, 3}^{\text{svd}}\|_F^2 = d_h$. $\square$
   *In particular: (i) **Motivating Marchenko-Pastur Bulk Heuristic vs. Gavish-Donoho [13] Piecewise Optimal Shrinkage (`exp76` Part 1 & Check 4F):** Conditional on an idealized i.i.d. isotropic perturbation-noise model at aspect ratio $\beta = d_h / D = 64 / 384 = 1/6$ (Marchenko-Pastur median $\mu_{1/6} = 0.94415701$, bulk upper edge $y_+ := 1 + \sqrt{1/6} \approx 1.408248$; we use this random-matrix model as a motivating design heuristic rather than an unconditional spectral proof, since real WM head updates $\hat{G}_h = -\frac{1}{BT}M_h^\top X$ have correlated pre-attention inputs $X$, Hadamard-$4$ multiplexing, and Wiener-MMSE key gathering), Gavish and Donoho (`arXiv:1405.7511` & `arXiv:1305.5870`) [13] prove that the asymptotic Frobenius-optimal singular-value shrinker for $y = \sigma_r / (\sigma_{\text{med}}/\sqrt{\mu_\beta})$ is the **piecewise** function $\eta^*(y) = \frac{1}{y}\sqrt{((y^2 - \beta - 1)^2 - 4\beta)_+}$ for $y > 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ and **$\eta^*(y) = 0$ for all bulk modes $y \le 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$** (noting that the radical argument $(y^2 - \beta - 1)^2 - 4\beta$ is also positive below the lower bulk edge $y < 1 - \sqrt{\beta}$, so the explicit cutoff $y > 1 + \sqrt{\beta}$ is essential). In linear tapering (`Proposition 8`), a trailing mode with $\sigma_r = \sigma_1 / 3$ retains $1/3 = 33.3\%$ of the leading mode's linear weight, whereas in cubic Gram tapering (`Proposition 9`), cubing $\sigma_r \mapsto \sigma_r^3$ suppresses $\sigma_r \le \sigma_1 / 3$ by **$\ge 27\times$ in amplitude ($\le 3.70\%$ of the leading cubic weight) and $\ge 729\times$ in squared energy** without requiring an SVD call! On our 4-mode testbed $\bar{G}=\operatorname{diag}(4,2,0,0), \hat{G}=\operatorname{diag}(4,2,1,1)$ (`verify_paper5.py` Check 4F), `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` cuts orthogonal noise-subspace energy from `50.0000%` ($\gamma=0$) and `41.1231%` ($\gamma=0.20$ linear) to **`37.6339%` ($\gamma_Q=0.20$) and `27.0936%` ($\gamma_K=0.35$)** while lifting $\cos(U_{\gamma, 3}^{\text{svd}}, \bar{G}): 0.670820 \to \mathbf{0.780414}$ ($\gamma=0.20$) and $\mathbf{0.853246}$ ($\gamma=0.35$) and $\cos(U_{\gamma, 3}^{\text{svd}}, U_{\text{exact}}(\bar{G})): 0.707107 \to \mathbf{0.778594}$ ($\gamma=0.20$) and $\mathbf{0.819635}$ ($\gamma=0.35$); (ii) **Escaping the 2-Pass Equilibration Diagonal Collapse of the Skip Branch in `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` (`gram3_prepolar_blend_batched` in `run_exp76` and `run_exp65`, Check 4F):** Unlike `PrePolar-Blend` (which forms its linear skip branch from the 2-pass equilibrated tensor $X = \mathcal{E}_2(\hat{G}_h)$ and therefore collapses $\operatorname{diag}(4,2,1,1) \mapsto I_4$ for all $\gamma \in [0, 1]$), `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` forms its cubic Gram skip branch $G_3 = (G_n G_n^\top) G_n$ directly from the **un-equilibrated** Frobenius-normalized tensor $G_n = \hat{G}_h / \|\hat{G}_h\|_F$ and blends $(1-\gamma) O_{\text{pe}} + \gamma \|O_{\text{pe}}\|_F \frac{G_3}{\|G_3\|_F}$ (where the polar branch $O_{\text{pe}} = \text{PolarExpress}(\mathcal{E}_2(\hat{G}_h))$ still uses 2-pass equilibration prior to `PolarExpress`, unlike `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` which uses the un-equilibrated $U_{\text{exact}}(\hat{G}_h)$). Consequently, on any positive diagonal matrix such as $\hat{G} = \operatorname{diag}(4,2,1,1)$ where $O_{\text{pe}} = \alpha_{\text{pe}} I_4$, `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` preserves the exact cubic singular-value contrast of `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` to machine precision ($< 10^{-15}$, verified in Check 4F); and (iii) **Real ClimbMix `d6` Checkpoint Audit (`exp76` Part 1):** Across all 6 blocks at Step 0 and Step 120 on ClimbMix `d6`, only **`13.03 / 64` (`20.36%`) to `16.36 / 64` (`25.56%`) of $W_Q$ modes and `15.75 / 64` (`24.61%`) to `20.64 / 64` (`32.25%`) of $W_K$ modes** exceed the reference MP edge $1 + \sqrt{1/6} = 1.408248$, with Step-120 median-to-max singular value ratio $\sigma_{\text{med}}/\sigma_{\max} = \mathbf{0.2098}$ on $W_Q$ vs. **$2.18\times$ smaller $\mathbf{0.0964}$ on $W_K$**; on Step-120 Nesterov momentum tensors $M_{\text{nesterov}}$, moving from hard polar ($\gamma=0$) $\to$ linear `PrePolar-Blend` ($\gamma=0.20$) $\to$ tier-asymmetric `Gram3-PrePolar-Blend` ($\gamma_Q=0.20, \gamma_K=0.35$) $\to$ `Exact-SVD Gram3 Taper` ($\gamma_Q=0.20, \gamma_K=0.35$) lifts batch-oracle gradient cosine from **`0.1113 -> 0.1297 -> 0.1556 -> 0.1568` (`+40.9%`) on $W_Q$** and **`0.0320 -> 0.0434 -> 0.0770 -> 0.0774` (`2.42x`, $+141.9\%$) on $W_K$**, while also lifting post-polar oracle cosine $\cos(U, U(G_{\text{oracle}}))$ from **`0.0940 -> 0.0986 -> 0.1005 -> 0.1016` on $W_Q$ and `0.0240 -> 0.0262 -> 0.0291 -> 0.0296` (`+23.3%`) on $W_K$**!*
8. **Proposition 10 (Superposed 4-Stage Post-Install Dual 1-Hop [MLP + Attention-Value] Highway Weight Maximization in Fixed $O(1)$ Pipeline Depth, `exp77` & Check 4G):** *In an $L$-layer causal Transformer with residual scalings $\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(i)}, \lambda_{x_0}^{(i)}$ and terminal multipliers $\gamma_i = \prod_{m=i+1}^{L-1}\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)}$, define the exact upstream-to-downstream residual multiplier from subblock $l$ to subblock $i$ as $\gamma_{l \to i}^{\text{attn}} := \prod_{m=l+1}^{i}\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)}$ for $i > l$, $\gamma_{l \to i}^{\text{mlp}} := \prod_{m=l+1}^{i}\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)}$ for $i \ge l$ (so $\gamma_{l \to l}^{\text{mlp}} = 1$ captures the intra-block $\text{attn}^{(l)} \to \text{mlp}^{(l)}$ path), and $\alpha_0^{(i)} := \prod_{m=0}^{i}\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)} + \sum_{m=0}^{i}\lambda_{x_0}^{(m)}\prod_{r=m+1}^{i}\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(r)}$ from $x_0$ to block $i$. For any downstream block $i \in \{0, \dots, L-1\}$ at token $n = (b, s)$, let $z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}$ and $z_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)}$ be the pre-`RMSNorm` inputs to $\text{MLP}^{(i)}$ and $\text{Attn}^{(i)}$ ($x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)} = \text{RMSNorm}(z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)})$, $x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)} = \text{RMSNorm}(z_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)})$), let $u_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i, 0)} := R_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i, 0)} \odot (\xi_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}/\sigma) = (W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(i)\top} u_{\text{bus}, n}^{(i, 0)}) \odot \phi'(h_{\text{pre}, n}^{(i)}) + O(\sigma) \in \mathbb{R}^{4D}$ be the 0-hop Stage-1 MLP hidden modulation, and let $u_{v, (b, \tau)}^{(i, 0)} := R_{v, (b, \tau)}^{(i, 0)} \odot (\psi_{v, (b, \tau)}^{(i)}/\sigma) = W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(i)\top} u_{\text{bus}, (b, \tau)}^{(i, 0)} + O(\sigma) \in \mathbb{R}^D$ be the 0-hop Stage-1 post-attention value modulation. Using the cached forward causal attention weights $A_{b, h, \tau, s}^{(i)}$ ($\tau \ge s$, **zero extra forward passes**), define the **causal future-query value-modulation gather** $\tilde{u}_{v, (b, s)}^{(i, 0, h)} := \sum_{\tau \ge s} A_{b, h, \tau, s}^{(i)} u_{v, (b, \tau)}^{(i, 0, h)} \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ (stacked across heads $h=1,\dots,H$ as $\tilde{u}_{v, n}^{(i, 0)} \in \mathbb{R}^D$). In the forward pass of batch $t$, inject independent Rademacher probes $\xi_{\text{hwy}, n}^{(i)}, \xi_{\text{vhwy}, n}^{(i)} \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^D)$ at the `RMSNorm` outputs $x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}, x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)}$ with tangent displacements $\Delta x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)} = \text{RMSNorm}(z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)} + \sigma \xi_{\text{hwy}, n}^{(i)}) - x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)} = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)})} P_{x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}} \xi_{\text{hwy}, n}^{(i)} + O(\sigma^2)$ (and analogously $\Delta x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)}$), and at step $t$ (for batch $B_{t-2}$) construct the **1-hop MLP and 1-hop Attention-Value outgoing-column WM rewards** against live $W_{\text{mlp\_fc}}^{(i)}(t), W_v^{(i)}(t)$ followed by reward-side tangent projection (`project_out_forward_radial_nullspace` in `exp77_1hop_mlp_wm_engine.py`):*
   $$
   R_{\text{hwy}, n, d}^{(i)} = \Delta x_{\text{mlp}, n, d}^{(i)} \sum_{p=1}^{4D} u_{\text{mlp}, n, p}^{(i, 0)} W_{\text{mlp\_fc}, p, d}^{(i)}, \quad \hat{g}_{\text{mlp\_hwy}, n}^{(i)} := P_{x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}}\!\left(R_{\text{hwy}, n}^{(i)} \odot \frac{\xi_{\text{hwy}, n}^{(i)}}{\sigma}\right), \qquad R_{\text{vhwy}, n, d}^{(i)} = \Delta x_{\text{attn}, n, d}^{(i)} \sum_{p=1}^{D} \tilde{u}_{v, n, p}^{(i, 0)} W_{v, p, d}^{(i)}, \quad \hat{g}_{\text{val\_hwy}, n}^{(i)} := P_{x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)}}\!\left(R_{\text{vhwy}, n}^{(i)} \odot \frac{\xi_{\text{vhwy}, n}^{(i)}}{\sigma}\right).
   $$
   *Then:*
   1. **(a) Conditional Expectation of the Implemented `exp77` Highway Estimator & Theoretical Leverage-Inversion Corollary:** *Conditioned on batch activations and downstream modulations, the first-order expectations of the implemented `exp77` highway estimators $\hat{g}_{\text{mlp\_hwy}, n}^{(i)}$ and $\hat{g}_{\text{val\_hwy}, n}^{(i)}$ (`exp77_1hop_mlp_wm_engine.py`) under the full tangent displacement $\Delta x_{n, d}^{(i, 1)}(\xi) = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n^{(i)})}(P_{x_n^{(i)}}\xi_n^{(i)})_d$ are*
      $$
      \mathbb{E}_{\xi_{\text{hwy}}}\!\left[\hat{g}_{\text{mlp\_hwy}, n}^{(i, 1)}\right] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)})} P_{x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}} \operatorname{diag}\!\left(1 - \frac{(x_{\text{mlp}, n, d}^{(i)})^2}{D}\right) W_{\text{mlp\_fc}}^{(i)\top} u_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i, 0)}, \qquad \mathbb{E}_{\xi_{\text{vhwy}}}\!\left[\hat{g}_{\text{val\_hwy}, n}^{(i, 1)}\right] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)})} P_{x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)}} \operatorname{diag}\!\left(1 - \frac{(x_{\text{attn}, n, d}^{(i)})^2}{D}\right) W_{v}^{(i)\top} \tilde{u}_{v, n}^{(i, 0)},
      $$
      *whose Euclidean discrepancy from the exact 1-hop MLP and 1-hop Attention-Value Surrogate-BP pre-`RMSNorm` Jacobians $g_{z_{\text{mlp}}, n}^{(i, \text{1hop})} = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)})}P_{x_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}} W_{\text{mlp\_fc}}^{(i)\top} u_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i, 0)}$ and $g_{z_{\text{attn}}, n}^{(i, \text{1hop})} = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)})}P_{x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)}} W_{v}^{(i)\top} \tilde{u}_{v, n}^{(i, 0)}$ is bounded by Proposition 1(c)'s coordinate-leverage bound $\left(\frac{\max_d (x_{n, d}^{(i)})^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|W^\top u_n^{(i, 0)}\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n^{(i)})}$. As a theoretical corollary (verified alongside the implemented uncorrected estimator using the full tangent displacement $(P_x \xi)_d$ with non-zero off-diagonal sampling variance to `< 1e-14` max absolute error in FP64 across all $2^{2\times 4} = 256$ Rademacher sign patterns on $N=2, D=4$ in `verify_paper5.py` Check 4G, and evaluated on 0-hop WM in `exp60` Arms 3 & 6, though not applied in the `exp77` training runs), inserting the closed-form diagonal leverage inverse $L(x) := \operatorname{diag}(1 - x_d^2/D)^{-1}$ prior to $P_x$ eliminates the $\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_d^2/D)$ factor identically.*
   2. **(b) Exact $O(1)$ 5-Stage Post-Install Pipeline Sufficiency & Linear Nesterov Superposition (`superposed_4stage` + `qk_p4comb`):** *Because all 1-hop paths traverse **5 linear weight stages** ($W_{\text{stage1}}^{(l)} \to W_{\text{proj}}^{(l)} \to W_{\text{fc}/v}^{(i)} \to W_{\text{proj}}^{(i)} \to W_{\text{lm}}$), a **fixed 4-slot ring buffer** (`prev1=B_{t-1}, prev2=B_{t-2}, prev3=B_{t-3}, prev4=B_{t-4}`) realizes every 1-hop highway with zero same-step relays: at step $t_0$, the forward pass constructs the $W_{\text{lm}}$ update (installed at $t_0+1$); `prev1` ($t_0+1$) constructs 0-hop Stage-2 modulations; `prev2` ($t_0+2$) reads live $W_{\text{lm}}(t_0+2)$ to construct 0-hop Stage-1 modulation and installs $W_{\text{fc}/v}^{(i)}$'s 0-hop update at $t_0+3$; `prev3` ($t_0+3$) reads live $W_{\text{fc}/v}^{(i)}(t_0+3)$ post-installation to construct the 1-hop highway bus and installs upstream $W_{\text{proj}}^{(l)}$'s 1-hop update at $t_0+4$; and `prev4` ($t_0+4$) reads live $W_{\text{proj}}^{(l)}(t_0+4)$ post-installation to construct the upstream Stage-1 1-hop updates. Constructing the 1-hop highway bus strictly post-installation of 0-hop updates in `prev3` and `prev4` distinguishes `superposed_4stage` from 0-hop `delayed_4stage` (a non-intervening-install staleness ablation).*
   *Proof:* By Proposition 1(c), for any linear receiver vector $a_n = W^{\top} u_n$, contracting the full first-order tangent-displaced `RMSNorm` probe $\Delta x_{n, d}^{(1)}(\xi_n) = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n)}(P_{x_n}\xi_n)_d = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n)}((1 - x_{n, d}^2/D)\xi_{n, d} - \frac{x_{n, d}}{D}\sum_{d' \neq d} x_{n, d'}\xi_{n, d'})$ with $a_{n, d}$ and multiplying by $\xi_{n, d}/\sigma$ yields the single-draw coordinate estimator $\frac{a_{n, d}}{\text{rms}(z_n)}((1 - x_{n, d}^2/D) - \frac{x_{n, d}}{D}\sum_{d' \neq d} x_{n, d'}\xi_{n, d}\xi_{n, d'})$. Since $\mathbb{E}[\xi_{n, d}\xi_{n, d'}] = 0$ for $d \neq d'$, its conditional expectation is $\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, d}^2/D) a_n$; applying the linear operator $P_{x_n}$ directly gives the implemented `exp77` expectation $\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, d}^2/D) W^\top u_n$ (and applying $L(x_n)$ prior to $P_{x_n}$ gives $\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} W^{\top} u_n = \nabla_{z_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i)}}\mathcal{L}_{\text{1hop}}$ for $W = W_{\text{mlp\_fc}}^{(i)}, u_n = u_{\text{mlp}, n}^{(i, 0)}$).
