For the value highway, since $y_{\text{attn}}^{(i, h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s}^{(i)} W_v^{(i, h)} x_{\text{attn}}^{(i)}(b, s)$, the exact partial derivative of $\sum_{\tau} \langle u_{v, (b, \tau)}^{(i, 0, h)}, y_{\text{attn}}^{(i, h)}(b, \tau)\rangle$ with respect to $x_{\text{attn}}^{(i)}(b, s)$ is $\sum_{h=1}^H W_v^{(i, h)\top} \sum_{\tau \ge s} A_{b, h, \tau, s}^{(i)} u_{v, (b, \tau)}^{(i, 0, h)} = W_v^{(i)\top} \tilde{u}_{v, (b, s)}^{(i, 0)}$, and chaining through $x_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)} = \text{RMSNorm}(z_{\text{attn}, n}^{(i)})$ gives the stated value-highway identities. Finally, summing $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}^{(l, \text{1hop})} = \sum_{i > l} \gamma_{l \to i}^{\text{attn}} \beta_{\text{val}} \hat{g}_{\text{val\_hwy}}^{(i)} + \sum_{i \ge l} \gamma_{l \to i}^{\text{mlp}} \hat{g}_{\text{mlp\_hwy}}^{(i)}$ aggregates all $i \ge l$ at step $t_0+3$ post-installation of 0-hop updates. $\square$
9. **Proposition 11 (3-Stage Low-Rank Predictive [`LP-WM`] & Oja-Stiefel Coordinate-Transport Bridges [`Oja-LP-WM`], 2-Call Column-Isolated Local Bridge Pullback vs. 1-Call Scalar-Contracted Transport Variance, Rescaled Step-1 Cross-Covariance Cold-Start Seeding, and Empirical $R_l^2(t)$ Wiener Gating, `exp78`–`exp79` & Check 4H):** *For each non-terminal block $l \in \{0, \dots, L-2\}$, let $\hat{h}_{l, n} := \text{RMSNorm}(x_{\text{out}, n}^{(l)}) \in \mathbb{R}^D$ be the normalized block-$l$ residual output at token $n \in \{1, \dots, N\}$, let $\hat{h}_{L, n} := x_{\text{final}, n} = \text{RMSNorm}(x_{L, n}) \in \mathbb{R}^D$ be the terminal normalized state, and define the **Rank-$r$ Coordinate-Transport Bridge** $B_l(h) := h + U_l (V_l^\top h)$ where $V_l \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ ($r \le D$) is a tall encoder (idealized on the Stiefel manifold $\operatorname{St}(D, r) := \{V \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r} : V^\top V = I_r\}$ and maintained **approximately column-orthogonal**, $V_l^\top V_l \approx I_r$, in code via finite-step Newton-Schulz polar retraction) and $U_l \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ is a forward displacement decoder trained against the readout-anchored forward target $d_{l, n}^{\text{tgt}} := (\operatorname{sg}(\hat{h}_{L, n}) - \hat{h}_{l, n}) + c_y w_{y_n}^{\text{norm}}$ (where $w_{y_n}^{\{ \text{norm} \}} := W_{\text{lm}}[y_n]/(\|W_{\text{lm}}[y_n]\|_2 + 10^{-6})$, with $c_y = 0.18$ on terminal bridge $B_l$ in WM and `joint_head` surrogate controls, and $c_y = 0$ on `pure_state` surrogate controls and on the optional next-block bridge $N_l(h) := h + U_{N, l}(V_{N, l}^\top h)$ predicting $d_{l \to l+1, n}^{\text{tgt}} := \operatorname{sg}(\hat{h}_{l+1, n}) - \hat{h}_{l, n}$). Let $z_{l, n} := V_l^\top \hat{h}_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^r$, let $\widehat{\Delta h}_{l, n} := U_l z_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^D$, and let $e_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^D$ be a downstream post-bridge modulation vector at token $n$. Then:*
   1. **(a) Embedded-Metric Stiefel Geometry vs. Implemented Retracted Alternating Least-Squares (`oja_stiefel_bridge_step`), Approximate Column-Normalized Tall Polar Retraction (`stiefel_polar_retract_tall`), Rescaled Step-1 Cross-Covariance Seeding, and Empirical $R_l^2(t)$ Wiener Gating:** *For any smooth objective $f(V)$ on $\operatorname{St}(D, r)$ with ambient Euclidean gradient $G = \nabla_V f(V) \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$, the canonical **embedded-metric Riemannian gradient** obtained by orthogonal projection onto $T_V \operatorname{St}(D, r) = \{\Delta \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r} : V^\top \Delta + \Delta^\top V = 0\}$ is*
      $$
      \operatorname{grad}_{\operatorname{St}} f(V) = G - V \operatorname{sym}(V^\top G) = G - \frac{1}{2} V\left(V^\top G + G^\top V\right)
      $$
      *(in contrast to the normal-complement projection $(I_D - V V^\top)G = G - V(V^T G)$, which discards skew-symmetric within-subspace rotations $V A$ for $A^\top = -A$: e.g., at $D=3, r=2, V=[e_1, e_2], G=VA$ with $A = \begin{pmatrix}0 & -1 \\ 1 & 0\end{pmatrix}$, $(I_3 - V V^\top)G = 0$ whereas $\operatorname{grad}_{\operatorname{St}} f(V) = G \neq 0$ and $\left.\frac{d}{dt}\langle V\exp(tA), G\rangle_F\right|_{t=0} = \|A\|_F^2 = 2$, verified in Check 4H). In our implemented `oja_stiefel_bridge_step` (`exp79_oja_stiefel_bridge_and_d12_scaling.py`), rather than Riemannian tangent-space gradient descent, $(U_l, V_l)$ are updated by **projected/retracted adaptive alternating least-squares** on the local forward bridge objective $\mathcal{L}_{\text{bridge}}^{(l)}(U_l, V_l) := \frac{1}{2N}\sum_{n=1}^N \|d_{l, n}^{\text{tgt}} - U_l V_l^\top \hat{h}_{l, n}\|_2^2$ with **zero reverse error pullbacks through Transformer backbone blocks**: first, given $z_{l, n} = V_l^\top \hat{h}_{l, n}$ and pre-update residual $e_{l, n}^{\text{pre}} := d_{l, n}^{\text{tgt}} - U_l z_{l, n}$, $U_l$ is updated via entrywise `AdamW` (`custom_entrywise_adamw_step`, $\beta_1=0.90, \beta_2=0.99, \epsilon=10^{-4}$) on $\widehat{G}_{U_l}$ followed by Frobenius trust-region clipping $\|U_l\|_F / \sqrt{D} \le 0.25$; second, using the updated $U_l$ and $e_{l, n}^{\text{total}} = e_{l, n}^{\text{pre}}$ (plus $+0.10\, s_{\text{ce}, b, n} W_{\text{lm}}$ in `joint_head` surrogate mode), the latent residual projection $z_{\text{err}, n} = U_l^\top e_{l, n}^{\text{total}}$ is evaluated via 1-call column-isolated Triton WM contraction (`R_z = compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(U_l, err_total, sigma * xi_z, G=N)`, `z_err = R_z * (xi_z / sigma)` in `column_isolated` mode, or `err_total @ U_dir` with column-normalized $U_{\text{dir}}$ in `scalar_mode`), forming the **ambient reconstruction-error Euclidean gradient** $\widehat{G}_{V_l}$, which is updated in $\mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ via entrywise `AdamW` ($\beta_1=0.90, \beta_2=0.99, \epsilon=10^{-4}$) and retracted to approximate column orthogonality ($V_l^\top V_l \approx I_r$) via `stiefel_polar_retract_tall`:*
      $$
      \widehat{G}_{U_l} := -\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N e_{l, n}^{\text{pre}} z_{l, n}^\top = \nabla_{U_l}\mathcal{L}_{\text{bridge}}^{(l)}(U_l, V_l), \qquad \widehat{G}_{V_l} := -\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N \hat{h}_{l, n} z_{\text{err}, n}^\top = -\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N \hat{h}_{l, n} (e_{l, n}^{\text{total}})^\top U_l^{\text{new}}.
      $$
      *For any full-column-rank matrix $X_0 \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ with singular values $\sigma_{0, 1}, \dots, \sigma_{0, r} \in (0, \sqrt{3})$, the tall Newton-Schulz iteration $X_{k+1} = \frac{1}{2} X_k (3 I_r - X_k^\top X_k)$ maps each singular value via $\sigma_{k+1} = \frac{1}{2}\sigma_k(3 - \sigma_k^2)$ with **exact quadratic error recurrence***
      $$
      1 - \sigma_{k+1}^2 = \frac{1}{4}\left(1 - \sigma_k^2\right)^2\left(4 - \sigma_k^2\right),
      $$
      *converging quadratically as $k \to \infty$ to the exact Stiefel polar factor $X_0 (X_0^\top X_0)^{-1/2} \in \operatorname{St}(D, r)$. Because naive Frobenius normalization $X_0 = \widetilde{V}_l / \|\widetilde{V}_l\|_F$ starts an already-orthonormal $r$-column matrix at $\sigma_0 = 1/\sqrt{r}$ (giving $\sigma_0 = 1/8$ and 5-step Gram residual $1 - \sigma_5^2 \approx 0.413606$ at $r = 64$), our implemented `stiefel_polar_retract_tall` (`exp79_oja_stiefel_bridge_and_d12_scaling.py`) instead normalizes each column of $\widetilde{V}_l$ to unit $\ell_2$ norm ($\widetilde{V}_{\text{col}, :, j} = \widetilde{V}_{l, :, j}/\|\widetilde{V}_{l, :, j}\|_2$, so $G_0 = \widetilde{V}_{\text{col}}^\top \widetilde{V}_{\text{col}}$ has $\operatorname{diag}(G_0) = I_r, \operatorname{tr}(G_0) = r$) and divides by the heuristic Gram-Frobenius scale $c_0 = \max(1, (\|G_0\|_F / \sqrt{r})^{1/2})$ ($X_0 = \widetilde{V}_{\text{col}} / c_0$; while $c_0$ is a heuristic scale rather than a worst-case spectral bound $\|G_0\|_2^{1/2}$, an already-orthonormal $r=64$ matrix has $\|G_0\|_F = \sqrt{r} \implies c_0 = 1$ and $\sigma_0 = 1$ exactly, and at the deployed dimensions $D = 384, r = 64$, finite $K_{\text{NS}}$-step retraction produces an **approximately orthogonal** matrix $V_l^\top V_l \approx I_{64}$ with $\max_{i, j}|(X_3^\top X_3 - I_{64})_{i, j}| < 3 \times 10^{-2}$ at the per-step $K_{\text{NS}} = 3$ retraction and $\max_{i, j}|(X_5^\top X_5 - I_{64})_{i, j}| < 10^{-4}$ at the initialization $K_{\text{NS}} = 5$ retraction, verified directly at $D=384, r=64$ in Check 4H). Moreover, whereas zero-initializing $U_l(0) = 0$ (`exp78`) yields zero bridge transport on Step 1 ($R_l^2(1) = 0$), **seeding $U_l(1) = \alpha C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}$ at Step 1 as a positive scalar multiple of the empirical rank-$r$ cross-covariance** $C_{\text{init}}^{(l)} := \frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N d_{l, n}^{\text{tgt}} z_{l, n}^\top = \frac{1}{N}(d_l^{\text{tgt}})^\top Z_l \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ ($Z_l := \hat{h}_l V_l \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times r}$) yields the exact Step-1 explained variance*
      $$
      R_l^2(1; \alpha) := 1 - \frac{\|d_l^{\text{tgt}} - \alpha Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\|_F^2}{\|d_l^{\text{tgt}}\|_F^2} = \frac{2\alpha N \|C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}\|_F^2 - \alpha^2 \|Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\|_F^2}{\|d_l^{\text{tgt}}\|_F^2}.
      $$
      *In particular, unscaled $\alpha = 1$ can overshoot when $\|Z_l\|_2^2 > 2N$ (for example, at $N=1, D=4, r=1$ with unit-RMS $\hat{h}_1 = (2, 0, 0, 0)^\top, V_l = e_1, d_1^{\text{tgt}} = (-1, \sqrt{3}, 0, 0)^\top$, we have $z_1 = 2$, $C_{\text{init}}^{(l)} = 2 d_1^{\text{tgt}}$, $\widehat{\Delta h}_1 = 4 d_1^{\text{tgt}}$, and $R_l^2(1; 1) = 1 - 9 = \mathbf{-8} < 0$), whereas **every** scale factor satisfying $0 < \alpha < \frac{2N\|C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}\|_F^2}{\|Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\|_F^2}$ guarantees strict improvement $R_l^2(1; \alpha) > 0$ whenever $C_{\text{init}}^{(l)} \neq 0$. In `oja_stiefel_bridge_step` (`exp79_oja_stiefel_bridge_and_d12_scaling.py`), we set $\alpha_{\text{init}} = \frac{0.030\sqrt{D}}{\|C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}\|_F}$ for non-scalar `Oja-LP-WM`, which achieves immediate positive explained variance across all retained `exp79` runs (`0.2798–0.2905` layer-mean [`0.2075–0.3622` per-layer] at Step 1 on column-isolated `d6` and `d12` `Oja-LP-WM`, vs. `0.1835–0.1935` layer-mean [`0.1285–0.2530` per-layer] after Step 1's unseeded AdamW step on `oja_lp_scalar`, growing to `0.5189–0.5658` layer-mean [`0.3346–0.6610` per-layer] at Step 720 and up to `0.7469` per-layer at Step 360, verified in Check 4H & Check 6AO), while weighting the bridge credit branch by the empirical explained-variance Wiener gate $g_l(t) := \operatorname{clip}(\text{ema}(R_l^2(t))^{1/2}\cdot c_g, g_{\min}, 1.0)$ ($c_g=1.35, g_{\min}=0.35$) attenuates early bridge approximation error (and in Stage-2 bus assembly in `exp78_local_predictive_wm.py`, the gated bridge pullback is combined with $0.20\,\|\text{bus}\|_2$ of the normalized local forward residual-error direction $P_{h_{\text{out}}^{(l)}}(0.65\,w_y^{\text{norm}} + 0.35\,e_l^{\text{pre}})$ and blended $0.70\,\text{bus}_{\text{raw}} + 0.30\,P_{h_{\text{out}}^{(l)}}(\text{bus}_{\text{raw}})$).*
   2. **(b) Operational Classification & Exact Variance of 2-Call Column-Isolated Local Bridge Pullback vs. Input-Space and Latent-Space 1-Call Scalar-Contracted Bridge Transport:** *To transport $e_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^D$ across the low-rank auxiliary branch $h \mapsto U_l(V_l^\top h)$ at single-token stream resolution $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$) while keeping the Transformer backbone strictly backward-free (`backbone_reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0` for $W_{\text{fc}}, W_{\text{proj}}, W_{q,k,v}$ and zero $A_i^\top u_v$ attention pullbacks), let $z_{\text{err}, n}^* := U_l^\top e_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^r$ and $a_n := V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^D$, and compare 1-call scalar-contracted Rademacher transport against our 2-call column-isolated local bridge pullback:*
      - **1-Call Scalar-Contracted Rademacher Bridge Transport (`is_scalar_oja` / `oja_lp_scalar` in `exp78_local_predictive_wm.py`, `exp79` Arms 3–4, `bridge_local_pullback_calls_per_step = 0`):** *In the implemented `is_scalar_oja` path, an input-space Rademacher probe $\xi_{h, n} \sim \operatorname{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^D)$ (`xi_hb_bus`) is projected into latent space as $\Delta z_{l, n} = \sigma V_l^\top \xi_{h, n} \in \mathbb{R}^r$, contracted columnwise against $U_l$ and $e_{l, n}$, summed across the $r$ columns into the scalar $r_{b, n} := \sum_{j=1}^r (e_{l, n}^\top U_{l, :, j})(\sigma V_{l, :, j}^\top \xi_{h, n}) = \sigma \xi_{h, n}^\top (V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n}) = \sigma \xi_{h, n}^\top a_n$, and reconstructed in $\mathbb{R}^D$ as $\widehat{a}_{\text{in}, n} := \frac{r_{b, n}}{\sigma}\xi_{h, n} = (\xi_{h, n}^\top a_n)\xi_{h, n}$. This estimator is conditionally unbiased ($\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{h, n}}[\widehat{a}_{\text{in}, n}] = a_n$), but incurs $(D - 1)$ off-diagonal input-space Rademacher interference terms with exact conditional covariance trace*
        $$
        \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}_{\xi_{h, n}}\!\left(\widehat{a}_{\text{in}, n}\right) = \mathbb{E}_{\xi_{h, n}}\!\left[\left\|\widehat{a}_{\text{in}, n} - V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n}\right\|_2^2\right] = (D - 1)\left\|V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n}\right\|_2^2 \quad \left(= (D - 1)\left\|U_l^\top e_{l, n}\right\|_2^2 \text{ when } V_l^\top V_l = I_r, \text{ or } 383\times \text{ signal energy at } D = 384\right),
        $$
        *where the first equality $(D - 1)\|V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n}\|_2^2$ holds for any matrix $V_l \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ and the second equality holds exactly on $\operatorname{St}(D, r)$ and approximately up to the finite-$K_{\text{NS}}$ retraction residual when $V_l^\top V_l \approx I_r$. Similarly, if Rademacher signs are drawn directly in the $r$-dimensional latent space as $\xi_{z, n} \sim \operatorname{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^r)$ (`R_scal = <e_l, U_l(sigma xi_z)>`, $\hat{z}_{\text{scal}, n} := \frac{R_{\text{scal}, n}}{\sigma}\xi_{z, n}$), the latent estimator is conditionally unbiased ($\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{z, n}}[\hat{z}_{\text{scal}, n}] = U_l^\top e_{l, n}$) with exact conditional covariance trace*
        $$
        \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}_{\xi_{z, n}}\!\left(\hat{z}_{\text{scal}, n}\right) = \mathbb{E}_{\xi_{z, n}}\!\left[\left\|\hat{z}_{\text{scal}, n} - U_l^\top e_{l, n}\right\|_2^2\right] = (r - 1)\left\|U_l^\top e_{l, n}\right\|_2^2 \qquad (63\times \text{ signal energy at } r = 64).
        $$
        *(For example, at $D=4, r=2, V_l=[e_1, e_2], U_l^\top e_{l, n} = (2, -1)^\top$ where $\|a_n\|_2^2 = \|U_l^\top e_{l, n}\|_2^2 = 5$, exhaustive $2^4=16$ input-space enumeration gives exact variance $(4-1)\times 5 = \mathbf{15}$, whereas $2^2=4$ latent-space enumeration gives $(2-1)\times 5 = \mathbf{5}$, verified in Check 4H. Note also that in `exp78`/`exp79`, `is_scalar_oja` sets `scalar_mode=True`, which additionally skips Step-1 cross-covariance seeding, sets pre-schedule bridge learning rates $(\eta_V, \eta_U) = (0.006, 0.006)$ instead of $(0.04, 0.004)$, and column-normalizes $U_l$ in the encoder update, so `exp79` Arms 3–4 evaluate that combined scalar bridge recipe.)*
      - **2-Call Column-Isolated Local Bridge Pullback (`column_isolated`, `exp78` & `exp79` Arms 5–10, 14–18, `backbone_reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0`, `bridge_local_pullback_calls_per_step = 20` on single-horizon `d6`):** *Viewing $U_l \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ as the outgoing weight matrix of latent coordinate $z_{l, n} \in \mathbb{R}^r$ inside our fused Triton WM kernel (`R_z = compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(U_l, e_l, sigma * xi_z, G=N)`) contracts each column $U_{l, :, j} \in \mathbb{R}^D$ **individually** with $e_{l, n}$ and $\Delta z_{l, n, j} = \sigma \xi_{z, n, j}$, yielding*
        $$
        R_{z, n, j} = \left(\sum_{d=1}^D e_{l, n, d} U_{l, d, j}\right)(\sigma \xi_{z, n, j}), \qquad u_{z, n, j} := \frac{R_{z, n, j} \xi_{z, n, j}}{\sigma} = \left(e_{l, n}^\top U_{l, :, j}\right)\xi_{z, n, j}^2 \equiv e_{l, n}^\top U_{l, :, j} = z_{\text{err}, n, j}^*, \qquad \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}_{\xi_{z, n}}\!\left(u_{z, n}\right) \equiv 0,
        $$
        *and passing $u_{z, n} \in \mathbb{R}^r$ within the same step into a second outgoing-column contraction against $V_l^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{r \times D}$ with probe $\Delta h_{l, n} = \sigma \xi_{h, n}$ (`R_h = compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast(V_l.t(), u_z, sigma * xi_h, G=N)`, $u_{h, n} := R_{h, n} \odot \xi_{h, n}/\sigma = V_l u_{z, n} = V_l(U_l^\top e_{l, n})$) evaluates the **exact two-map local reverse derivative (local pullback)** $B_l^\top e_{l, n} = e_{l, n} + V_l(U_l^\top e_{l, n})$ across the block-local rank-$r$ auxiliary bridge $(U_l, V_l)$ with **zero conditional variance** (`var = 0.0` vs. $15.0$ input-space and $5.0$ latent-space on $D=4, r=2$, verified in Check 4H) while performing **zero reverse error matrix multiplications through Transformer backbone weights** ($W_{\text{fc}}, W_{\text{proj}}, W_{q,k,v}$) and **zero $A_i^\top u_v$ attention pullbacks**.*
   *Proof:* For (a), any tangent vector $\Delta \in T_V \operatorname{St}(D, r)$ satisfies $V^\top \Delta + \Delta^\top V = 0$, while any normal vector under the canonical ambient inner product $\langle A, B\rangle_F = \operatorname{tr}(A^\top B)$ has the form $V S$ for symmetric $S = S^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{r \times r}$; solving $V^\top(G - V S) + (G^\top - S V^\top)V = 0$ with $V^\top V = I_r$ yields $S = \frac{1}{2}(V^\top G + G^\top V) = \operatorname{sym}(V^\top G)$. Differentiating $\mathcal{L}_{\text{bridge}}^{(l)}(U_l, V_l) = \frac{1}{2N}\sum_n \|d_{l, n}^{\text{tgt}} - U_l V_l^\top \hat{h}_{l, n}\|_2^2$ with respect to $U_l$ and $V_l$ gives the alternating Euclidean gradients $\widehat{G}_{U_l}$ and $\widehat{G}_{V_l}$. For any $X_k \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times r}$ with singular values $\sigma_k \in (0, \sqrt{3})$, $X_{k+1} = \frac{1}{2}X_k(3I_r - X_k^\top X_k)$ maps each singular value via $\sigma_{k+1} = \frac{1}{2}\sigma_k(3 - \sigma_k^2)$, so
   $$
   1 - \sigma_{k+1}^2 = 1 - \frac{1}{4}\sigma_k^2(9 - 6\sigma_k^2 + \sigma_k^4) = \frac{4 - 9\sigma_k^2 + 6\sigma_k^4 - \sigma_k^6}{4} = \frac{1}{4}\left(1 - \sigma_k^2\right)^2\left(4 - \sigma_k^2\right),
   $$
   which converges quadratically to $\sigma_\infty = 1$ ($X_\infty^\top X_\infty = I_r$) whenever $\sigma_0 \in (0, \sqrt{3})$. For Step-1 seeding $U_l(1) = \alpha C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}$ with $C_{\text{init}}^{(l)} = \frac{1}{N}(d_l^{\text{tgt}})^\top Z_l$, expanding $\|d_l^{\text{tgt}} - \alpha Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\|_F^2 = \|d_l^{\text{tgt}}\|_F^2 - 2\alpha \langle d_l^{\text{tgt}}, Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\rangle_F + \alpha^2 \|Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\|_F^2$ and substituting $\langle d_l^{\text{tgt}}, Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\rangle_F = \langle (d_l^{\text{tgt}})^\top Z_l, C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}\rangle_F = N\|C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}\|_F^2$ yields the exact quadratic formula for $R_l^2(1; \alpha)$, which is strictly positive for all $0 < \alpha < \frac{2N\|C_{\text{init}}^{(l)}\|_F^2}{\|Z_l C_{\text{init}}^{(l)\top}\|_F^2}$. For (b), for any vector $w \in \mathbb{R}^M$ ($M = D$ with $w = a_n = V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n}$ in input space, or $M = r$ with $w = U_l^\top e_{l, n}$ in latent space) and $\xi \sim \operatorname{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^M)$, since $\xi_i^2 \equiv 1$ and $\mathbb{E}[\xi_i \xi_{i'}] = 0$ for $i \neq i'$, the $i$-th coordinate of $\widehat{w} = (\xi^\top w)\xi$ satisfies $\widehat{w}_i - w_i = \sum_{i' \neq i} w_{i'} \xi_i \xi_{i'}$ with $\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}_i - w_i)^2] = \sum_{i' \neq i} w_{i'}^2$, and summing over $i = 1, \dots, M$ gives $\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{w} - w\|_2^2] = (M - 1)\|w\|_2^2$ (where $\|V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n}\|_2^2 = \|U_l^\top e_{l, n}\|_2^2$ when $V_l^\top V_l = I_r$). Conversely, under 2-call column-isolated contraction at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$), coordinate $j$ multiplies its individual column inner product $e_{l, n}^\top U_{l, :, j}$ by $\xi_{z, n, j}^2 \equiv 1$ to materialize $u_{z, n, j} = e_{l, n}^\top U_{l, :, j}$ deterministically, and the second call contracts $u_{z, n}$ against the columns of $V_l^\top$ with $\xi_{h, n, d}^2 \equiv 1$ to yield $u_{h, n, d} = (V_l u_{z, n})_d = (V_l U_l^\top e_{l, n})_d$ with zero variance. $\square$

### 3.5 Fused Register-Tiled Contraction, Optimizer Co-Design, and Node-Perturbation Controls

1. **Eliminating 3D Outer-Product Materialization via Register-Tiled Contraction (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel`):** Building on the SRAM-fused outgoing-column contraction concept introduced in [5] (version 5), notice that in the stream-partitioned WM reward (Section 3.2),
   $$
   R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{j=1}^{D_{\text{out}}} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)} \, \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{D_{\text{out}}} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})\right),
   $$
   the 3D plasticity tensor $\Delta \tilde{W}^{(m+1, g)} \in \mathbb{R}^{G \times D_{\text{out}} \times D_{\text{in}}}$ never needs to be materialized in GPU HBM. In our fused Triton kernel (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` with tile sizes `BLOCK_G=4, BLOCK_R=64, BLOCK_C=64` for $M_g=1$, and `_triton_wm_reward_mg_grouped_kernel` for $M_g > 1$), each thread block loads a `(BLOCK_R, BLOCK_C)` tile of $W_{\text{rec}}$ from L2 cache once for `BLOCK_G` streams and accumulates `R[g, c]` directly in registers with **`0.0 MB` temporary HBM allocation**, cutting the 13-matrix `d6` WM kernel suite **$38.4\times$ from `134.31 ms` to `3.50 ms/step`** at $G=2,048$ (and **$4.4\times$ from `4.51 ms` to `1.03 ms/step`** at $G=64$).
2. **Joint Co-Design of the Optimizer and Delayed WM Credit:** Adapting the `Pre-Polar Muon` optimizer foundation of [8] to delayed WM, we note that the downstream optimizer step installs $W^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})$—which acts as Stage $m$'s **credit reader**:
   - **Update Orthogonalization and Receiver Stable Rank (Descriptive Association):** On our Seed-46 Block-0 diagnostic audit (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`), under `AdamW` (`adamw_hidden`), Stage-2 receiver matrices $W^{(2)}$ exhibit low stable rank (`mlp.c_proj` $\operatorname{srank}(W) := \|W\|_F^2 / \|W\|_2^2 = 2.75$, `attn.c_proj` $\operatorname{srank}(W) = 1.11$). `Pre-Polar Muon` [8] orthogonalizes each step's momentum update direction (which does not constrain the accumulated weight matrix $W$ to a Stiefel manifold); descriptively, this update orthogonalization is associated with a higher accumulated receiver stable rank on Block-0 `mlp.c_proj` ($127.24$ under `paper20_prepolar` and $141.33$ under `codesigned_prepolar`, $51.4\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`) and `attn.c_proj` ($54.85$ and $52.72$, $47.5\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`).
   - **Scale-Matched Sparse Embedding Velocity & Post-Installation Drift Annealing:** Because `wte` is initialized at $\sigma_{\text{init}} = 1.0$ before `RMSNorm(e_tok)` ($\|W_{\text{wte}}[v]\|_2 \approx \sqrt{D} = 19.6$), small embedding learning rates (`lr_wte = 3e-3` to `4e-3`) rotate normalized token directions slowly. Matching sparse embedding step size on `d6` ($BT = 2,048$) to $\sigma_{\text{init}} = 1.0$ (`lr_wte = 0.12, lr_ve = 0.06, betas = (0.80, 0.95)`) and linearly annealing learning rates (`lrm: 1.0 -> 0.2`) increases held-out validation attention-map shift $\|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(360) - \mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F / \|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F$ (Seed 46) by **$9.82\times$ in Layer 0 (`0.0838 -> 0.8233`)** and **$1.42\times$ averaged across all 6 layers (`0.6046 -> 0.8587`)**.
3. **Author-Defined Node-Perturbation Comparators (`SR-LNP` and `SR-NP`):** We compare Delayed WM against two specified node-perturbation recipes on the matched 4-matrix parameter subset (neither comparator is a zero-same-step-relay delayed pipeline, and neither is claimed as a replica of published Dust [9]):
   - **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, `train_dust_d6_fast` in `run_exp37_38_wm_vs_dust.py`):** Uses the same terminal-skip residual factors ($\gamma_l$ for blocks and $\alpha_0$ for `wte`) as Delayed WM, but differs in credit timing (it constructs $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}$ via $K \in \{4, 16, 64\}$ perturbation sub-passes and immediately uses $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}$ on the same step for embedding and hidden-layer estimates, whereas Delayed WM uses 3-stage delayed packets), perturbation location, and attention-credit treatment (scoring value perturbations locally with $\gamma = 0$ rather than via the forward attention-filtered trace). Thus, `SR-LNP` vs. Delayed WM compares two complete forward-only recipes rather than isolating sibling variance alone.
   - **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, `train_faithful_dust_d6` in `run_exp42_compliant_fp32_and_faithful_dust.py`):** An author-defined suffix-recomputed comparator on the matched 4-matrix `d6` subset ($K_{\text{site}} = 4 \implies 76$ suffix-recomputed draws/step $+ 24$ attention-internal draws/step) implementing Gaussian perturbations $a_t \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I)$, draw-mean centering $c_s^{(i)} = \tilde{\ell}_s - \ell_s^{(i)}$, suffix recomputation from block $l+1$ onward (`_run_suffix_from_block`), per-head local attention-output alignment $c_{s, h}^{(i)} = -\langle \hat{g}_{\text{attn\_out}, s, h}, \Delta o_{s, h}^{(i)} \rangle$, and future-token reward accumulation $r_{t, h}^{(i)} = \sum_{s \ge t} \gamma^{s - t} c_{s, h}^{(i)}$ with $\gamma = 0.98$ (whose discounted accumulation is verified by the `1.47` unit test). In `train_faithful_dust_d6`, both projection writers (`attn.c_proj` and `mlp.c_proj`) use $\hat{g}_{\text{wr}}$ obtained by perturbing the block-output residual after the MLP (which for `attn.c_proj` omits its path through the same block's MLP, a path not restored by suffix recomputation from block $l+1$ onward), and `lm_head` receives an analytic local loss gradient rather than vocabulary-slab perturbations.

---

## 4. Experimental Results and Mechanistic Verification

![Six-panel summary of genuinely backward-free 3-stage (`Oja-LP-WM`, Proposition 11) and Superposed 4-Stage Dual 1-Hop Highway Weight Maximization (Proposition 10), fused Triton kernels, co-designed optimizer, Wiener-MMSE key gather, Per-Head Rectangular Stiefel PolarExpress, Linear/Cubic Gram Spectral Tapering, and 12-Layer d12 scaling across exp26–exp79](supplement/figure-1.png)

### 4.1 Experimental Setup, Arm-Classification Ledger, Attention Backend, and Timing Provenance

All experiments (`396` multi-seed experimental arms = `1,187` total runs across seeds $\{46, 47, 48\}$ and $\{42, 43, 44\}$ in `exp18, exp21, exp26`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp79`: **`279` three-seed strictly delayed zero-relay Delayed-WM arms [`837` runs]**, **`3` three-seed same-clock compositional-relay diagnostic controls in `exp53`–`exp54` [`9` runs]**, **`13` three-seed node-perturbation arms [`11` `SR-LNP` + `2` `SR-NP` = `39` runs]**, **`4` head/lexical-only backward-free anchors [`11` runs: three 3-seed + one 2-seed]**, **`7` three-seed local-pullback diagnostic controls in `exp36` [`21` runs, `Rev. Matmuls = 1`]**, and **`90` three-seed backpropagation reference arms [`270` runs: `36` global BP + `11` block-skip surrogate BP + `26` sub-block direct-skip surrogate BP + `11` 1-hop/all-hop highway surrogate BP + `6` low-rank/Oja coordinate-bridge surrogate BP across `d6` 360/720-step, `d12` 360/720-step, widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, and Native `50.3M`]**) use the supplied `rustbpe` tokenizer (`vocab_size = 8,192`) and ClimbMix data stream on two Transformer regimes:
1. **ClimbMix `d6` ($L=6, D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}, H=D/64, D_h=64, V=8,192, B=8, T=256, BT=2,048$ tokens/step, $360$–$1,004$ steps $= 0.737\text{M}$–$2.056\text{M}$ tokens):** At our primary width $D=384$ ($H=6$ heads), total parameters $= 26,346,060$. The model has three value-embedding layers at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5`, each with a value-gate matrix `ve_gate` of shape $6 \times 32 = 192$ weights (`576` `ve_gate` weights total), plus `12` residual-mixing scalars (`resid_lambdas, x0_lambdas`). Accordingly: `active_params = 24,576,000` ($24.58\text{M}$, `93.2815%`) in the 4-matrix configuration (`mlp.c_fc, mlp.c_proj, attn.c_v, attn.c_proj` active per block, `attn.c_q, attn.c_k` $= 1,769,472$, `576` `ve_gate` weights, and `12` residual scalars frozen); `25,460,736` ($25.46\text{M}$, `96.6396%`) when `attn.c_q` or `attn.c_k` is also active (`exp30`–`exp31`, `exp45`), or when $(W_Q, W_K)$ are active on half the blocks (`3` of `6` blocks in `exp50`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp62`–`exp64`); `25,165,824` (`95.5202%`) when $(W_Q, W_K)$ are active only on the 2 full-window blocks `(3, 5)` (`exp51`); `26,345,472` ($26.35\text{M}$, `99.9978%` of total parameters) when all 6 principal hidden projections are active across all 6 blocks (`exp45`, `exp47`, `exp49`, `exp50`, `exp53`, `exp56`–`exp60`, `exp62`–`exp64`, `exp66`, where the `588` frozen entries consist of the `576` `ve_gate` weights at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5` plus the `12` residual-mixing scalars); and `26,346,048` (`99.99995%` of total parameters and `100%` of matrix weights, leaving only the `12` residual-mixing scalars frozen) when `ve_gate` is also updated via Delayed WM (`exp49`, `exp51`). In Section 4.7 (`exp39`, `exp56`, `exp62`–`exp64`), we also scale width across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ (`active_params` $\in \{14.55\text{M}, 24.58\text{M}, 36.70\text{M}, 67.11\text{M}\}$ for 4-matrix and $\{15.33\text{M}, 26.35\text{M}, 39.84\text{M}, 74.19\text{M}\}$ for 6-matrix). Disjoint evaluation splits (`evaluate_bpb_disjoint`) score `dev_tokens = 261,889` (`dev_bytes = 1,037,477`) and `valb_tokens = 262,009` (`valb_bytes = 1,050,500`), where $\text{CE (nats)} = \text{BPB} \cdot \ln(2) \cdot (\text{bytes}/\text{tokens})$ and $\text{PPL} = \exp(\text{CE})$.
2. **Native `50,332,176` ($L=8, D=512, H=4, D_h=128, V=8,192, B_{\text{eff}}=64, T=2,048, BT=131,072$ tokens/step, $100$ steps $= 13,107,200$ training tokens):** Total parameters $= 50,332,176$, with `active_params = 46,137,344` ($46.14\text{M}$, `91.67%`) in the 4-matrix configuration (`c_q, c_k` $= 4,194,304$, `512` `ve_gate` matrix weights, and `16` residual scalars frozen), `active_params = 48,234,496` ($48.23\text{M}$, `95.83%`) in the 5-matrix Spherical Active-$W_Q$ (`exp46`) and Upper-Half `(4, 5, 6, 7)` Active-$(W_Q, W_K)$ (`exp55`, `exp61`, `exp65`) configurations (where `2,097,152` `c_k` or lower-half `c_q, c_k` weights, `512` `ve_gate` weights, and `16` scalars are frozen), and `active_params = 50,331,648` ($50.33\text{M}$, `99.9990%` of total parameters) in the 6-matrix Decoupled Active-$(W_Q, W_K)$ configuration (`exp48`, where all 6 principal hidden matrix families are active and the `528` frozen entries consist of `512` `ve_gate` matrix weights across the 4 value-embedding layers plus `16` `resid_lambdas, x0_lambdas` scalars). To fit stream-partitioned eligibility-trace tensors in GPU memory, the backward-free Native `50.3M` runs accumulate `grad_accum_steps = 16` physical microbatches of `micro_batch_size = 4` at $G_{\text{micro}} \in \{256, 1024, 8192\}$ (`G_total` $\in \{4096, 16384, 131072\}$ streams/step, corresponding to $M_g \in \{32, 8, 1\}$ tokens/stream), departing from the supplied task's single-microbatch $B=64$, $499.65\text{M}$-token backpropagation recipe. Disjoint evaluation splits score `dev_tokens = 523,826` (`dev_bytes = 2,126,272`, $\text{bytes}/\text{token} = 4.059119$) and `valb_tokens = 523,715` (`valb_bytes = 2,129,527`). Because both `DEV` and `VAL-B` are evaluated at checkpoints across the ablation campaign, `VAL-B` serves as a second disjoint validation stream rather than an unopened holdout.
3. **Attention Backend and Timing Provenance (`model_and_data.py`, `benchmark_uncontended_timings.json`):** In `model_and_data.py`, the `fa3` handle wraps PyTorch's native fused `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(..., is_causal=True)`. In Tables 4–6, `Run GPU` reports the **measured 3-seed training-region GPU time (`total_train_gpu_s_mean`)** summed over forward and learning-rule CUDA intervals across all training steps—excluding data loading, host-to-device input transfer, and evaluation checkpoints—so matched-time comparisons match **measured training-region GPU time** rather than total elapsed wall-clock time or FLOPs. Furthermore, as shown in Figure 1E and Tables 5–6, the specialized 4-matrix trainer in `exp37`/`exp39` (`~18.7–18.8 ms/step`, giving `6.74s` at 360 steps, `9.41s` at 500 steps, and `13.57s` at 720 steps) has slightly higher per-step throughput than the general 6-matrix/multi-probe trainer in `exp47`/`exp49`–`exp66` (`~21.35–21.65 ms/step` when running 4-matrix controls, giving `8.20s` at 384 steps, `9.39s` at 438 steps, `15.62s` at 720 steps, `18.91s` at 875 steps, and `21.55s` at 1,004 steps) due to the general trainer's per-block probe-list and gate bookkeeping; we therefore plot their trajectories as separate trainer curves in Figure 1E and conduct all 4-vs-6-matrix matched-training-time comparisons (`384, 438, 720, 840, 875, 1,004` steps) strictly inside the same `exp49`–`exp66` trainer. `Est.` reports the **30-step steady-state benchmark estimate (`est_360step_s` in `benchmark_uncontended_timings.json`)**, which measures 35 steps on an uncontended RTX 4090, discards 5 warmup steps, and excludes evaluation, dataloader, and host-transfer overhead. In Tables 3 and 7, `Fwd / Rule / Wall (ms)` reports per-step forward GPU time, learning-rule GPU time, and either total step GPU time (`Fwd + Rule` in `exp40`–`exp48`, `exp55`, `exp61`, `exp65`) or complete elapsed wall-clock step time including evaluation checkpoints (`t_wall_step_ms_including_eval` in `exp26`–`exp36`).

### 4.2 Verification of Propositions 1 and 2: Stream Partitioning, Normalization Controls, and Filtered Traces (`exp26`, `exp28`, `exp35`)

Table 1 reports the single-step cosine similarity at initialization between our backward-free eligibility-trace pseudo-gradient estimator and the **language-model cross-entropy loss gradient** $\nabla_W \mathcal{L}_{\text{CE}}$ (computed via a separate diagnostic reference call `loss_ref.backward()`) on `d6` ($BT = 2,048$) across stream counts $G = BT / M_g$ (`exp26_results.json`, `exp28_results.json`). We emphasize that Table 1 is a single-step directional diagnostic rather than a claim of maintained gradient alignment throughout training.

**Table 1:** Single-step cosine alignment with the language-model cross-entropy loss gradient $\nabla_W \mathcal{L}_{\text{CE}}$ on ClimbMix `d6` ($BT = 2,048$) across stream counts $G \in \{1, 64, 256, 2048\}$ (`exp26`, `exp28`).

**Stream Scale-Normalization Controls (`exp35` Part A):** To test whether the stream scaling effect in Table 1 survives when update magnitude is held invariant to $M_g$ (Proposition 1), we ran a controlled 3-seed comparison on `d6` setting `scale_factor = float(M_g)` so that $\widehat{G}_{\text{norm}}(G) = M_g \widehat{G}_{\text{code}}(G)$ has the identical conditional mean $\frac{1}{N}\sum_n a_{n, i} x_n$ across all stream sizes:
- At $G = 1$ ($M_g = 2,048$): `scale_factor = 2048.0` yields **`2.6097 ± 0.0111` DEV BPB** (`2.5719 ± 0.0106` VAL-B), compared to `scale_factor = 1.0` (`2.5939 ± 0.0109` DEV BPB).
- At $G = 64$ ($M_g = 32$): `scale_factor = 32.0` yields **`2.3361 ± 0.0084` DEV BPB** (`2.2995 ± 0.0086` VAL-B), compared to `scale_factor = 1.0` (`2.3364 ± 0.0051` DEV BPB).
- At $G = 256$ ($M_g = 8$): `scale_factor = 8.0` yields **`2.3008 ± 0.0074` DEV BPB** (`2.2635 ± 0.0065` VAL-B), compared to `scale_factor = 1.0` (`2.2966 ± 0.0115` DEV BPB).
- On the canonical `Pre-Polar Muon` trainer (`strict_post_install`, `entrywise_b09_eps1e4`, $G=64$, `exp35` Part A2): `scale_factor = 1.0` reaches **`2.2541 ± 0.0013` DEV BPB** and `scale_factor = 32.0` reaches **`2.2539 ± 0.0013` DEV BPB** (agreeing to within $0.0002$ BPB).
These controls establish that the learning gains from increasing $G$ ($M_g \to 1$) are driven by the elimination of within-stream cross-token pair interference (Proposition 1(b)), not by artificial update rescaling.

### 4.3 Verification of Propositions 3 and 4: 20-Step Post-Installation Credit Drift, Kurtosis Audit, and Batch-Size Occupancy (`exp31`, `exp34`–`exp36`)

To test how receiver weight installation affects Stage-1 (`c_proj` receiver) and Stage-2 (`lm_head` receiver) rewards, we measured the 20-step mean cosine similarity, relative L2 norm drift $\|u_{\text{post}} - u_{\text{pre}}\|_2 / \|u_{\text{pre}}\|_2$, activation fourth moments, and batch-size occupancy on `d6` (`exp31`, `exp34`–`exp36` Part 0, Table 2):

1. **Stage-1 Receiver Drift (`exp31` Part 0, `exp35` Part 0):** When Stage 2 (`mlp.c_proj, attn.c_proj`) is updated with `AdamW`, one receiver step $\delta W^{(2)}$ induces a **`23.56%` relative L2 drift** in Stage-1 rewards ($\cos = 0.976474$). Replacing `AdamW` on Stage 2 with Stiefel-orthogonalized `Pre-Polar Muon` reduces Stage-1 relative L2 reward drift to **`2.85%` ($\cos = 0.999597$)** in `exp31` and **`4.04%` ($\cos = 0.999238$)** in `exp35` Part 0.
2. **Activation Kurtosis Expectation Bound vs. Realized Token Ratios (`exp35` Part 0):** Across the 20-step diagnostic trajectory (`run_part0_kurtosis_and_rmsnorm_variance_audit`, which updates `lm_head` and block-0 `mlp.c_proj` over 20 steps on `d6`), the normalized fourth moment $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}) = \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D x_{\text{final}, i}^4$ has token mean **`2.9398`** (token max `4.2932`). Proposition 4 bounds the **expected** first-order same-token residual ratio $\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[\|P_x b\|_2^2]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}\|_2^2} \le \frac{\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}})}{D}$; on these states, the mean theoretical expectation bound is **`0.007656`** and the empirical mean ratio across all $40,960$ token evaluations is **`0.007458`**, while individual realized draws $\xi_n$ can fluctuate higher (token maximum **`0.124439`**).
3. **Stage-2 Strict Post-Install Terminal Drift Across the $2 \times 2 \times 2$ Optimizer Cube (`exp34` Part 0, Table 2) and Batch-Size Occupancy (`exp36` Part 0A):** Across all 8 cells of `{entrywise, row_iso} x {beta1=0.5, 0.9} x {eps=1e-8, 1e-4}`, combining $(\beta_1=0.9, \epsilon=10^{-4})$ cuts current-gradient drift by **$47.8\%$ (`0.3511 -> 0.1832`)** and total post-install drift from `39.41%` to **`26.05%` ($\cos = 0.964738$)**. Furthermore, holding the `d6` architecture and sequence length $T=256$ fixed while sweeping batch size $BT \in \{512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192\}$ (`exp36` Part 0A) shows that singleton target-token occupancy decreases monotonically (`0.3957 -> 0.3516 -> 0.2958 -> 0.2265 -> 0.1620`) and post-installation relative L2 credit drift decreases monotonically (**`44.88% -> 41.95% -> 39.41% -> 37.46% -> 35.68%`**), confirming that larger effective token batches dilute single-token self-update collisions in $\delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$.

**Table 3:** Controlled 3-seed ClimbMix `d6` progression, complete 16-cell $2 \times 2 \times 2 \times 2$ Stage-2 Clock $\times$ Optimizer cube (`exp34` + `exp35` Part B1), 4-cell True Both-Stages Pre-Install comparison (`exp35` Part B2), compliant zero-pullback pure-`FP32` trace-cache clock controls (`exp42` Part A), local-pullback `s_lm @ W_lm` clock controls (`exp36` Part A, `Rev. Matmuls = 1`), and realized-component reader controls (`exp36` Part B).

**Key Mechanistic Findings:**
1. **Stage-1 vs. Stage-2 Post-Installation Drift Separation and Compliant Zero-Pullback Pure-`FP32` Clock Controls (`exp35` Part B2, `exp42` Part A):** Comparing True Both-Stages Pre-Install `(t-1, t-1, t)/(t-2, t-1, t)` with Stage-2 Pre-Install `(t-1, t-1, t)/(t-2, t, t)` shows that delaying Stage-1 reward construction until step $t$ shifts DEV BPB by only **`+0.0013` to `+0.0024` BPB** across all 4 `entrywise` cells (`2.1341 -> 2.1360` at `b05, 1e-8`; `2.1195 -> 2.1217` at `b05, 1e-4`), whereas delaying Stage-2 reward construction until step $t$ (`strict_post_install`) shifts DEV BPB by **`+0.0219 ± 0.0020` BPB** at `(entrywise, b05, 1e-8)` (`2.1360 -> 2.1579`). Because `exp34` cached `mod_bus_base` in `BF16` on the pre-install path and cached `x_final_clean` in `BF16` on the post-install path (`zero_dw_bf16_cache_rel_l2_diff_mean = 0.001687`), and because `exp36` Part A used a local pullback `s_lm @ W_lm` (`Rev. Matmuls = 1`), `exp42` Part A (`exp42_fp32_triton_results.json`) executes a strictly zero-pullback (`reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0`) pure-`FP32` trace-cache control via `_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` (`zero_dw_fp32_cache_rel_l2_diff_mean = 0.0`). Across 3 seeds (`46, 47, 48`), the compliant zero-pullback pure-`FP32` clock contrast is **`2.1359 ± 0.0022` vs. `2.1579 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB (`+0.0219 ± 0.0019` BPB)** at `b05, 1e-8` and **`2.1274 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`2.0901 ± 0.0001` VAL-B BPB)** at `b09, 1e-4`, confirming that the clock shift is a genuine receiver-installation effect.
2. **Row-Isotropic vs. Entrywise Preconditioning and Realized-Component Readers (`Proposition 4`, `exp34`–`exp36`):** Under `row_iso` preconditioning, Pre-Install and Strict Post-Install agree to within $\le 0.0003$ BPB across all 4 cells (`2.1577` vs. `2.1580`; `2.1662` vs. `2.1659`; `2.1296` vs. `2.1298`; `2.1335` vs. `2.1334`), consistent with Proposition 4's radial cancellation under scalar $c_n$. Under `entrywise` preconditioning, increasing $\epsilon: 10^{-8} \to 10^{-4}$ improves Strict Post-Install by **`-0.0279 ± 0.0001` BPB** (`2.1579 -> 2.1300`), and combining $(\beta_1=0.9, \epsilon=10^{-4})$ reaches **`2.1273 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** (`2.1274 ± 0.0002` in pure `FP32`). Conversely, folding the internal `Pre-Polar Muon` operator (`2.1613` vs. `2.1579` at `b05, 1e-8`; `2.1338` vs. `2.1273` at `b09, 1e-4`, `exp36` Part B) or the `lm_head` `AdamW` preconditioner (`2.2128` vs. `2.1579`) directly into the WM reader degrades learning because forward perturbations propagate through the unpreconditioned forward weights $W_{\text{live}}$.

### 4.5 Fused Register-Tiled Triton WM Speedup and Node-Perturbation Comparator Timing (`exp37`, `exp42`)

Profiling `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` in `exp34` revealed that materializing the 3D intermediate outer-product tensor $\Delta \tilde{W}^{(m+1, g)} \in \mathbb{R}^{G \times D_{\text{out}} \times D_{\text{in}}}$ in GPU HBM (`3,498.9 MB/step` across 13 matrices on `d6`) consumed **`134.31 ms/step`** (`86.3%` of total step time). Table 4 compares our fused register-tiled Triton kernels (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` for $M_g=1$ and `_triton_wm_reward_mg_grouped_kernel` for $M_g > 1$), batched `Pre-Polar Muon` (`prepolar_muon_orthogonalize_batched`: `10.35 -> 1.37 ms/step`), **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`)** (`train_dust_d6_fast`, using `@torch.compile` fused TensorCore chunk kernels `fused_bus_ce_chunk`, `fused_wte_dust_chunk`, `fused_mlp_dust_chunk`), and **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`)** (`train_faithful_dust_d6` in `run_exp42_compliant_fp32_and_faithful_dust.py`, which executes exact suffix recomputation from block $l$ onward across $K_{\text{tot}} = 76$ block-level draws plus $24$ local attention draws per step with $\gamma = 0.98$) on an uncontended RTX 4090 (`benchmark_uncontended_timings.json`, `exp37_39_master_results.json`, `exp42_faithful_dust_results.json`). ### 4.6 Joint Co-Design of the Optimizer, Three-Tier Attention Hierarchy, Full 6-Matrix + Exploratory `ve_gate` Routing, `Wiener-MMSE` / `Hadamard-4` / Sparse `Top-K=32` Key Gather, Groupwise/Headwise Polar Granularity $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$, and Same-Trainer Matched-Training-Time Controls (`exp38`, `exp45`, `exp47`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp59`, `exp60`, `exp63`, `exp64`, `exp66`, `exp67`)

Table 5 reports our controlled 3-seed optimizer co-design, 5/6-matrix attention-routing, exploratory active-`ve_gate`, $2 \times 2$ block-depth factorial, closed-form `Wiener-MMSE` / `Hadamard-4` / sparse `Top-K=32` / Stage-2 leverage-inversion controls, groupwise/headwise polar granularity ($g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ on $W_Q, W_K$ and $g_V \in \{1, 6\}$ on $W_V$, `exp64`, `exp66`, `exp67`), and same-trainer matched-training-region-time ablations on ClimbMix `d6` ($360$–$1,004$ steps, Strict Post-Installation Construction `(t-1, t [post-Stage3], t)/(t-2, t [post-Stage2], t)`, `exp37_39_master_results.json`, `exp45_d6_attn_routing_results.json`, `exp47_d6_6mat_joint_qk_results.json`, `exp49_d6_equal_compute_and_ve_gate_results.json`, `exp50_d6_equal_compute_qk_schedules_results.json`, `exp51_d6_720step_scaling_and_bp_controls_results.json`, `exp59_rake_key_gather_and_clock_controls_results.json`, `exp60_hadamard_qk_multiplexing_and_leverage_inversion_results.json`, `exp66_per_head_package_deconfounding_and_real_d6_audit_results.json`, `exp67_head_grouping_granularity_and_720st_exact_svd_results.json`) alongside the Seed-46 Block-0 geometric diagnostic (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`, `geo_and_attn_steering_audit.json`):
1. **Orthogonalized Update Directions and Receiver Stable Rank (`51.4x` Descriptive Association on Seed 46 Block 0):** Measuring Block 0 at step 360 on Seed 46 (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`), under `AdamW` on hidden matrices (`adamw_hidden`), Stage-2 receiver columns collapse onto a low-rank subspace (`mlp.c_proj` stable rank $\operatorname{srank}(W) = 2.75$, off-diagonal column coherence $0.3829$; `attn.c_proj` $\operatorname{srank}(W) = 1.11$, coherence $0.6024$). Under `Pre-Polar Muon`, orthogonalizing each step's momentum update direction is descriptively associated with a higher Block-0 weight stable rank on `mlp.c_proj` (**`127.24` under `paper20_prepolar`, coherence $0.0419$; and `141.33` under `codesigned_prepolar`, coherence $0.0411$, $51.4\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`**) and `attn.c_proj` (**`54.85` under `paper20_prepolar` and `52.72` under `codesigned_prepolar`, $47.5\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`**).
2. **Scale-Matched Sparse Embedding Velocity (`9.82x` Layer-0 / `1.42x` 6-Layer Mean Attention Shift) & Drift Annealing:** Matching sparse embedding angular velocity (`lr_wte = 0.12, lr_ve = 0.06, betas = (0.80, 0.95)`) to $\sigma_{\text{init}}(\text{wte}) = 1.0$ before `RMSNorm(e_tok)` (`-0.0194` BPB gain alone: `2.1273 -> 2.1079`) and linearly annealing learning rates (`lrm: 1.0 -> 0.5`: `-0.0296` BPB to `2.0783`; `lrm: 1.0 -> 0.2`: `-0.0101` BPB to `2.0682`) improves 3-seed 360-step `d6` validation BPB from `2.1273 ± 0.0001` to **`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`2.0315 ± 0.0004` VAL-B BPB, `292.66` PPL)**—within **`0.0150` BPB** of matched-subset Exact Backpropagation (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`). On the first held-out validation batch (`split="val"`, `B=8, T=256`, Seed 46), the co-designed schedule increases causal attention-map relative Frobenius shift $\|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(360) - \mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F / \|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F$ by **$9.82\times$ in Layer 0 (`0.0838 -> 0.8233`)** and **$1.42\times$ averaged across all 6 layers (`0.6046 -> 0.8587`)** (`geo_and_attn_steering_audit.json`: per-layer shifts `[0.0838, 0.5327, 0.6784, 0.7494, 0.7733, 0.8100] -> [0.8233, 0.8472, 0.8682, 0.8639, 0.8690, 0.8807]`) even with $W_Q, W_K$ frozen. Notably, matched-subset Exact Backpropagation also improves by **`-0.0548` BPB (`2.1080 ± 0.0006 -> 2.0532 ± 0.0008`)** under the same optimizer package, whereas applying the same package to `SR-LNP (K=16)` (`dust_K16_matched_codesigned_opt`) yields **`2.2602 ± 0.0010` vs. `2.2603 ± 0.0018` DEV BPB** (`-0.0001` BPB), establishing an empirical interaction between the optimizer package and the credit estimator in that `SR-LNP` configuration.
3. **Three-Tier Attention Credit-Assignment Hierarchy ($W_V$ vs. $W_Q$ vs. $W_K$), Key Truncation vs. Future-Query Accumulation, and 5-Matrix Spherical Active-$W_Q$ (`exp45`, `exp47` Part 1, `exp49` Part 1):** Building on the per-head attention-output reward, separate attention-internal jittering (including gates), token-local query credit ($\gamma = 0$), and discounted future-token key credit ($\gamma = 0.98$) formulations of Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`) and concurrent `general_pretraining_18/v9`–`v10` [5], extending our backward-free Stage-1 estimator to `attn.c_q` ($W_Q$) and `attn.c_k` ($W_K$) reveals a three-tier hierarchy in single-draw ($K=1$) credit assignment at $M_g = 1$, governed by the effective coupled subspace dimension $m_{\text{eff}}$. In our diagnostic suite (`run_attention_subspace_cosine_diagnostic`), pre-attention activations are held fixed and downstream gradient $\nabla_{y_{\text{attn}}}\mathcal{L}$ is supplied as an oracle downstream vector (`gy_attn` from `loss.backward()` at initialized `seed = 46`, Layer 0) specifically to isolate single-block attention transport mechanics from multi-layer delayed credit variance:
   - **Tier 1 (`attn.c_v`, $m_{\text{eff}} = 1$, Coordinate-Isolated & Forward-Factorizable via $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}$):** By Proposition 2, perturbing $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ coordinate-by-coordinate and contracting against $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ isolates every head coordinate without cross-coordinate or cross-token coupling, satisfying an exact conditional single-block reconstruction identity **`cos = 1.0000`** given oracle downstream credit at $K=1$ (distinct from its `+0.9069` end-to-end delayed backward-free cosine in Table 1).
   - **Tier 2 (`attn.c_q`, $m_{\text{eff}} = d_h = 64$, Token-Local in Time $\tau$ / Coupled Across $d_h$ Head Channels):** Because query $q_\tau^{(h)}$ enters only row $\tau$ of the causal attention matrix $\mathbf{A}_{b, h, \tau, :}$, perturbing $q_\tau^{(h)} \mapsto q_\tau^{(h)} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{q, \tau}^{(h)}$ alters only output token $\tau$'s attention output $\Delta y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$, so **zero cross-time interference arises at $M_g = 1$**. However, all $d_h = 64$ coordinates of $q_\tau^{(h)}$ mix into the scalar dot product $\langle q_\tau^{(h)}, k_s^{(h)}\rangle / \sqrt{d_h}$, sharing a single per-head reward $R_{q, \tau}^{(h)} = \sum_{c=1}^{d_h} R_{\text{attn\_proj}, \tau, (h, c)}$. For this specific single-draw per-head estimator at the initialized diagnostic state (`attention_subspace_cosine_diagnostic`), empirical cosine alignment is **`cos = 0.1357` at $K=1$** (comparable to $1 / \sqrt{d_h} = 0.1250$), scaling with draw count $K$ to **`0.2657` at $K=4$, `0.4748` at $K=16$, and `0.7385` at $K=64$**.
   - **Tier 3 (`attn.c_k`, Non-Local Across Future Queries $\tau \ge s$, Truncation Bias vs. Discounted Future-Query Credit, Closed-Form Wiener-MMSE Key Gather [`Proposition 6`, `exp47` Part 1, `exp59`], & Sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ Key Gather [`exp60`]):** Key $k_s^{(h)}$ enters the attention distribution of *every future query* $\tau \in \{s, \dots, T-1\}$ via the query-dependent softmax Jacobian $\operatorname{diag}(A_\tau) - A_\tau A_\tau^T$, which does not factorize into a query-independent forward trace. Conditioned on fixed pre-attention activations and downstream sensitivity vectors $\{g_\tau\}$, the discounted future-query key estimator satisfies the first-order expectation identity $\mathbb{E}[\widehat{g}_{k_s}^{(\gamma_K)}] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau + O(\sigma)$. Multiplying `r_head` at position $s$ by $\xi_{k,s}$ at the *same* position $s$ ($\gamma_K = 0$) captures only the diagonal self-attention sensitivity $J_{ss}^\top g_s$ while omitting all future-query credit $\sum_{\tau > s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$ (`cos = 0.0076` at $K=1$, `0.0534` at $K=64$). In our pure-Python 2-token finite-difference Rademacher enumeration (`eps_fd = 1e-5`, verified to tolerance `1e-9` in `verify_paper5.py`), same-position key correlation yields $(0.0, 0.0)$ across all 16 perturbation patterns when loss is supported at token 2, whereas accumulating future query rewards $\widetilde{R}_{k,s}^{(\gamma_K)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s, \tau - s \le W_l} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} R_{\text{head},\tau}$ at $\gamma_K = 1$ restores all future terms and matches the finite-difference key derivative $[0.317182, 0.422909]$. Across an 8-point discount horizon sweep $\gamma_K \in \{0.00, 0.50, 0.75, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95, 0.98, 1.00\}$ on `d6` Layer 0 (`exp47` Part 1), $K=64$ cosine alignment rises from **`0.0534` ($\gamma_K=0.00$) $\to$ `0.0887` ($0.50$) $\to$ `0.1171` ($0.75$) $\to$ `0.1329` ($0.85$) $\to$ `0.1423` ($0.90$) $\to$ `0.1529` ($0.95$) $\to$ `0.1565` ($0.98$)** before dipping to **`0.1340` ($\gamma_K=1.00$)**: $\gamma_K = 1$ removes truncation bias completely, while $\gamma_K < 1$ (e.g., $\gamma_K \in [0.85, 0.98]$) introduces a truncation bias whose magnitude depends on the omitted future sensitivity mass $\sum_{\tau > s} (1 - \gamma_K^{\tau - s}) J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$ in exchange for lower cross-time key superposition variance across the $T - s$ future query terms. Going beyond fixed recency discounting, **Proposition 6 (`exp59`)** shows that for a general causal gather matrix $W_{\tau, s}$ in $\widetilde{R}_{k, s}^{(W)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} R_{\text{head}, \tau}$, because the forward softmax perturbation $k_{s'} \mapsto k_{s'} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}$ already multiplies key $s$'s contribution inside query $\tau$'s reward by $A_{\tau, s}$ ($\mathbb{E}[R_{\text{head}, \tau}\tilde{\xi}_{k, s}/\sigma] = A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}$), naive attention-weighted (`Rake`) gathering $W_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$ weights each future query by $A_{\tau, s}^2$ in expectation, whereas the closed-form parameter-free **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter** $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1)A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \in [0, 1]$ eliminates the $A_{\tau, s}^2$ squaring bias on dominant links ($A_{\tau, s} \to 1 \implies W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}} \to 1$) while suppressing cross-key noise when query $\tau$ attends elsewhere ($A_{\tau, s} \ll \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2 \implies W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}} \to 0$). On `d6` ($M=4$, `exp59` Part 1), moving from $\gamma_K=0.85$ $\to$ `rake_attn` ($W_{\tau, s}=A_{\tau, s}$) $\to$ `wiener_mmse` ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(4)$) increases 6-block mean key cosine alignment **`2.98x` at Step 0 (`0.0317 -> 0.0870 -> 0.0945`; Upper-Half `3..5`: `0.0338 -> 0.0870 -> 0.0935`)** and **`3.22x` at Step 120 after attention heads sharpen (`0.0284 -> 0.0727 -> 0.0914`, $+25.7\%$ over `rake_attn`; Upper-Half `3..5`: `0.0223 -> 0.0618 -> 0.0753`, `3.38x`)**. Moreover, because $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M)$ is rational quadratic in $A_{\tau, s}$, **retaining only the Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ keys per query row (`wiener_mmse_topk32`, `exp60` Part 1)** preserves **`99.77%` of $\|W^{\text{MMSE}}\|_F^2$** at Step 120 (`98.71%` at Step 0) and matches dense $T \times T$ `wiener_mmse` key cosine to within `0.0001` (`0.1020` vs. `0.1021`).
   - **5-Matrix Spherical Pre-Polar Muon for `attn.c_q` (`2.0682 -> 2.0641 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB) & Factorized Controls (`exp45`, `Proposition 5`):** Because `q_r = norm(apply_rotary_emb(q, cos, sin))` is $0$-homogeneous in $\|W_Q^{(l)}\|_F$, standard unconstrained `Pre-Polar Muon` updates (`wm_G2048_codesigned_active_q_snr_matched_triton`, $\eta_Q = 0.001$: `2.0681 ± 0.0006`) or properly bound per-head scalar temperatures (`wm_G2048_codesigned_per_head_temp_triton`, where `block.attn.head_log_temp` is bound to the module for evaluation parity: `2.0681 ± 0.0006`) leave DEV BPB virtually unchanged (`-0.0001` BPB). By combining (i) unit-RMS tangent query perturbation, (ii) spherical Frobenius retraction $\|W_Q^{(l)}\|_F \equiv \|W_{Q, 0}^{(l)}\|_F$, (iii) high momentum $\beta_Q = 0.95$, and (iv) $\sqrt{d_h}$-scaled angular step size $\eta_Q = \sqrt{d_h}\,\eta_V = 0.048$, `wm_G2048_codesigned_spherical_active_q_triton` (`25,460,736 / 26,346,060 = 96.64%` active parameters) reaches **`2.0641 / 2.0636 / 2.0647` $\to$ `2.0641 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`2.0273 ± 0.0005` VAL-B BPB, `289.43` PPL)**—improving upon the 4-matrix baseline by **`-0.0041` BPB (`-3.23` PPL)** and capturing **`45.6%`** of the exact-BP 4-to-5-matrix $W_Q$-unfreezing gain (`2.0532 -> 2.0442`, `-0.0090` BPB). To factorize the geometric vs. hyperparameter components cleanly, we evaluate two matched-hyperparameter ($\beta_Q = 0.95, \eta_Q = 0.048$) Euclidean controls in `exp45`: (a) `wm_G2048_codesigned_euclidean_active_q_matched_hp_triton` (`spherical_qk = False, unit_rms_qk_pert = False, tangent_proj_qk = True`), which removes spherical Frobenius retraction and unit-RMS perturbation scaling while retaining tangent projection (`2.0648 / 2.0643 / 2.0655` $\to$ **`2.0649 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `290.02` PPL**); and (b) `wm_G2048_codesigned_unprojected_active_q_matched_hp_triton` (`tangent_proj_qk = False, spherical_qk = False, unit_rms_qk_pert = False`), which additionally removes query tangent projection (`2.0648 / 2.0644 / 2.0655` $\to$ **`2.0649 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `290.03` PPL**). Both controls attribute `-0.0033` BPB of the `-0.0041` BPB gain to step-size/momentum matching ($\eta_Q=0.048, \beta_Q=0.95$) and `-0.0008` BPB specifically to spherical Frobenius retraction + unit-RMS perturbation scaling; consistent with **Proposition 5**, while unprojected reward reconstruction retains single-site radial noise of relative energy $\approx 1/d_h = 1.53\%$ at $d_h=64$ (`exp49` Part 1), the projected and unprojected `exp45` arms are empirically nearly indistinguishable (`2.0649` vs. `2.0649`).
4. **Full 6-Matrix (`26,345,472 / 26,346,060 = 99.9978%` Active), Exploratory Active-`ve_gate` (`26,346,048 = 100%` Matrix Weights Active), $M \in \{2, 4\}$ Amortized Multi-Probe Probing, Wiener-MMSE / Hadamard-$4$ / Sparse Top-$K=32$ Key Gather (`Proposition 6`, `exp59`, `exp60`), Groupwise/Headwise Polar Granularity $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ (`Proposition 7`, Zhang et al. [12], `exp64`, `exp66`, `exp67`), $2 \times 2$ Depth Factorial, and Same-Trainer Matched-Training-Time Controls (`exp47`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp59`, `exp60`, `exp64`, `exp66`, `exp67`):**
   In `exp45`, naively activating both $W_Q$ and $W_K$ (`wm_G2048_codesigned_spherical_active_qk_triton`: `2.0669 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB) degraded performance relative to 5-matrix Active-$W_Q$ (`2.0641 ± 0.0006`). Across `exp47` Part 2, `exp49` Part 2, `exp50`, `exp51`, `exp59`, `exp60`, `exp63`, `exp64`, `exp66`, and `exp67` (`79` three-seed arms = `237` runs on ClimbMix `d6`, where 6-matrix arms without active gates train `26,345,472 / 26,346,060 = 99.9978%` of parameters—leaving the `576` `ve_gate` weights of shape $6 \times 32$ at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5` and `12` residual scalars frozen—and active-`ve_gate` arms train `26,346,048 / 26,346,060 = 99.99995%` = `100%` of matrix weights), we diagnose and resolve all three causes of that degradation and compare fixed-token vs. matched-training-region-time performance:
   - **Cause 1 — Cross-Probe Perturbation Contamination in Joint SDPA (`-0.0014` BPB Main Effect from Decoupling):** Following the separate attention-internal query and key jittering principle of Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`), perturbing both queries and keys simultaneously in a single FlashAttention call `sdpa(q_r_p, k_r_p, v)` produces $\Delta y_{qk} = \Delta y_q + \Delta y_k + O(\sigma^2)$, so the query reward $R_q \propto \langle w_{\text{head}}, \Delta y_{qk}\rangle$ suffers cross-probe interference from the independent key perturbation $\Delta y_k(\xi_k)$ (and vice versa). Evaluating **decoupled local attention probes** $\Delta y_q = \text{sdpa}(q_{\text{pert}}, k_{\text{clean}}, v_{\text{clean}}) - y_{\text{clean}}$ and $\Delta y_k = \text{sdpa}(q_{\text{clean}}, k_{\text{pert}}, v_{\text{clean}}) - y_{\text{clean}}$ during the originating forward pass ($t$, performing $1$ clean attention evaluation plus $2M$ perturbed attention evaluations per active block) and consuming them two steps later ($t-2$) eliminates cross-probe contamination: at $\gamma_K = 0.0, \eta_K = 0.012$, moving from coupled same-position (`2.0657 / 2.0657 / 2.0665` $\to$ `2.0660 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB) to decoupled same-position (`2.0649 / 2.0641 / 2.0650` $\to$ `2.0647 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB) improves 6-matrix learning by **`-0.0013` BPB**, and at $\gamma_K = 0.85, \eta_K = 0.012$, moving from coupled future-accumulated (`2.0646 / 2.0646 / 2.0647` $\to$ `2.0646 ± 0.0001` DEV BPB) to decoupled future-accumulated (`2.0640 / 2.0629 / 2.0625` $\to$ **`2.0631 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB, `2.0264 ± 0.0011` VAL-B BPB, `288.64` PPL**) improves learning by **`-0.0015` BPB** (or `-0.0018` BPB over step-multiplexed alternating $W_Q/W_K$ steps `2.0649 ± 0.0007`).
   - **Cause 2 — Key Truncation vs. Discounted Future-Query Accumulation & SNR-Matched Key Step Size (`-0.0015` BPB Main Effect):** Combining SNR-matched key learning rate $\eta_K = 0.012, \beta_K = 0.98$ with discounted future-query key accumulation improves decoupled 6-matrix learning from `2.0647 ± 0.0005` ($\gamma_K = 0.00$) to **`2.0638 / 2.0635 / 2.0624` $\to$ `2.0632 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`288.73` PPL)** at $\gamma_K = 0.98$ and **`2.0640 / 2.0629 / 2.0625` $\to$ `2.0631 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`288.64` PPL)** at $\gamma_K = 0.85$, surpassing 5-matrix Active-$W_Q$ (`2.0641 ± 0.0006`) by **`-0.0010` BPB** at $M=1$.
   - **Amortized Multi-Probe ($M \in \{2, 4\}$) Local $(W_Q, W_K)$ Probing, Pre-Rotary Reward-Side Tangent Projection (`Proposition 5`), Closed-Form Wiener-MMSE / Hadamard-$4$ / Sparse Top-$K=32$ Key Gather (`Proposition 6`, `exp59`, `exp60`, `exp63`), Groupwise/Headwise Polar Granularity $g \in \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$, Tier-1 $W_V$ Grouping (`Fixed-QK+V`), & Exact FP32 SVD Deconfounding (`Proposition 7`, Zhang et al. [12], `exp64`, `exp66`, `exp67`), & Exploratory Active `ve_gate` (`2.0631 -> 2.0552 ± 0.0007 -> 2.0519 ± 0.0009 -> 2.0504 ± 0.0007 [g=3] -> 2.0486 ± 0.0002 [g=2] -> 2.0458 ± 0.0003 [g_QK=1] -> 2.0455 ± 0.0001 [g_QK=1 Exact SVD] -> 2.0443 ± 0.0002 [g_QKV=1]` DEV BPB, `exp47` + `exp49` + `exp59` + `exp60` + `exp63` + `exp64` + `exp66` + `exp67`):** Because the clean pre-synaptic input $x_{\text{attn\_in}}(t-2)$ and outgoing receiver columns $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)}(t)$ are shared across local attention probes on the same clean forward trunk, averaging $M$ independent Rademacher query/key probes inside the post-synaptic modulation vector $\bar{u}^{(1)} = \frac{1}{M}\sum_{r=1}^M R^{(1, r)} (\xi^{(1, r)}/\sigma)$ *before* the single **Stage-1** outer-product `bmm` (`-(bar_u.t() @ x_attn_in) / BT`) and single `Pre-Polar Muon` orthogonalization step reduces conditional probe sampling variance by $1/M$ (given shared activations, readers, and downstream credit) with **zero additional Stage-1 outer-product matmuls or Muon steps**. Across Seeds `46, 47, 48`, $M=2$ decoupled 6-matrix Delayed WM reaches **`2.0589 / 2.0596 / 2.0604` $\to$ `2.0596 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`2.0228 ± 0.0006` VAL-B BPB, `285.87` PPL)** (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_lrk012_triton`), and **`2.0602 / 2.0595 / 2.0589` $\to$ `2.0595 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`2.0227 ± 0.0008` VAL-B BPB, `285.81` PPL)** (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_rng_matched_lrk012_triton`, which draws probes $r \ge 2$ from isolated CUDA RNG generators so the main model/data RNG stream is bit-for-bit identical to $M=1$). Note that in the code, Rademacher signs $\xi_q, \xi_k \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ are drawn in the **pre-rotary coordinate frame** (`q_pert = q + sigma * rms_q * xi_q`, followed by `q_r_p = norm(apply_rotary_emb(q_pert, cos, sin))`) and projected onto the pre-rotary tangent space $P_{q_n}, P_{k_n}$ at consumption time; because orthogonal RoPE $R_\theta$ commutes with `RMSNorm` and $P_u$ and preserves inner products and covariance trace, Proposition 5's fourth-moment identity applies in that pre-rotary coordinate frame. Removing reward-side tangent projection $P_{q_n}, P_{k_n}$ (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_no_reward_proj_triton`, `exp49`) scores **`2.0603 / 2.0598 / 2.0593` $\to$ `2.0598 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`2.0229 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `286.03` PPL)** in `10.72s`. Unfreezing the 3 value-embedding gate matrices `ve_gate` (`576` parameters of shape $6 \times 32$ across zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5`, `wm_d6_6mat_plus_ve_gate_m2_rng_matched_triton`, `exp49`, so `26,346,048 / 26,346,060 = 99.99995%` of total parameters and `100%` of matrix weights are active, leaving only `12` residual scalars frozen; gate perturbation is credited to Dust [9], Section 2.3) yields **`2.0590 / 2.0593 / 2.0589` $\to$ `2.0591 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`2.0221 ± 0.0003` VAL-B, `285.43` PPL)** in `11.12s`; because its paired difference relative to the isolated-RNG $M=2$ reference is $-0.00047 \pm 0.00064$ BPB (seedwise differences $-0.0012, -0.0002, 0.0000$), we treat the `ve_gate` improvement as an exploratory completeness check rather than a robust general gain. Scaling amortized local probing to $M=4$ (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m4_rng_matched_lrk012_triton`, `exp49`) reaches **`2.0545 / 2.0553 / 2.0558` $\to$ `2.0552 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`2.0183 ± 0.0005` VAL-B BPB, `282.43` PPL)** in `13.94s` (paired improvement $-0.00433 \pm 0.00131$ BPB over $M=2$)—improving upon 4-matrix Delayed WM (`2.0682 ± 0.0006`) by **`-0.0130` BPB (`-10.23` PPL)**, reaching numerical proximity (**`+0.0020` BPB**, rather than statistical equivalence) to the 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation reference (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`), and capturing **`60.2%`** (`0.0130 / 0.0216`, vs. **`40.3%`** = `0.0087 / 0.0216` at $M=2$) of the full 360-step 4-to-6-matrix exact-BP gain (`2.0532 -> 2.0316` = `-0.0216` BPB). Raising $\eta_K: 0.012 \to 0.024$ under fixed $\gamma_K = 0.85$ (`wm_d6_6mat_g085_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_360steps_triton`, `exp63` Arm 1) reaches **`2.0537 / 2.0527 / 2.0530` $\to$ `2.0531 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`2.0161 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `280.83` PPL, `11.80s`, `-0.0021` BPB over $\eta_K=0.012$)**. Replacing fixed exponential decay $\gamma_K = 0.85$ at matched $\eta_K=0.024, M=4$ (`exact_exp49_opt=True`) with causal attention-weighted `Rake` ($W_{\tau, s}=A_{\tau, s}$), the closed-form **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter** ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(4) = \frac{4 A_{\tau, s}^2}{3 A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2}$, `Proposition 6`, `exp59`), **Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ Temporal Probe Multiplexing** (`c(s, i) = (i + (i // 32) + s) mod 4`, eliminating `48/63 = 76.2%` of intra-head cross-coordinate interference pairs including all 32 RoPE rotary pairs, `exp60`), **Sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ `Wiener-MMSE` Key Gather** (`exp60`), and **Groupwise/Headwise Stiefel `PolarExpress` / Exact FP32 SVD Polar Factor** (`Proposition 7`, Zhang et al.
